APM PMS Q&A SOP: the session run on Monday 2-Nov, on the 30-Oct close

Readings as at Fri 30-Oct-2026, a Friday and the October month-end, so all five whole-holding sell tests were read on this close; the run started at 13:50 UK on Mon 2-Nov, before Monday's close. It applies your Position Entry and Exit Rules as changed on 2-Nov-2026: the 10-day stop-loss rule for Early Innings Probing Bets (each tranche's MA and its stop-loss, the MA less 6%), no MA SLs on Middle Innings Core Investments, and Fevertree an Early Innings Probing Bet. The next reading is Fri 6-Nov (Industry RS below 30; the 200D MA of relative price flat or falling over the month).

The three things that matter most this week

1. The rules sell two holdings at the Tue 3-Nov session: Arteche (4.28% of capital) and Alfen (4.6%).

  • Arteche, the whole holding: its relative price closed the 30-Oct month-end 34.7% below its 52W high, against the 25% line, and Arteche is not under your one-off in/out link. Against the rule this time: all three tranches were bought with relative price already at or past the line, and of the rule's three past relative-price sales on Arteche only one would have helped against the 30-Oct close (+5.6%, (4.9%) and (8.5%)).
  • Alfen, all three tranches: exit confirmed on 28-Sep under the 10-day stop-loss rule (three consecutive closes at or below the 150D MA in the bottom half of the day's range), not sold. The 150D MA is €12.76, the stop-loss €12.00, the 30-Oct close €10.24, 19.8% below the MA. Your 2-Nov ruling cleared the earlier confirmations of four other Probing Bets (Hilton Food, D'Ieteren, Fevertree and IMCD) whose price had closed back above the MA; Alfen's confirmation of 28-Sep is not among them and stands. Why it matters now: Alfen's third-quarter update comes on Wed 4-Nov, the day after the sale; FY2026 EBITDA consensus implies about €5.8m in the second half, only €1.8m above the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's €4m invalidation line, and Jefferies is already below it.

2. Thursday 5-Nov can move two holdings at once: Helios Towers (5.17%) and IMCD (2.0%) both report.

  • Helios Towers: its update can fire a fundamental exit. The memo's Invalidation test is organic tenancy additions below 600 a quarter; Morgan Stanley's preview implies about 526, Deutsche Bank's about 800, and the company's guidance 495 to 745. On the Sell Criteria it missed a sell signal by one Stock RS point at 30-Oct (Stock RS 70, Sector RS 41), and a further 4.7% fall in its relative price would reach the line of 25% below its 52W high, which is next read at the 30-Nov month-end.
  • IMCD: tranche 1 is clear under the 10-day stop-loss rule, 1.3% above its rising 20D MA (€100.92) and 7.8% above the stop-loss (€94.86). At a flat SP IMCD's 20D MA would stand only about 0.4% below the close on results day, Thu 5-Nov, so a close at or below the MA that day, followed by a second close at or below it with the days-after volume average at 0.75 times the 50-day average, would confirm the exit and sell at Tue 10-Nov.

3. Five old holdings now carry sell signals and wait for a replacement under your one-off in/out link (16.67% of capital), and the breadth test adds signals to three of them. The data, rebuilt at 14:31 UK on 2-Nov, now compute "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50" for the 30-Oct month-end: it gives a sell signal on Xvivo, Theon and Avanza. Xvivo carries two sell signals, Theon four, Avanza two, Bally's Intralot three and CVS Group one. Why it matters: one purchase releases one holding, so the proposed order of sale in Group 3 (Portfolio) decides which goes first; Xvivo's three tranches, triggered by the 30% fall on 2-Oct, also sell on their own at the Tuesday after their release (a close of SEK 232.30, or the 63rd trading day, 5-Jan-2027).

How this note is built

Group 1 (Market) sets the week, the month and your 28-Oct question on de-grossing or rotation. Group 2 (Individual Positions) takes the 16 holdings in the order they most need your thinking. Group 3 (Portfolio) gives the themes across holdings, each holding's add stage, the add and exit candidates and the proposed order of sale under the link; then come what I will do unless you say otherwise, and the data faults.

About this run

  • Your three AlphaSense daily screens could not be read (details in the data faults); the Developments rest on the research filed, the earnings reviews and the data files.
  • The dashboard data were rebuilt under your 2-Nov rules at 14:31 UK while this note was being written; readings are unchanged at 30-Oct, and every section was checked against the rebuilt data.
  • Your Notion position notes were read (the latest, "[PM] All live stocks @ 28-31 Oct-26", edited 31-Oct), and each section quotes you before answering.

Group 1 (Market)

The Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.7% in the week to Friday 30-Oct-2026 and 3.2% over the month, but the leadership was defensive: Healthcare and Consumer staples led October, while Industrials and capital goods and Technology fell. Over three months the Stoxx Europe 600 is down 1.5%, and over twelve months it is up 12.5%. On Friday 30-Oct, the month-end reading day, the Stoxx Europe 600 fell 0.4%. The month's basis: the one-month figures in this group are for the 21 sessions from the 1-Oct close to 30-Oct, the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's one-month basis. Over calendar October, from the 30-Sep close, the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.9%, because its 1.3% fall on 1-Oct is outside the 21 sessions. On the calendar basis Healthcare rose 6.3%, Consumer staples 6.1% and Financials fell 2.0%; the holdings' sections, like this group, use the 21-session basis.

  • October's leaders (the 21 sessions to 30-Oct): Healthcare +8.3%, Consumer staples +8.0%, Professional, business and consumer services +6.4%, Defence +4.6% and Infrastructure +4.5%.
  • October's laggards: Industrials and capital goods (1.3)%, Technology (0.5)%, Materials (0.4)% and Financials +0.1%.
  • Over three months the split is wider: Transportation is up 8.2% and Healthcare 7.9%, and Energy, commodities and metals mining is up 6.7%; Real assets are down 9.5%, Defence is down 6.5% and Media is down 6.4%.

In the last week the defensive rotation paused in Consumer staples but not in Healthcare. Consumer staples fell 0.1% in the week to 30-Oct while Healthcare rose 2.2%. Industrials and capital goods rebounded 2.0% and Defence 4.7% in the week, but each of them fell 1.3% on Friday 30-Oct, and Technology fell 2.1% that day. What would settle it: a second week in which Industrials and capital goods and Technology hold their rebound would read as a retest inside an uptrend; another week like Friday 30-Oct's would read as the October pattern resuming.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "But is it de-grossing or the start of a proper rotation?" The October data fit both readings, and two observations point to more than de-grossing.

  • For a lasting rotation: the leadership has held for a month in Healthcare and Consumer staples, and Healthcare's lead widened in the last week; the Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment of 30-Oct moved Defence from Neutral to Underweight on a third weak relative strength reading.
  • For de-grossing: the index itself rose 3.2% over the 21 sessions to 30-Oct (1.9% over calendar October), and 58% of stocks stand above their absolute 200D MA, up from 54% a week earlier; a de-grossing inside a rising market tends to reverse once crowded positions are cut.
  • The reading that would decide it: whether Healthcare and Consumer staples keep outperforming in a week when the index falls.

The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment of 30-Oct-2026 keeps the ideal portfolio defensive. Its tilts are: Overweight Consumer staples and Energy, commodities and metals mining; Double Underweight Consumer discretionary and Real assets; Underweight Media and, from this week, Defence; Neutral elsewhere, Industrials and capital goods included. The same assessment holds Momentum at Underweight and Fragility at Double Underweight. The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment's reason is a risk-off regime beneath a steady index, "led now by credit and the cost of debt", with the US ten-year yield above 5% after a hawkish Federal Reserve hold. That matches your own judgement of 28-Oct: "the risk of an equity market sell-off is clearly increasing as bond yields and rates rise".

The 16 holdings come in the order they most need your thinking. Last Fevertree and Sdiptech.

Arteche

Middle Innings Core Investment · 4.28% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 2.00%, bought 26-Jun-2026; tranche 2, 1.28%, bought 19-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 1.00%, bought 29-Oct-2026) · readings as at Fri 30-Oct-2026

1. SP and MAs

Arteche's SP gave back its September advance and then failed to hold a bounce: it fell 20.2% from €37.7 (30-Sep) to €30.1 (23-Oct), rose 5.6% to €31.8 (29-Oct) and closed at €30.6 on 30-Oct, at the day's low and 0.8% below its rising absolute 200D MA (€30.85). Arteche is up 65.3% over 12 months, up 5.2% over 3 months and down 13.8% over one month, and 30.5% below its €44.0 52W closing high (8-May). Over the 20 sessions to 30-Oct Arteche fell 15.0% while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 2.4%: after allowing for the market through Arteche's beta, (17.1) points of the fall were Arteche's own (data file).

The moves since 30-Sep, each set against the industry (Industrials and capital goods) and peers:

  • 1-Oct, (5.8%), Arteche's own day: the industry fell 1.3% and the cohort of Arteche's peers 0.4%; Arteche traded 1.94x its 50D average volume, the heaviest day of the decline.
  • 6 to 21-Oct, (11.8%), and 22 to 23-Oct, (6.5%), the sector's falls: ABB, Prysmian, Legrand and Siemens Energy fell 9% to 17% over the first; on 22-Oct the industry fell 2.2% and Siemens Energy 8.6%. Arteche's estimates did not move (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).
  • 26 to 29-Oct, +5.6%, the group's bounce, which Arteche joined only lightly (item 9 (Peers)).
  • 30-Oct, (3.8%), the sector's day, not Arteche's own: the key-days table shows the cohort flat only because Landis+Gyr rose 10.1%; Siemens Energy fell 6.9%, Nexans 7.6%, Schneider Electric 4.1%, Prysmian 3.9% and ABB 3.3% (chart files).

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Nov) adds two dated items:

  • 28-Oct: GE Vernova agreed to buy Trench, the instrument-transformer leader, for about $9bn, 18x 2026 EBITDA and 13x 2028. Hypothesis: Arteche's 3.2% gap-up open on 29-Oct, which no peer matched, was the market reading the Trench price across to Arteche, a top-two maker in the same niche; sellers faded it on 0.57x volume. No source read ties the open to the deal.
  • Spain holds a snap general election on 29-Nov-2026, which the memo expects to push Redeia's network plan into late 2027; Arteche's Strategic Plan 2030 ("October or November 2026") is not dated and may slip past it.

Hypothesis 1 (the better supported): October was a sector and factor de-rating on unchanged estimates. For it: three of the four down moves since 30-Sep were sector days, and Arteche's (13.8%) month sits inside the large names' range (Schneider Electric (18.8%), ABB (11.2%)). Against it: 1-Oct was Arteche's own heavy-volume fall.

Hypothesis 2: the market has started to de-rate Arteche's own premium, the "Pfisterer template" the memo warns of. For it: from 23 to 30-Oct Arteche rose 1.7% against 2.6% to 11.4% for the large names, and the memo puts Arteche's multiple at the 91st percentile of its history. Against it: no estimate cut, no downgrade, and lighter selling than in the June to July decline (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)). Test: the next sector up day: if Arteche again rises less than half as much as Legrand, Siemens Energy and ABB, Hypothesis 2 gains weight.

The MAs: Arteche closed below its 20D MA on 8-Oct, its 100D and 150D on 19-Oct, its 50D and absolute 200D on 22-Oct, back above the absolute 200D on 27-Oct and below it again on 30-Oct; the 20D and 100D MAs are falling, the others rising. Precedent: the rising absolute 200D MA was Arteche's floor in 2026: it closed 1.9% above it at the 29-Jul low and 1.9% below it at the 1-Sep low, back above the next day, before the 34.6% September rise.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "APM to confirm the successful retest of the rising 200D MA". Not confirmed on the 30-Oct close. Arteche held the line on closes from 27 to 29-Oct (1.8% to 3.3% above it) on 0.53x to 0.75x its 50D average volume, then closed 0.8% below it at the day's low. It has closed below on 4 of the last 7 sessions, never by more than 1.7%: by your own principle ("if the price holds < 5% below an MA, then it has not triggered an exit; it is holding/testing", your note on Carlsberg), Arteche is still testing the line, not through it.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Arteche against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes (+3.6% over 20 sessions, +12.1% over 63).
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes (€33.03 against €30.85).
  • The 50D MA above the 150D MA and the absolute 200D MA: partly (€32.32: above the absolute 200D MA, below the 150D MA since 1-Sep).
  • The SP above each of the 50D, 150D and absolute 200D MAs: fails (5.3% below the 50D, 7.4% below the 150D, 0.8% below the absolute 200D).
  • Near its 52W high: fails (30.5% below).
  • Away from its 52W low: passes (62.7% above €18.81).
  • Stage 3 red flags, from the 29-Jul low: the largest one-day fall (1-Oct, (5.8%), the session after the high, cause not in the sources read): present; the largest one-week fall ((8.2%) to 23-Oct): present; wider swings (average daily range 3.1% in August, 3.7% in the last 10 sessions): partly; lower lows (€34.3 on 8-Oct to €30.1 on 23-Oct, still above the 1-Sep €28.0): present in October; more down days (11 against 6 in the decline): present; a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume (1-Oct at 1.94x): partly; tracking the MA instead of bouncing (seven sessions within 3.3% of it): present for now.

Verdict: neither a healthy retest nor a broken long-term trend, but a pullback to a rising absolute 200D MA inside a medium-term trend already damaged, with all seven Stage 3 flags present or partly present. What would settle it: a close above the 50D MA (€32.32) on 1.5x volume makes it a healthy retest; a close more than 5% below the absolute 200D MA (about €29.3) on above-average volume makes it a broken trend.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

A correction first: two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug and the 18-Sep index review, 13.69x) lift Arteche's 50D average on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard to 30,867 shares, against 23,784 without them. Ratios below leave them out, with the dashboard's figure in brackets. The 31-Aug day leaves the average about 6-Nov and the 18-Sep day about 27-Nov, when the dashboard's ratios will read about 30% higher with no change in trading.

Arteche's October decline was sold lightly and faded on falling volume; the bounce has found no buyer of size either.

  • Heavy-volume days (1.5x or more) in 25 sessions: two, both at the top. 29-Sep, +5.0% on 47,667 shares (€1.79m, 1.75x), an accumulation day; 1-Oct, (5.8%) on 53,152 shares (€1.89m, 1.94x), closing at 0.31 of its range, the only distribution day in the 25 sessions. No session since has traded above 1.21x.
  • Selling dried up through the decline: down days averaged 1.06x the 50D average from 30-Sep to 14-Oct and 0.73x from 14 to 23-Oct, though the second half fell further (11.2% against 10.1%).
  • Buying was absent in the bounce: the up days from 23 to 29-Oct averaged 0.56x; the 30-Oct fall came on 0.60x (0.46x). The 5D average is 0.57x the 50D average, the 10D 0.67x, the 20D 0.78x.
  • Up-day against down-day volume: the dashboard's ratio is 1.17 over 5 sessions and 0.93 over 20.

October's was the lightest of Arteche's three declines of about 20% this year, with down days averaging 0.91x (1 of 11 heavy), against 1.06x (2 of 14) from 8-May to 10-Jun and 1.09x (3 of 15) from 22-Jun to 29-Jul without the 25-Jun placement. The precedent that matters: the May to June decline also ended quietly, and its light-volume rebound failed into the larger June to July fall; that fall ended with a 6.1% up day on 2.42x (2.14x) on 30-Jul, the day after the low. Arteche's October low has had no such day.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Volume on the down days was much less than for Volex - which is a relative positive". Confirmed: in the 25 sessions to 30-Oct Arteche had 1 distribution day against Volex's 4, and its heaviest October down day was 1.94x against Volex's 3.80x (19-Oct). The qualification: light selling with light buying says holders are not leaving and new buyers are not arriving; it does not yet say which side wins.

Every MA event in October came on below-average volume: the 22-Oct break of the 50D and absolute 200D MAs on 0.79x, the 27-Oct recovery on 0.75x, the 30-Oct loss on 0.60x. On 30-Oct Arteche's large peers traded 1.4x to 1.8x their 50D averages: the sector's sellers were active, Arteche's holders largely were not.

Arteche's relative price is 34.7% below its 52W high (12-May) and 5.3% below its relative 200D MA, which it crossed below on 22-Oct. Relative price confirmed the SP's October low (both lows fell on 23-Oct; relative price was only 0.2% above its low on 30-Oct). It was 18.8% below its 52W high at the 30-Sep month-end and has been past the 25% line since 13-Oct; with the market up 3.2% in the month, Arteche looks weaker on the relative chart than on the price chart.

Confirms a low: an up day on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 35,700 shares, €1.09m), closing in the top third of its range and above the 50D MA (€32.32). Confirms the decline continues: a close below €30.1 on more than 23,800 shares.

3. RS

Arteche's RS fall began in its sector, spread to its industry and has now reached Arteche itself: Stock RS fell 8 points on 30-Oct alone, to 76, the reading now closest to a second sell signal.

  • Sector RS (Electrical equipment, grid and automation): 75 (2-Oct), 26 (16-Oct), 13 (23-Oct, its 12-month low), 39 (29-Oct), 25 at the 30-Oct month-end (26 in the earlier build of the data file); below 50 since 16-Oct, from 86 at 31-Jul.
  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 79 (9-Oct), 46 (23-Oct), 53 (29-Oct), 46 (30-Oct): 16 points above 30; 12-month low 40 (March 2026).
  • Stock RS: 94 (2-Oct), 83 (23-Oct), 84 (29-Oct), 76 (30-Oct): 6 points above 70 and 26 above 50; 95 at both the 31-Jul and 30-Sep month-ends; 12-month low 73 (11-Sep).

The order of the turn is a rotation out of the sector carrying Arteche down last: the sector broke between the 9 and 16-Oct Fridays, the industry between 16 and 23-Oct, Arteche's Stock RS in the last week; on 29-Oct the sector rebounded 26 points while Arteche's Stock RS rose 1.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Industry RS is still fine. Same for the 200D MA slope and Stock RS". Two of the three still hold on the 30-Oct readings; Stock RS has weakened: Industry RS 46 and the relative 200D MA rising 3.03% over the month still hold, but Stock RS fell from 84 to 76, and with Sector RS at 25 a Stock RS below 70 at the 30-Nov month-end gives a sell signal (item 11 (the stock against each Sell Criterion)).

Precedent: the last time Arteche's Stock RS stood this close to 70 (73 on 11-Sep), it was back at 96 within two weeks, after Spain announced over €17bn of grid investment to 2030 (La Moncloa, 16-Sep). The mechanical risk: as November 2025's closes (€19.6 to €23.2) become the 12-month base, Arteche's 12-month gain at a flat €30.6 narrows from 65% to about 32% by early December, which plausibly weighs on Stock RS with no further fall. Against peers: Arteche's 76 is still the second-highest Stock RS in its sector, after Cenergy (79); Schneider Electric and Siemens Energy are at 18. Test: the Fri 6-Nov Stock RS reading.

4. Consensus by named FY

Arteche's estimates have not moved since 30-Sep, through the whole 20.2% fall: the SP led down on its own, which fits a de-rating, not an earnings scare. FY2026 EPS (€1.077), FY2027 EPS (€1.333), FY2027 revenue (€684m) and FY2027 EBITDA (€126.2m) are unchanged over one month, three analysts each. Over 12 months the upgrades were margin-led: FY2027 EPS +34.2%, EBITDA +26.8%, revenue +7.4%. FY2026 consensus EBITDA (€103.7m) is 3.3% above the top of the guidance Arteche raised on 28-Jul (€92.1m to €100.4m).

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "ISSUE - Why are FY 2028 EPS estimates falling? MAJOR ISSUE. Ditto EBITDA. It's a 2028-specific issue (2027 is clean)". FY2028 is not falling now: it fell from March to August and has recovered most of it. The real point is that analysts have moved the earnings step into FY2027 and flattened FY2028.

  • The path: FY2028 EPS €1.58 (31-Mar), €1.54 (30-Jun), €1.50 (31-Aug), €1.56 (30-Sep and 30-Oct): (5.1%), then +4.0%. FY2028 EBITDA €149.9m (31-Mar), €134.0m (30-Jun), €148.4m (30-Sep): (10.6%), then +10.8%. FY2028 revenue dipped 0.7% and is now €786.2m, a new high.
  • Part of the dip is who was counted: in June and July only two analysts contributed to Arteche's FY2027 estimates, against three before and since, and the memo (1-Nov) infers the unnamed third contributor sits near €1.28 for FY2028, well below Santander and JB Capital. Against it: FY2028 EPS was lowest at the August month-end, with three analysts counted, so composition explains part of the dip, not all; the sources read do not name the analyst who cut.
  • The trajectory has flattened: from the March to the October month-end FY2027 EPS rose 17.6% while FY2028 fell 1.3%; implied growth from FY2027 to FY2028 fell from 39% to 17% for EPS, from 35% to 18% for EBITDA and from 19% to 15% for revenue. The implied FY2027 EBITDA margin rose from 17.1% to 18.4% and FY2028's fell from 19.3% to 18.9%: the step between them shrank from 2.3 points to 0.4.

Why it matters: this plateau is the memo's central disagreement with the market: it holds the EBITDA margin at 18.3% to 18.6% from FY2027 (18.0% in FY2026), puts FY2027 EPS at €1.22 after minorities against the €1.333 mean, and expects the first FY2027 guidance (24-Mar-2027) to trigger cuts. Test: whether FY2028 moves up with FY2027 after the Strategic Plan 2030, or stays flat beside it.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

No analyst has cut anything on Arteche through the October fall: 3 Buys of 3, and EPS, EBITDA, revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth all +33% (one of three raising, none cutting) at the 28 and 30-Oct readings. The +33% reflects a raise inside the counting window, not a new one: consensus has not changed since 30-Sep. Ratings have been all Buys since the 31-Aug month-end, with no Sell in 24 months.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "SS EPS breadth turned negative in Jun-Aug 26. Unclear since then. Data issue". From the data file's monthly counts for FY2027: EPS Net Upgrade Breadth was (50%) at the June and July month-ends (one of two analysts cutting), +33% at August, +67% at September and +33% at October. It turned positive at the August month-end and has stayed positive. The "unclear" reading is a gap between series: the monthly counts run to 31-Aug and the daily readings from there to 30-Oct. With two contributors, one analyst made the whole (50%); the sources read do not name the house.

Arteche's average PT (€39.11) is 27.8% above the SP (€30.6), wider than at any month-end since August 2024.

  • The PT has followed the SP: it rose 58% in six months, in steps after SP rises (€24.8 to €34.2 over the May and June 2026 month-ends, after the May spike), and has not moved since 29-Sep. The memo puts the two named domestic brokers' PTs at €37 to €42; your 1-Oct note records Santander's raise to €42 from €32.
  • Hypothesis: the gap is wide because analysts have not reacted to a sector de-rating, not because they see more value; if the PTs follow the SP, they will not cushion a further fall.
  • A data fault, still open: the daily average PT read €47.0 on 15, 16 and 17-Oct and €39.11 on every other day; no broker note explains it.

Where the sell-side could turn: the memo expects "a consensus cut with no downgrade" as coverage widens and at the March 2027 guidance. Test: the first change to Arteche's FY2027 consensus after the Strategic Plan 2030; a cut would end the divergence between a 20% SP fall and +33% breadth.

9. Peers

Arteche fell with the large grid and electrification names in October, and has lagged them for a week: from 23 to 30-Oct Arteche rose 1.7%, against 11.4% for Prysmian, 6.9% for Siemens Energy, 5.6% for Legrand and 4.3% for ABB. Arteche's daily moves correlate most with Legrand (0.48 over 63 sessions, up from 0.36 over 126), Siemens Energy, Siemens and ABB, all outside its cohort: the large names set Arteche's direction. From 30-Sep to 23-Oct Arteche fell 20.2%, in line with Schneider Electric (20.8%), Prysmian (20.1%) and Alfen (18.3%); Pfisterer (9.8%) and R&S Group Holding (6.3%) fell less because they had fallen earlier.

Your four peer checks:

  • Sell-side estimates: rising at the large names, falling at the small cohort peers. Over 3 months FY2027 EPS rose 4% to 7% at Schneider Electric, ABB, Prysmian and NKT, with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of 52% to 64% at ABB, Prysmian and Schneider Electric; it fell 7% at Pfisterer and 5% at R&S Group Holding, both with negative breadth. Oddo BHF cut its Pfisterer PT to €81 from €91 on 23-Oct; Bernstein started at €102 (boerse-global.de, 24-Oct).
  • SP technicals: almost the whole sector is below its absolute 200D MA. Of 14 sector and cohort names checked, only Landis+Gyr, Siemens and Cenergy close above it; Arteche (0.8% below) is nearer to it than any other name below it (Siemens Energy 17.7%, Pfisterer 25.7% below).
  • Newsflow: the leaders' results are strong. Schneider Electric's record third-quarter revenue and raised guidance (16-Oct); ABB's third-quarter orders up 40% to $12.06bn (20-Oct); Prysmian's beat and raised guidance (Jefferies, 29-Oct; +6.6% that day).
  • Company guidance: raised at the leaders, confirmed at Pfisterer, cut at R&S Group Holding ("temporary moderation" in parts of the distribution transformer market, 5-Aug); R&S Group Holding's Capital Markets Day is on 4-Nov.

Who turned first: R&S Group Holding and Pfisterer broke down months before Arteche (Pfisterer's Stock RS 31 three months ago, 5 now); Alfen (51 to 16) and the large names (Schneider Electric 69 to 18, ABB 72 to 34) in October; Arteche last (95 to 76). The exception is Landis+Gyr, the one cohort peer rising: +10.1% on 30-Oct on 3.58x its 50D volume, Stock RS 74 from 5 three months ago, for a cause not in the sources read.

What the peers imply for Arteche:

1. SP and MAs

Alfen's SP has round-tripped its spring advance: €8.26 (20-Mar) to €19.67 (26-May) to €9.95 (26-Oct), and closed the week at €10.24. Over 12 months Alfen is up 6.6% (from €9.61 on 4-Nov-2025); over 3 months it is down 19.7% (from €12.76 on 4-Aug); over the month to 30-Oct it is down 15.7% (from €12.14 on 1-Oct).

  • The decline from the 17-Aug high (€14.25) to the 26-Oct low (€9.95) is (30.2%) over 50 sessions, the third decline of 25% or more in nine months, after (27.8%) to 20-Mar and (37.4%) to 29-Jul.
  • The latest legs: 22-Sep to 26-Oct, €12.67 to €9.95, (21.5%) in 24 sessions with 18 down days; then 26 to 30-Oct, €9.95 to €10.24, +2.9%. The 26-Oct close was Alfen's lowest since 15-Apr.

About half of Alfen's 22-Sep to 26-Oct leg was its sector's, and half was its own. Over that leg Alfen fell 21.5%, against an average fall of 11.9% for nine cohort and sector peers with chart files (Prysmian (20.4%), Schneider Electric (20.1%), Pfisterer (18.2%), ABB (16.1%), Siemens Energy (15.6%), Arteche (13.6%), Cembre (10.1%), R&S Group Holding (0.1%), Landis+Gyr +6.7%).

Alfen's beta of 1.45 turns that 2.4% rise into an expected rise of 3.5% for Alfen, so the stock-specific part is the 14.2% fall less the 3.5% rise, (17.7%), which is 1.1 times Alfen's usual 20-session move.

In the week to 30-Oct Alfen decoupled from its sector in both directions (item 9 (Peers)): it lagged the sector's rebound and fell least on the 30-Oct selling day.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file, plus the AlphaSense operating-leverage report filed on 31-Oct (Files/ALFEN-NL/47-kq-research).

  • Alfen, half-year results, 18-Aug (earnings review), mixed: adjusted EBITDA €16.4m in line; gross margin 26.1% from 30.0%; second-quarter storage intake €11m against ING's €33m; guidance unchanged (revenue €435m to €475m, adjusted EBITDA margin 4% to 7%).

Healthy retest or broken trend? Alfen against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: fails. It is flat (+0.1% over 20 sessions) and edging down since 16-Oct.

The largest one-day fall since the March advance began came the day after the high (27-May, (10.2%)) and the largest five-session fall ((17.1%) to 24-Aug) after mixed results; lower lows (€12.31, €11.10, €9.95); 35 down days against 18 up since 17-Aug. 2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

On the figures: volume against the 50D average on the volume script's basis; no mechanical-volume day falls in the last 25 sessions, but two (31-Aug and 18-Sep) sit inside the 50D average and lift it.

The PMS Per-stock Dashboard counts three distribution days, up from one on 23-Oct; from the chart file I cannot reproduce the dashboard's extra two on your definition.

On that basis Alfen's only distribution day was a sector day: on 15-Oct Prysmian traded 2.8x, Siemens Energy 2.6x and Pfisterer 2.1x, each down 2.2% to 5.2%. Up-day volume against down-day volume (the dashboard's basis: average volume on up days divided by average volume on down days): 0.86 over 20 sessions and 1.06 over 5. Over the whole decline from 17-Aug the volume script reads 0.88, with five heavy down days and one heavy up day, against 0.96 for the 26-May to 29-Jul decline and 0.69 for the February to March decline.

Alfen's first close below its 150D MA in this decline, on 24-Aug, came on 2.72x.

Precedent: the March low ended on a heavy up day; the July low and this one did not. After the 20-Mar low (€8.26) Alfen rose 8.6% on 2.2x on 23-Mar, closing at its high, and never closed below that day's low again. After the quiet 29-Jul low (€12.31) the heavy up days came three weeks later (14, 17 and 20-Aug, up to 3.3x) and each failed within three sessions. After the 26-Oct low there is no heavy up day yet.

3. RS

Alfen's Stock RS is 16 at the 30-Oct month-end, far below both sell-test lines (70 and 50), and it has stopped falling but not turned. It was 51 at the 30-Sep month-end, 55 at 31-Jul and 87 on 10-Jul; it has been below 70 at every reading since 19-Aug and below 50 since 9-Oct. Over the last three Fridays it read 17, 16 and 16.

Alfen's Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods) is 46, 16 points above the line of 30 that its sell test uses: 79 on 2 and 9-Oct, 66 on 16-Oct, 46 on 23-Oct, 53 on 29-Oct, 46 on 30-Oct. It has fallen 33 points in three weeks.

The order of the turn: Alfen first and alone, then its sector, then its industry. Alfen's Stock RS fell from 87 (10-Jul) to 30 (31-Aug) while its Sector RS held between 73 and 92. The sector followed from 2-Oct (75 to 13 by 23-Oct), the industry from 9-Oct (79 to 46). Alfen broke down on its own results in August and was then caught in the rotation out of the whole group.

Alfen's RS is not leading its peers out. In the week to 30-Oct Stock RS rose from 16 to 18 and back to 16, while Sector RS rose from 13 (23-Oct) to 39 (29-Oct) and fell back to 25 (30-Oct); Landis+Gyr's Stock RS rose from 45 to 74 over the month. 4. Consensus by named FY

Alfen's consensus has not changed for a month while its SP fell 15.7%: in October the SP fell alone, in August it fell with the estimates. From 30-Sep to 30-Oct FY2026, FY2027 and FY2028 revenue and EPS consensus did not change, and EBITDA moved by less than 2% in each year. From 17-Aug to 2-Sep the SP fell 22.1% as FY2027 EBITDA consensus was cut 6.5% in August (€30.73m at the 31-Jul month-end to €28.73m at 31-Aug).

The cuts of the last three months fall hardest on the nearest year: analysts have lowered the base, not the recovery.

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26): EBITDA €22.15m, (12.5%) over 3 months, (31.5%) over 12; the only October move was +1.9% (from €21.74m). EPS (€0.149), from €0.18 three months ago. Revenue €455.3m, +1.1% over 3 months.
  • FY2027: EBITDA €28.69m, (6.6%) over 3 months, (29.1%) over 12; revenue €492.5m, +0.7% over 3 months; EPS €0.35, +13.3% over 3 months on 7 analysts, almost all of it one step (+22% from the 31-Aug month-end, €0.29, to 29-Sep, after (7%) in August), so on a small, low base it carries little weight.
  • FY2028: EBITDA €34.37m, (4.7%) over 3 months; revenue €534.0m, +0.7%; EPS €0.50, +3.4%.

Implied margins and growth put the recovery in FY2027 and FY2028. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 4.9% (FY2026) to 5.8% (FY2027) and 6.4% (FY2028). Revenue grows 8.2% and then 8.4% a year; EBITDA grows 29.5% and then 19.8%. Revenue estimates edging up while EBITDA is cut means analysts expect more revenue at a lower margin: the storage mix and personnel costs behind the cuts described in items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth).

The second half of FY2026 is where consensus comes up against the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's invalidation line. First-half adjusted EBITDA was €16.4m. FY2026 consensus of €22.15m therefore implies about €5.8m in the second half; Jefferies' €18.8m (21-Aug) implies about €2.4m. The memo's invalidation test is second-half adjusted EBITDA below €4m. Caveat: consensus may mix reported and adjusted EBITDA (first-half reported EBITDA was €11.0m), so the implied figure is approximate. Inference: the market's estimate sits only about €1.8m above the memo's invalidation line, and one named broker is already below it. The company's guidance (revenue €435m to €475m, adjusted EBITDA margin 4% to 7%) spans second-half adjusted EBITDA from about €1m to about €17m, so its low end (about €1m) sits below the memo's €4m invalidation line, and the guidance does not rule out a breach.

The SP did not lead the estimates in October; it may be anticipating them. Hypothesis: the SP is pricing a cut at the 4-Nov third-quarter update (storage intake, the second-half cost overlap, charging). For it: Alfen's own share of the October fall (item 1 (SP and MAs)) and Deutsche Bank's 20-Oct charging data. Against it: no named broker change since ING's PT of 7-Sep is in the sources read, and consensus has not moved. Test: Alfen's daily EBITDA consensus in the week after 4-Nov.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Alfen's ratings follow its SP and its news, not its valuation. At the May-2025 month-end, after the SP fell from €15.40 to €11.41, Buys went from 4 to none and Sells from 1 to 4. Sells peaked at 7 of 10 at the October-2025 month-end. The only Buy of 2026 appeared at the May-2026 month-end, near the high, and was gone by the August month-end, after Jefferies' downgrade. Today: no Buys, 6 Holds and 4 Sells among 10 analysts, unchanged since 30-Sep.

Alfen's average PT (€10.88, unchanged since 29-Sep) is now 6.3% above its SP, but only because the SP fell to it. At the 31-Aug month-end the average PT (€10.79) stood 5.8% below the SP (€11.46); from April to August 2026 it stood below the SP at every month-end, by up to 38% in May. Named PTs in the sources read: Jefferies €14 (from €18, 21-Aug), Deutsche Bank €13 (19-Aug), ING €10 (7-Sep), the last now 2.3% below the SP.

  • Precedent, both ways: the average PT moved above the SP at the February-2026 month-end, three weeks before the 20-Mar low, and the SP then rose 138%; but it also stood above the SP at every month-end from July to October 2025 while the SP drifted down. The PT gap alone does not call the turn.

Alfen's Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on revenue, PT and EPS and negative on EBITDA, unchanged since the 30-Sep month-end:

  • revenue +33% (9 analysts: 3 raising, none cutting); PT +20% (10: 3 raising, 1 cutting); EPS +14% (7: 2 raising, 1 cutting);

Hypotheses for Xvivo's flat week, ranked by the weight of evidence. 1. A pause inside a downtrend, before the next leg down. For: Xvivo's SP is below every MA, its relative price set its lowest closes since March 2026 this week, and FY2027 consensus EPS is still falling (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). 4. Consensus by named FY

Xvivo's analysts cut FY2027 hardest after the third-quarter report, and cut profit about three times as fast as revenue. Over the month to 30-Oct, FY2027 consensus EPS fell 13.2% (SEK 6.04 to SEK 5.24), FY2027 EBITDA 10.9% (SEK 341m to SEK 304m) and FY2027 revenue 4.3% (SEK 1,255m to SEK 1,201m). FY2027 EPS has now fallen 22.6% from its 31-May peak of SEK 6.77, and 20.7% over six months.

  • The cuts came in steps (daily series): FY2027 EPS SEK 5.81 on 21-Oct, SEK 5.62 on 22-Oct, SEK 5.50 on 23-Oct and SEK 5.24 on 28-Oct.
  • FY2028 was cut less: FY2028 EPS fell 8.3% over the month to SEK 8.69, revenue 0.9% and EBITDA 4.5%, so analysts are pushing earnings further out rather than cutting the trajectory. Implied EPS growth from FY2027 to FY2028 widened to 65.6% from 56.9% at the 30-Sep month-end; from FY2026 to FY2027 it narrowed to 44.0% from 72.8%.
  • FY2026 EPS rose 4.2% over the month to SEK 3.64 on tax and financial income (DNB Carnegie raised its FY2026 EPS 10% to SEK 3.9 on 23-Oct, AlphaSense earnings summary of 30-Oct): the lift comes from tax and financial income, not from operations, so it does not recur.
  • The implied EBITDA margin: FY2027 25.3% (from 27.2% at the 30-Sep month-end), FY2028 30.6% (from 31.7%), against FY2026's 20.5%; Xvivo's 2027 targets (an EBITDA margin above 30%) now sit a full year beyond consensus.

Hypothesis: consensus is converging on the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's numbers faster than the memo's calendar. For: the memo (25-Oct) has FY2027 EPS of SEK 3.82 and expected a 25% to 35% consensus cut through four reports; the AlphaSense earnings summary of 30-Oct finds about a third of it landed in a week, and the 28-Oct cutters sit below DNB Carnegie's SEK 5.7. Against: the memo's amortisation and tax adjustments, worth 8% to 10% of FY2027 EPS, are not yet in any broker's numbers and are due at the 28-Jan-2027 report, so the convergence is incomplete. Test: FY2027 EPS at the 30-Nov month-end; a figure below SEK 5.0 says the reset is still running.

Xvivo's SP led its estimates down: relative price peaked on 21-Apr, six weeks before FY2027 EPS peaked, and the largest single consensus cut (28-Oct) came after the SP's 13.1% fall, on a day the SP rose.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Xvivo's Net Upgrade Breadth is as negative as it can be on profit: every analyst with an estimate has cut FY2027 EPS and EBITDA, and none has raised.

  • EPS (FY2027): (100%), 0 raising and 6 cutting at the 30-Oct month-end, from 0% (one up, one down) at the 30-Sep month-end; it was negative at every month-end from April to August 2026, between (17%) and (67%).
  • EBITDA (FY2027): (100%), 0 raising and 7 cutting, from (29%) at the 30-Sep month-end.
  • Revenue (FY2027): (86%), 6 of 7 cutting on 28-Oct, from 0% at the 30-Sep month-end and +83% at the May month-end.
  • PT: (57%), 4 of 7 cutting on 28-Oct, from (14%) at the 30-Sep month-end; Xvivo's Sell-side Momentum rating is F (28-Oct).

The brokers cutting, and their stated logic (AlphaSense earnings summary, 30-Oct; share-price drivers report, 31-Oct):

  • DNB Carnegie (23-Oct): Buy and SEK 330 kept; FY2027 EPS cut 4% to SEK 5.7 and FY2028 3% to SEK 8.6 "for lower projected abdominal sales"; it calls the Lung Bioengineering loss "overdone", offset by the expansion of organ procurement hubs and price initiatives.
  • RedEye (23-Oct): fair value cut from SEK 385 to SEK 360, moving to 90% discounted cash flow because of the Lung Bioengineering uncertainty, sizing the drag at 5% to 6% of 2027 group sales and lowering its 2027 thoracic sales; it keeps a SEK 748 bull case and a SEK 140 bear case.
  • The 28-Oct cutters: not named in the sources read; they took FY2027 EPS 4.7% lower in one day and PT breadth from (43%) to (57%).
  • The link: the one cut with a stated reason outside the customer is DNB Carnegie's on abdominal sales, the segment the earnings summary of 30-Oct says the case treats as predictable; the cuts are spreading beyond the customer.

Xvivo's ratings still say nothing: 6 Buys of 6 at every month-end since October 2025, through a 90% rise and a 34.6% fall; ratings here follow valuation, not momentum, and the 100% Buy share can only fall.

Xvivo's average PT (SEK 324.9 on 28-Oct) is 58.5% above the SP and falling into it: down 6.1% since 29-Sep and 9.8% since the May month-end, while the SP fell 25.9%. Precedent: with a falling PT about 69% above the SP (March 2025 month-end) Xvivo fell a further 43%; at about 72% (February 2026 month-end) its low came two sessions later. The gap alone has not marked Xvivo's lows; the test is whether PTs stop falling once the remaining brokers have updated.

9. Peers

Xvivo's cohort has rotated to the stocks whose estimates rise, and Xvivo is the only one of the five still falling (the peer table on the page). Over the month to 30-Oct Bonesupport rose 43.8%, Vitrolife 24.4%, Ambu 21.0% and Surgical Science 14.9%, against Xvivo's (8.3%); Xvivo is the only cohort member below its 50D and absolute 200D MAs.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Bonesupport, Vitrolife, etc., up lots in October... Why?" Two causes, in the sources read: their own third-quarter results, with Bonesupport up 24.3% on 7.1 times its average volume on its 22-Oct beat and Vitrolife up 5.0% and 5.9% on 22 and 23-Oct after its own; and a rotation into Healthcare, whose Industry RS rose from 46 on 16-Oct to 86 on 30-Oct (the sell-side estimates bullet below).

Your four peer checks, for Xvivo's cohort:

  • Sell-side estimates: Bonesupport's, Vitrolife's and Surgical Science's one-year-forward EPS rose 6% to 11% over 3 months, with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of +80% to +100%; Ambu's fell 2%; Xvivo's fell 14%, with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of (86%). The ranking matches only at the ends: Xvivo, with the deepest cuts, is the only faller, but Surgical Science, with the largest EPS rise (11%), rose least of the four (14.9%), and Ambu rose 21.0% on a 2% EPS cut, so October's healthcare rotation lifted the cohort as well as its estimates.
  • SP technicals: Bonesupport (Stock RS 97) and Surgical Science (98) lead; Vitrolife (62) and Ambu (48) were below 25 a month ago. This week Bonesupport fell 2.3% after its 24.3% results-day rise.
  • Newsflow: Bonesupport's third-quarter report of 22-Oct beat on sales and operating profit on United States sales (Redeye, 22-Oct); Getinge's third-quarter report beat on margins with its Paragonix organ-transport business "outstanding" (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 26-Oct), which supports the demand for organ perfusion but says nothing about Xvivo's margins. Traferox received United States clearance for a cold heart preservation system in September (AlphaSense earnings summary, 30-Oct), a new competitor in the market Xvivo's heart application targets.
  • Company guidance and communications: Bonesupport beat and raised; Terumo raised its guidance in August 2026 with its organ business (OrganOx) growing 39% to 50% (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo); Xvivo gives no numerical guidance and its 2027 margin targets are, on consensus, a year late (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).

Who turned first: Surgical Science's Stock RS (from 4 three months ago), then Bonesupport and Vitrolife after their 22-Oct reports, while Xvivo's fell from 75 on 18-Sep; every other sector stock on the page rose 6% to 16% over the month. Xvivo's daily moves track no stock closely (Vimian, the most correlated, at 0.29): Xvivo trades on its own news.

What Xvivo's peers imply: for the rules, the peers matter through Xvivo's Sector RS, which at 67 now keeps the test "Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70" clear; a reversal of October's healthcare rotation would bring it back by the 30-Nov month-end. For the stock, the peers show what the market pays for (rising estimates), and Xvivo has none. The next read-across is TransMedics' third-quarter report on 5-Nov, which the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo names as the peer result that has moved Xvivo's multiple in sympathy.

The three findings that matter most for your decision on Xvivo:

1. SP and MAs

Theon's SP has rebounded 11.8% from its 8-Oct closing low (€27.22) to €30.42, after a 29.0% fall from its 14-Aug closing high (€38.32); the rebound is all Defence's, and Theon is still below its falling 100D, 150D and absolute 200D MAs.

  • From the 31-Jul month-end close to 30-Oct Theon fell 8.4% against Defence's (2.1%) and the Stoxx Europe 600's (0.4%); over the Market chart's own 63-session window (from 4-Aug) it fell 12.6% against (6.5%) and (1.5%). Over 12 months it rose 4.1% against +6.2% and +12.5% (Market chart, computed).
  • Theon's daily moves have run at 1.29 times Defence's over 63 sessions (correlation 0.62, computed). At that ratio Defence's 9.5% rise from 8 to 30-Oct accounts for about 12 points, more than Theon's whole 11.8% rebound.
  • Theon's +5.6% last week is 0.6 times its usual weekly move once the market's part of it is removed, so it is not a major move on your rule; Defence rose 4.7% over the same week.

The last week, leg by leg against Defence (Market chart):

  • 22 to 27-Oct: Theon +7.5% against Defence's +2.3%, carried by 26-Oct (+4.0% on 2.07 times the 50D average volume, item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).

Its cause is not in the sources read; Jefferies (28-Oct, "Defense Likely at Peak Fundamentals vs. Aero") and Bernstein (28-Oct, on soldier-equipment demand) wrote on the industry that day, neither on a Theon-specific change.

Theon announced that day the completion of its 80% purchase of Merio (gyrostabilised gimbals), with a €5m drone-system order on completion (Theon press release; MT Newswires, 29-Oct).
  • Read 28 and 29-Oct together: from the 27 to the 29-Oct close Theon rose 2.1% against Defence's 3.0%, so the 29-Oct jump repaired Theon's own 28-Oct fall and added nothing of its own. On 30-Oct Theon fell 0.7% and Defence fell 1.3%.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file, with Theon's 29-Oct press release and the broker notes cited in the AlphaSense research filed on 29 to 31-Oct (Files/THEON-NL/47-kq-research and 48-kq-analysis).

Two hypotheses, ranked by the weight of evidence.

  • Hypothesis 1, the stronger: Theon is a higher-beta passenger on Defence, whose multiple is being cut. For it: the 1.29 ratio explains the whole rebound; the research of 29-Oct finds the European defence de-rating since July "primarily driven by valuation multiple normalization and crowded position unwinding", with the industry's premium over the Stoxx Europe 600 down from 60% to 12% (Bernstein, 9-Oct) while estimates held. Against it: Defence has already turned up on the short term. Test: Theon's ratio to Defence on the next Defence fall; Rheinmetall's results, 5-Nov.
  • Hypothesis 2: the market also charges Theon for its own debt. For it: Theon lagged Defence by 6.3 points over 3 months and by 5.0 since the 30-Apr close; ING cut its PT for leverage (15-Sep, items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)). Against it: since 8-Oct Theon has moved with Defence at its usual ratio, so no new debt discount was added in October. Test: Theon's net debt commentary and book-to-bill at its 9-Nov third-quarter trading update.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "SP has been choppy and sideways all through 2026. I think that is still better than the rest of the defence industry - but check". Year to date yes, otherwise no: Theon is up 14.6% since 31-Dec-2025 against Defence's +13.1%, but behind Defence over 3, 6 and 12 months (above).

On 28-Oct you wrote: "Real shame. Why has the trade rolled over... Confusing at 15X P/E with guidance upgrades". The sources read give three causes, one of them Theon's own: Defence's multiple fell (Hypothesis 1); ING applied a lower multiple for leverage (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)); and the guidance upgrades raised FY2026 EPS and EBITDA but not FY2027 EPS, which fell 2.3% over 12 months on the interest of the €325m term loan (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).

  • Theon regained its 20D MA on 23-Oct and its 50D on 29-Oct (€30.64 against €30.54), then slipped below the 50D on 30-Oct (€30.42 against €30.44). It is 4.9% below its 100D (€31.98), 4.8% below its 150D (€31.94) and 3.6% below its absolute 200D MA (€31.54).

  • Near its 52W high: fails, 20.6% below €38.32.
  • Away from its low: passes, 29.0% above its 52W low (€23.58, 26-Nov-2025) and 11.8% above 8-Oct.
  • Stage 3 (topping) red flags: the largest falls since the August top, partly (28-Oct's (4.6%) is second to 24-Aug's (4.9%), though on light volume); wider swings, present (an average daily move of 3.3% over five sessions against 1.9% over the previous 20); lower lows, absent since 8-Oct (higher lows on 22-Oct, €27.92, and 28-Oct, €28.64); more down days than up, absent since 8-Oct (9 up, 7 down); a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume, absent; the SP tracking a rising MA, not applicable.
  • Verdict: a broken trend on the medium-term tests, with a three-week base forming inside it. Would settle it: a close above €31.98 on heavy volume (the base breaking out) or below €27.92 (the decline resuming).

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Mechanical-volume days: 31-Aug and 18-Sep (the September option expiry) sit in Theon's 50D average; the volume script's ratios without them match the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's (up/down volume 1.22 over 20 sessions), so the figures below are the dashboard's.

Theon's rally from the 8-Oct low is being bought: up days carry twice the down days' volume.

  • From 8 to 30-Oct Theon's 9 up days averaged 150,413 shares and its 7 down days 96,324, so the average up day traded 1.56 times the average down day, and total up-day volume was 2.01 times total down-day volume (computed); on the same total-volume basis the ratio from 14-Aug to 8-Oct was 0.62. Over 5 sessions the ratio is 1.31 and over 20 it is 1.22 (PMS Per-stock Dashboard); 20D volume runs 1.06 times the 50D, steady rather than surging.
  • Precedent: Theon's completed rallies of 13% or more in 12 months ran at up/down volume of 1.65 to 3.37 and went 25% to 34%; this one is at 2.01 and 11.8%.

Theon's heavy-volume days (1.5 times the 50D average or more) in 25 sessions: four before the low, three of them falls; three after it, all rises.

  • Before: 28-Sep, (3.5%) on 1.85 times (196,948 shares, €5.76m), closing at the low as Defence fell 0.4%; 1-Oct, a 1.2% gap up after Theon's 30-Sep order release sold to 7% of the range on 1.57 times (€4.89m); 7-Oct, (3.7%) on 2.22 times (244,311 shares, €6.80m); 8-Oct, (2.2%) on 1.50 times (€4.55m), the low.
  • 15-Oct: +5.6% on 1.65 times (185,061 shares, €5.36m), closing at 98% of the range, with MilDef +11.2% on 3.7 times.
  • 26-Oct: +4.0% on 2.07 times (233,420 shares, €6.99m), closing at 82% of the range; Theon's own buying, since only Exail Technologies among the peers traded heavily.
  • 29-Oct: +7.0% on 1.70 times (198,986 shares, €6.10m), closing at 86% of the range; Kongsberg Gruppen (4.4 times), MilDef (2.2 times) and Ovzon (1.9 times) traded heavily too, so this was the group's volume.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Large down-day on 28-Oct". Large, but light: Theon fell 4.6% on 94,196 shares (€2.70m), 0.82 times the 50D average, closing at the low; few holders sold, and 29-Oct recovered it on twice the volume. It was not a distribution day.

Theon's distribution days are falling away: the PMS Per-stock Dashboard counts 3 in the 25 sessions to 30-Oct, from 4 on 23-Oct, all on 6 to 8-Oct; none since the low, against five accumulation days (9, 15, 16, 26 and 29-Oct). The count reaches zero by 12-Nov unless new ones appear.

On 28-Oct you wrote: ""Since the 8-Oct low, Theon's heavy volume has come on up days, and its one pullback was light" is positive though - confusing". Both readings are true, on different horizons. The volume says sellers were exhausted at 8-Oct; the MAs and RS (items 1 and 3) say the medium-term trend is still down. This volume is what a base looks like before a breakout, and also what a rally inside a downtrend looks like before it fails at falling MAs; it cannot yet tell them apart. Four of the five earlier declines in 12 months ended with a heavy-volume up day within ten sessions of the low (8-Dec-2025, +7.6% on 1.5 times; 7-Jan, +5.9% on 1.9 times; 18-Feb, +6.6% on 2.0 times; 1-Apr, +8.4% on 2.9 times), and 15-Oct, the fifth session, fits. The exception, the 13-May low, had no day above 1.1 times in its first ten sessions and still began the 33.4% rise to the 14-Aug high.

Theon's relative price is 20.8% below its 52W high (7-Apr-2026) and 7.9% below its relative 200D MA at the 30-Oct month-end; it did not confirm the SP's 14-Aug high (9.5% below that day), and is 9.8% above its 13-Oct low.

  • On 28-Oct you wrote: "Currently right at the 25% less than 52W relative high line - almost exit criteria - at ~28.5 EUR". Right for 28-Oct, when Theon closed at €28.64, 25.5% below; but the test reads the month-end, and on Fri 30-Oct it read 20.8%, no sell signal. At a flat market the line for the 30-Nov reading is a close of €28.80, 5.3% lower; the 7-Apr high stays in the window until April 2027.

Volume that would confirm a turn: a close above €31.98 (the 100D MA) on 1.5 times the 50D average or more, on a day Defence rises less. That would confirm the decline resumes: a close below €27.92 (the 22-Oct low) on above-average volume, Theon's first distribution day since 8-Oct.

3. RS

Theon's Stock RS and Sector RS have turned up from their October lows, but Defence's Industry RS has not moved: the turn is the stock's and the sector's, not yet the industry's.

  • Theon's Stock RS was 32 at the 30-Oct month-end, from 19 a month earlier (30-Sep), 57 three months earlier (31-Jul) and 96 twelve months earlier (31-Oct-2025). Its low was 16 on the 9-Oct Friday reading.
  • Theon's Sector RS (Defence - Non-primes) was 21 at 30-Oct, from 8 on 23-Oct, 13 at 30-Sep, 5 at 31-Jul and 98 a year ago.
  • Defence's Industry RS has read 13 at seven readings in a row since 30-Sep, against 40 at 31-Aug and 99 a year ago, although Defence beat the Stoxx Europe 600 by 6.1 points from 8 to 30-Oct. Hypothesis: the reading weighs longer periods, in which Defence lagged (3 and 12 months, item 1 (SP and MAs)), so one month's outperformance has not lifted its rank.

Against the Sell Criteria's lines, Theon sits below every one: Stock RS 32 is 38 points below 70 and 18 below 50; Sector RS 21 is 29 below 50; Industry RS 13 is 17 below 30.

The order of this turn matches Theon's last one, in July 2026: the stock first, then the sector, then the industry.

  • In July Theon's Stock RS rose from 30 (3-Jul) to 69 (24-Jul) while Industry RS stayed at 7 to 13 and Sector RS at 4 to 11. Defence's Industry RS then jumped from 13 (31-Jul) to 59 (7-Aug) and 73 (11-Aug), and Theon's SP rose 15.4% from 31-Jul to its 14-Aug high, against Defence's 8.4%.
  • Now Theon's Stock RS has doubled from 16 to 32 in three weeks and Sector RS has risen from 8 to 21 in one, while Industry RS waits at 13.
  • The difference from July: in July Theon's absolute 200D MA was rising and its PT Net Upgrade Breadth was +56% (30-Jun month-end); now that MA is falling, PT Net Upgrade Breadth is (25%) and FY2027 EPS is being cut (items 4, 5 and 6). Would confirm the rotation: Defence's Industry RS above 30 at a Friday reading. Would refute it: Theon's Stock RS back below 22, its 23-Oct reading.

The links: Theon's Stock RS rose with the up-day volume of 15, 26 and 29-Oct (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)) and Defence's rally, not with estimates, since FY2027 EPS fell over the month (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). Hensoldt's Stock RS rose from 21 to 46 and Kongsberg Gruppen's from 41 to 75 over the same month (item 9 (Peers)), faster than Theon's.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts are raising Theon's FY2026 EPS and every year's EBITDA, while trimming FY2027 and FY2028 EPS: the operating case is being upgraded, and the shareholder's line beyond this year is not.

  • Theon's FY2026 EPS consensus rose 2.6% on 30-Oct, to €1.563, after a 6.3% rise at the 30-Sep month-end; it is up 9.1% in 3 months and 11.6% in 12. The cause of the 30-Oct step is not in the sources read; the 30-Sep step followed the 7-Sep first-half report, which raised FY2026 targets to about €600m of revenue and an adjusted EBIT margin above 26% (earnings review).
  • Theon's FY2027 EPS consensus fell 1.6% over the month to 30-Oct, to €1.654, and is down 2.3% over 12 months. Theon's FY2028 EPS fell 0.7% over the month and 1.7% over 3 months, though it is up 12.2% over 12.
  • Theon's EBITDA consensus rose over every period for every year: FY2026 +1.7% in a month and +12.6% in 12 to €161.8m; FY2027 +1.2% and +18.0% to €202.8m; FY2028 +1.7% and +32.6% to €242.5m.
  • Theon's revenue consensus is flat for FY2026 (€600.6m) and edging up for FY2027 (+0.4% in a month, €743.1m) and FY2028 (+0.6%, €871.7m).

The gap between EBITDA and EPS growth is the whole debate about Theon: FY2027 EBITDA is forecast to grow 25.3% over FY2026, but FY2027 EPS only 5.8%.

  • The implied growth from FY2027 to FY2028 is 19.6% for EBITDA and 19.2% for EPS, so the gap is a FY2027 gap. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 26.9% (FY2026) to 27.3% (FY2027) and 27.8% (FY2028).
  • The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Oct) puts the gap down to 8.6m new shares and about €550m of peak net debt; Deutsche Bank trimmed EPS in September for the term loan's interest. Each acquisition (Merio, completed 29-Oct; HGH, expected early 2027) adds EBITDA before EPS.
  • Hypothesis: the market prices Theon on FY2027 EPS, which has fallen, not on EBITDA, which has risen, so guidance upgrades have not lifted the SP (your 28-Oct question, item 1 (SP and MAs)). Against it: Defence's multiple fell over the same weeks with its estimates intact, so the multiple did most of the work.

The analysts' margins sit above the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's counter-case. Theon's consensus EBITDA margin rises each year to FY2028 (27.8%), so analysts model no margin compression; the experts quoted in the memo expect operating margins to fall to 15% to 18% at the cycle's peak as the OCCAR framework volumes come through. The memo's Invalidate test is a reported half-year adjusted EBIT margin below 26%.

What would confirm the operating upgrades are reaching the shareholder: FY2027 EPS revisions turning up after Theon's 9-Nov third-quarter trading update, with a book-to-bill of 1.0 or more (the memo's test). What would refute it: a further FY2027 EPS cut at higher interest or share count.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Theon's analysts raise EBITDA while cutting EPS and PTs: FY2027 EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth is +50%, FY2027 EPS Net Upgrade Breadth (14%) and PT Net Upgrade Breadth (25%).

  • Theon's FY2027 EPS Net Upgrade Breadth turned negative at the 30-Oct month-end, (14%) with 2 raising and 3 cutting of 7, after four month-ends at zero.
  • Theon's FY2027 EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth rose to +50% (4 raising, none cutting, of 8) from +30% at 30-Sep; revenue Net Upgrade Breadth is +25% (2 raising, none cutting, of 8, 28-Oct).
  • Theon's PT Net Upgrade Breadth is (25%) (none raising, 2 cutting, of 8, 28-Oct), from (11%) at 30-Sep, +10% in July and August and +78% in May.
  • What it means: nothing in Theon's breadth points to weaker demand; it points to less EPS per euro of EBITDA (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)) and a lower price for it.

The brokers cutting, and their stated logic:

  • ING (Tijs Hollestelle), 15-Sep: Buy kept; PT cut to €38 from €42.50 on a lower 2027 multiple of enterprise value to earnings before interest and tax (EBIT) of 16.1 times, "citing investor sensitivity to leverage", benchmarked to land-defence peers whose multiple fell from 21.6 to 17.2 times between January and September 2026; Theon removed from ING's favourites list on near-term macro headwinds. ING also called Theon's raised margin target "a highly positive surprise" (earnings review; research filed 29 to 31-Oct).
  • On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note, quoting a sentence from the APM's earlier commentary (whose item numbers differ from today's): ""ING put its PT cut (item 1 (SP and MAs)) down to the European defence sector as a whole, not to Theon's numbers, which rose (item 3 (Consensus by named FY); earnings review)" is instructive of the issues - what does the note say". ING's cut is a multiple cut on two counts, the land-defence peers' lower multiple and a leverage discount, with Theon's forecasts raised: better operations, a lower price for them.
  • Deutsche Bank (Sriram Krishnan): Buy and €39 PT kept after the first half, but EPS trimmed in September for the financing costs of the €325m term loan; Krishnan warned that integration delays at the French acquisitions could stop Theon reaching its raised margin target (earnings review; research filed 31-Oct).
  • The second PT cut in Theon's count and the three FY2027 EPS cuts at 30-Oct are not named in the sources read.

Theon's ratings have not moved with the SP's fall: 8 Buys and no Sells at 30-Oct, against 8 Buys, 1 other and no Sells at 30-Sep; one neutral analyst left the count. Theon's analysts upgraded after falls before (Buys from 5 to 7 at the November 2025 month-end as the SP fell 17.9%), but with every remaining analyst at Buy, ratings cannot signal a turn; PTs and breadth will.

Theon's average PT, €39.88, is 31.1% above its SP; its rise from €39.13 (29-Sep) came as the PTs counted fell from 9 to 8, not from a raise. The two times in 12 months the gap reached about 35% (the November 2025 and June 2026 month-ends), Theon's SP rose by about a quarter over the next six to seven weeks.

Would confirm a turn: two PT raises with no new cut, taking PT Net Upgrade Breadth to zero or above. Would refute it: further FY2027 EPS cuts after the 9-Nov update.

9. Peers

In the rebound from the 8-Oct low Theon trails the defence electronics leaders: Kongsberg Gruppen +22.1%, Ovzon +18.9%, MilDef +18.0% and Hensoldt +14.6%, against Theon's +11.8% (chart files, 8 to 30-Oct). Only Exosens (+3.5%) and Exail Technologies (+1.0%, anchored by Thales's agreed €134 a share for the Gorgé family's stake) rose less. In the fall from 14-Aug to 8-Oct Theon's (29.0%) was the cohort's worst, against Hensoldt's (22.3%), Exosens' (17.7%) and Kongsberg Gruppen's (13.4%).

Theon's four peer checks, against its closest peers:

  • SP technicals: Hensoldt has closed above its absolute 200D MA at 14 of the 16 sessions since 8-Oct and is 7.5% above it; Kongsberg Gruppen crossed above its absolute 200D MA on its 29-Oct results and is 8.8% above it; MilDef is 39.0% above its own and 2.6% from its 52W high. Theon (3.6% below), Exosens (5.4% below) and Ovzon (18.5% below) are the three cohort members still below their absolute 200D MAs.
  • Sell-side estimates: Theon's 3-month EPS revision, (1%), is the cohort's second weakest, against MilDef's +24%, Exosens' +7%, Kongsberg Gruppen's +3% and Hensoldt's +2% (PMS Per-stock Dashboard peer panel).
  • Newsflow: Kongsberg Gruppen's third-quarter report (29-Oct) showed a strong order backlog (DNB Carnegie, 29-Oct) with margin risks (Morgan Stanley, 29-Oct: "Margin Risks Overshadow Improved Revenue Visibility"), and its SP rose 13.0% on 4.4 times its 50D average volume. Jefferies upgraded Hensoldt to Buy on 5-Oct ("Sensing Opportunity in the Pullback").
  • Company guidance: Exosens raised its FY2026 guidance on 17-Sep on a faster capacity ramp; Theon raised its own on 7-Sep. Both raised, and both still lag.

Hypothesis: the market is treating the night-vision pair, Theon and Exosens, as short-cycle soldier equipment rather than long-cycle defence electronics.

  • For it: Theon and Exosens are tied by Theon's 9.8% stake and the 400,000-tube supply contract, and Exosens is Theon's most correlated stock (average correlation 0.54, PMS Per-stock Dashboard). Both are the cohort's laggards since 8-Oct despite raised guidance at both. The industry research of 29 and 30-Oct finds short-cycle land equipment and ammunition hit hardest and long-cycle electronics, missiles and air defence given a premium (Bernstein, 29-Sep). The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's counter-case calls soldier-equipment demand "a two-to-three-year replenishment surge that peaks in 2027 to 2029".
  • Against it: Bernstein (28-Oct) argues European armies are moving to one night-vision device per soldier, which makes the demand durable; and Exosens' estimates are rising fast (+7% in 3 months), so its lag is not an estimates problem.
  • Test: Exosens' and Theon's moves against Hensoldt's on Hensoldt's nine-month results (5-Nov) and Theon's trading update (9-Nov). If both night-vision names lag again on a good Defence day, the market is pricing the segment, not Theon's debt alone.

What the peers imply for Theon's sell tests: Theon's Stock RS (32) sits with Exosens (43) and Ovzon (22), not with Hensoldt (46) or Kongsberg Gruppen (75). A peer rally lifts Theon's Sector RS faster than its Stock RS and relative price, because Theon captures less of it than the leaders.

What the rules say for Theon now, and what decides the next step

  • Theon is a passenger on Defence. On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Really, to own Theon is to underwrite the defence trade. What is going on there?". Agreed: at 1.29 times Defence's daily moves, Defence explains all of Theon's rebound since 8-Oct (item 1 (SP and MAs)). Morgan Stanley (23-Oct) names a sharp, sustained fall in European government bond yields as the signal that Defence's de-rating is reversing: the yields your 28-Oct market note sees rising.
  • The operating case is being upgraded; the shareholder's case is not. FY2026 EPS and every year's EBITDA are rising, while FY2027 EPS, its Net Upgrade Breadth and PT Net Upgrade Breadth are falling (items 4, 5 and 6). On 28-Oct you wrote: "Holding here looks like hope. This looks classic Stage 3 IMHO. Exit unless we can prove strong upgrade scope". The data show no upgrade scope at the FY2027 EPS line yet; the 9-Nov trading update is the dated event that could.
  • A base is forming inside a broken trend (items 1 and 2).
  • What the rules say: Theon is a Middle Innings Core Investment, sold by the six Sell Criteria alone. At the Fri 30-Oct readings four give whole-holding sell signals: Industry RS 13; the relative 200D MA (1.17%) over the month; Sector RS 21 with Stock RS 32; and SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth (25%) with Stock RS 32. Under your one-off in/out link Theon is sold when its replacement is bought, one purchase releasing one holding in the Assistant Portfolio Manager's order of sale; nothing sells it at the Tue 3-Nov session otherwise.
  • The tranches: tranche 1 (2.00%; 22-Jun-2026 is the start of the position record, which may not be its purchase date) and tranche 2 (2.05%, bought 30-Jun-2026) hold until then; neither has a 30% fall triggered.
  • The nearest further tests: relative price is 20.8% below its 52W high, from 23.8% a week earlier, 21.2% four weeks earlier and 17.2% thirteen weeks earlier; a close below €28.80 at the 30-Nov month-end (5.3% lower, at a flat market) gives that sell signal. Both tranches' 30% level is €26.82, 11.8% lower; a close below it triggers both, each sold at the Tuesday session after its release, even under your link.
  • How one fall would set off both: at Theon's 1.29 ratio, a Defence fall of about 4% (with the Stoxx Europe 600 flat) reaches the €28.80 line and about 9% reaches €26.82; Defence fell 17.6% from 14-Aug to 8-Oct.
  • What would take the standing signals away: the relative 200D MA cannot turn up by Fri 6-Nov (relative price would need to rise 59%); by the late-November Fridays it needs relative price about 7% above 30-Oct's, held through November, because the values leaving it (late January to February 2026) average 18.03 against today's 16.85 (computed). Industry RS needs Defence above 30 at a Friday reading (13 to 59 in one week in August); the Sector RS test needs Sector RS of 50 or Stock RS of 70 at the 30-Nov month-end.
  • The record: on today's rules Theon's Industry RS, Sector RS and relative-price tests read as sell signals around the 30-Jun month-end, the day of tranche 2's purchase. A sale at the Tue 7-Jul session (€32.64) would have been 7.3% above today's close, though it would have missed the 17.4% rise to the 14-Aug high, which came with an industry rotation (Industry RS 13 to 73 in six weeks).
  • Next readings and adds: Fri 6-Nov reads Industry RS and the relative 200D MA, and both will very probably still give sell signals; Mon 30-Nov reads the other three. The rules block an add because four Sell Criteria give sell signals, although 2.63% of room remains below the 6.68% limit.
  • Where Theon stands for an add: none of the three stages (pulling back or basing; may be retesting a rising MA or forming a volatility contraction pattern; healthily retesting a rising MA or breaking out of one). The pullback has become a broken trend. The Master Dashboard (built 1-Nov) points to the second stage ("Healthy Retest: Plausible (4 of 6 tests)" at the 50D MA), but that 50D MA fell 8.15% in 20 sessions, so it is not a rising MA; Basing reads "Possible" (1 of 4 tests), the pullback and both volatility contraction pattern tests none. Neither add trigger is present (item 12 (adding capital)).
  • Dates: Hensoldt's nine-month results and Rheinmetall's results, 5-Nov; Theon's third-quarter trading update, 9-Nov (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's date; the data file shows only FY2026 results, projected for 19-Mar-2027); the Bundestag budget vote, 27-Nov; the Boxer options, early December.
  • What would change this reading: a close above €31.98 on heavy volume, with a book-to-bill of 1.0 or more on 9-Nov, would start to repair the trend; a close below €27.92 would end the base.

Richard decides.
