    • Why: Item 1 (SP and MAs) finds four of your six trend-health tests failing, and item 4 (Consensus by named FY) finds FY2027 EPS cut, so the upgrade scope is not yet shown.

Avanza

Middle Innings Core Investment · 3.07% of capital in 2 tranches (tranche 1, 0.50%, bought 22-Jun-2026, the start of the position record rather than a confirmed purchase date; tranche 2, 2.57%, bought 16-Oct-2026) · readings as at Fri 30-Oct-2026

1. SP and MAs

Avanza's SP has fallen 13.5% from its 28-Aug high, and its bounce from the 23-Oct low is the weakest of its four lows in 12 months.

  • Over 3 months Avanza's SP fell 10.6% (from 394.9 on 4-Aug to 353.1 on 30-Oct), against the Stoxx Europe 600's (1.5%) and Financials' (3.3%). Over 12 months it is down 3.0%.
  • Over the 20 sessions to 30-Oct Avanza fell 9.6% while the market rose 2.4%; the part of the fall not explained by the market, given Avanza's beta of 0.9, is (11.8%), 1.8 times its usual 20-session move.
  • The moves between turning points, on closes: +26.8% from 322.1 (13-May) to 408.3 (28-Aug); (14.4%) to 349.7 (23-Oct); +1.0% to 353.1 (30-Oct), not yet a swing.

Inside the decline, the week before the results was Avanza's own and the week after was its sector's (compounded closes).

  • 12 to 16-Oct: Avanza fell 5.1%, while Nordnet fell 1.5%, Fineco 0.1% and Flatex 0.3%. The selling started on 13-Oct, three sessions before the 16-Oct third-quarter report; its cause is not in the sources read.
  • 16 to 23-Oct: Avanza fell 4.4%, less than Nordnet's 5.7% and Flatex's 12.3%. Over these five sessions the platforms fell together.
  • 23 to 30-Oct: Avanza rose 1.0% while Nordnet rose 2.9% (up on all five days). Avanza's (0.9%) fall on 30-Oct matched the industry's (Financials (1.0%), cohort average (1.6%)), so that day was not Avanza's own; its lag behind Nordnet over the week is its own.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file.

  • 16-Oct (Avanza, third-quarter report; earnings review filed 22-Oct): operating profit SEK 920m, about 2% below the company-compiled consensus, with every core revenue line short (brokerage 5%, currency income 8%); annualised income per customer fell 6% in the quarter to SEK 2,170 while savings capital rose 21% year on year. Avanza cut its 2026 Danish cost to SEK 40m from SEK 50m.
  • No Avanza Development is in the sources read for 23 to 30-Oct.
  • Hypotheses, ranked. (1) The market has repriced Avanza's revenue per customer ahead of the analysts: for, the pre-results selling, the results-day volume and a falling average PT with FY2027 EPS flat (item 4 (Consensus by named FY), Consensus); against, the fall since 16-Oct matches Nordnet's. (2) The rest is a de-rating of the Nordic and continental brokers: for, Sector RS from 85 (31-Jul) to 17. Test, 4 to 6-Nov: Avanza against Nordnet on the Riksbank decision.

The MAs: Avanza has closed below its rising 150D MA since 16-Oct and its rising absolute 200D MA since 19-Oct, and has not regained either in ten sessions.

  • The order of the breaks: the 20D MA (1-Sep), 50D (2-Sep), 100D (24-Sep; regained with the 20D on 12-Oct on 0.65x and lost again with it on 13-Oct on 2.20x), 150D (16-Oct, on 4.23x the 50D average volume) and absolute 200D (19-Oct, on 1.98x).
  • The 20D (366.65) and 50D (381.44) MAs are falling, (5.25%) and (3.02%) over 20 sessions; the 100D (385.86) is flat over 5 sessions; the 150D (373.29) and absolute 200D (361.53) are rising, +1.49% and +0.94% over 20 sessions.
  • Avanza's May precedent: below its 150D and absolute 200D MAs from 24-Apr, 5.0% under the absolute 200D MA at the 13-May low, both regained on 21-May; then that MA was flat to falling, now it is rising, the better structure.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Avanza against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes; +0.94% over 20 sessions and +0.18% over 5.
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes (373.29 against 361.53).
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: partly; above both, but falling.
  • The SP above each MA: fails; Avanza closed below all five, by 3.7% (20D), 7.4% (50D), 8.5% (100D), 5.4% (150D) and 2.3% (absolute 200D).
  • Near its 52W high: fails; 13.5% below the 408.3 closing high of 28-Aug.
  • Away from its 52W low: partly; 13.7% above the 310.48 closing low of 24-Feb.
  • Stage 3 red flag, the largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began: present; the (2.8%) falls of 16 and 19-Oct and the (7.8%) five-session fall to 19-Oct exceed the advance's largest, (2.6%) and (4.2%). The 16-Oct fall came on results that missed, not on good news.
  • Wider swings: absent (mean absolute daily move 1.12% against 1.10% in the advance).
  • Lower lows: present (375.9 on 24-Sep, 349.7 on 23-Oct; relative price lower again on 30-Oct).
  • More down days than up days: present; 25 down and 20 up since 28-Aug.
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: present; the 12-Oct close back above the 20D and 100D MAs on 0.65x was reversed on 13-Oct ((1.4%) on 2.20x).
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly; for ten sessions Avanza has hugged the underside of its rising absolute 200D MA without bouncing back above it.
  • Verdict: the long MAs are intact, but the SP and the red flags (four of the six present, a fifth in part) say broken trend more than healthy retest. It would be settled by a close above the absolute 200D MA (361.5) on 1.5x the 50D average volume or more (a retest that held), or a close below 349.7 on above-average volume (the decline continuing).
  • The counter-case: your 31-Oct principle (on Carlsberg) that a price holding less than 5% below an MA "is holding/testing" fits Avanza, 0.1% to 3.1% below its absolute 200D MA for ten sessions; against it, relative price kept falling while the SP held (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Avanza's decline from 28-Aug has the largest share of heavy-volume down days of its four declines in 12 months, and the heavy volume has drained away since 21-Oct without buyers stepping in.

  • 7 of Avanza's 23 down days in the decline traded at 1.5x the 50D average or more, against 3 of 15 (April to May 2026), 2 of 8 (February) and 4 of 31 (October 2025 to January 2026) (counted from the daily volume data, mechanical-volume days left out).
  • Up-day volume averages 0.77 times down-day volume in this decline, level with February's 0.77 and below April to May's 0.94.
  • On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "The ratio of down-day to up-day volume this month was much more negative than during any other pullback over LTM". The data confirm it for October's concentration of heavy-volume selling (seven heavy days in the twelve sessions from 6 to 21-Oct, six of them down days) and for the per-day ratio, which ties February's as the worst. On total volume the April to May decline was worse (0.22 against 0.51), because it had 15 down days in 19 sessions.

The heavy-volume days of the last 25 sessions cluster from 6 to 21-Oct (volume against the 50D average; value traded).

  • 6-Oct: (2.5%) on 1.71x (SEK 144.5m), closing at the day's low, below the 20D and 100D MAs; the session after the September monthly statistics, when Nordnet fell 1.8% and Flatex 7.1% on heavy volume too.
  • 13 and 14-Oct: (1.4%) on 2.20x (SEK 190.8m), closing in the lowest fifth of the range, then (1.2%) on 1.77x (SEK 154.0m); both before the results.
  • 16-Oct, results day: (2.8%) on 4.23x (SEK 387.0m), Avanza's heaviest day since 21-Apr; it opened 3.8% lower, traded below its absolute 200D MA and closed in the top 30% of the range, below its 150D MA. You bought tranche 2 that day.
  • 19 and 20-Oct: (2.8%) on 1.98x (SEK 180.1m), closing below the absolute 200D MA in the lowest 21% of the range; then (0.2%) on 1.68x (SEK 155.7m).
  • 21-Oct: +1.6% on 1.56x (SEK 149.0m), the only heavy up day, given back on 22-Oct ((2.2%), closing at the low). (1-Oct, +0.2% on 1.95x, was the other heavy day.)

Avanza's distribution-day count is 6 in 25 sessions, down from a peak of 8 on 20-Oct, against 4 on 2-Oct and 1 or 2 through August. The 20-session up/down volume ratio is 0.61 (0.6104 on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard); the 5-session ratio is 1.38, because the bounce had four up days. The mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep move these ratios by 0.01 or less.

The bounce from the 23-Oct low is on falling volume: 1.12x, 0.84x, 1.00x, 0.75x and 0.63x the 50D average in the five sessions to 30-Oct.

  • Precedent: Avanza's February and May declines turned on runs of up days on 0.5x to 0.7x, January's on +1.3% on 1.6x. Light volume after a low is the pattern that preceded Avanza's last two recoveries.
  • What differs now: five sessions after each earlier low Avanza was up 3.1% (January), 3.4% (February) and 6.4% (May); five sessions after 23-Oct it is up 1.0%. Light volume with a small price gain says the selling has paused, not that buyers have returned.
  • Peers on Avanza's heavy days: on 16-Oct Nordnet, AJ Bell and Integrafin traded at 1.6x to 2.1x but moved (0.6%) to +2.5%; on 19-Oct Nordnet fell 2.3% on 0.7x. Those two days were Avanza's own selling.

Avanza's relative price made the decline's lowest close on 30-Oct (195.5), 15.4% below its 52W high of 15-Apr-2026, while the SP stayed 1.0% above its 23-Oct low.

  • Relative price did not confirm the SP's 28-Aug high (it stood 4.6% below its own high then) and has been below its relative 200D MA since 16-Oct, by 6.6% on 30-Oct.
  • Relative price at the SP's earlier lows: 195.4 (20-Jan, today's level), 184.7 (24-Feb, an SP of about 334 at a flat market) and 190.4 (13-May, about 344).

Confirms a low for Avanza: an up day on 1.5x the 50D average or more, closing in the top third of its range and above the absolute 200D MA (361.5), with relative price rising. Confirms the decline continues: a close below 349.7 on above-average volume, taking relative price towards the 184.7 to 190.4 band where the February and May declines ended.

3. RS

Avanza's Sector RS fell to 17 at the 30-Oct month-end, the lowest in the file (from 26-Sep-2025), with Avanza's Stock RS at 33 and Financials' Industry RS at 59.

  • Avanza's Stock RS read 70 (31-Aug), 56 (30-Sep) and 33 (30-Oct); its Sector RS 83, 68 and 17; Financials' Industry RS 86, 79 and 59. Stock RS has fallen 23 points in a month and 37 in three.
  • Against the Sell Criteria's lines: Stock RS is below 70 and 50; Sector RS is far below 50; Industry RS is 29 points above 30.

The order of the turn: the sector first, then Avanza, with Financials last. Avanza's Sector RS fell from 87 (24-Jul) to 62 (25-Sep) while Financials held 86 until 18-Sep and Avanza's Stock RS held 52 to 73. Avanza's Stock RS then fell fastest in the results fortnight, from 51 (9-Oct) to 39 (16-Oct). This is a sector de-rating with Avanza's own results inside it, not an industry rotation.

Avanza's Stock RS (33) is 20 points below Nordnet's (53), its closest peer; the Sector RS is held down by members at Stock RS 2 to 12 (item 9, Peers).

Precedent: Avanza's RS lows have come near SP lows, and Sector RS has recovered fast before. Avanza's Stock RS was last this low on Fri 8-May-2026 (33), three sessions before the 13-May SP low; its lowest in the file is 22 (20-Feb). Its Sector RS went from 38 at the 27-Feb month-end to 73 at the 31-Mar month-end, a 35-point rise in a month; the same rise now would take it to 52, just over the line.

4. Consensus by named FY

Avanza's consensus has barely moved after a third quarter that missed on every core revenue line, while the SP has fallen 8.4% in the month: the price is ahead of the estimates.

  • FY2026 EPS: 19.36, down 0.9% in a month, flat over 3 months, up 6.6% over 6 and 21.9% over 12.
  • FY2027 EPS: 21.06, down 0.1% in a month, up 1.6% over 3 months and 7.5% over 6.
  • FY2028 EPS: 23.27, up 1.2% in a month, 2.8% over 3 months and 8.7% over 6.
  • Revenue: FY2026 SEK 5,200m ((1.0%) in a month), FY2027 SEK 5,743m (flat), FY2028 SEK 6,322m (+0.8%). Avanza, a bank, has no EBITDA consensus.

The analysts cut the near year and raised the far year in October, so the trajectory steepened. Implied EPS growth from FY2027 to FY2028 is 10.5%, up from 9.3% three months ago; from FY2026 to FY2027 it is 8.8%, up from 7.0%. Hypothesis: the far-year lift is the expected Riksbank hike on 4-Nov (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo puts one 25 basis point hike at about SEK 160m a year of net interest income); the SEK 10m lower Danish cost falls in 2026, so it cannot explain the FY2028 lift, and FY2026 EPS fell 0.9% despite it. Against: the third quarter's lower income per customer would, if it lasts, cut every year.

The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (25-Oct): it puts FY2027 EPS at SEK 20.2, 4.1% below the 30-Oct consensus of 21.06, and DNB Carnegie's FY2027 EPS at SEK 19.29, 8.4% below that consensus; it expects consensus cuts of 2% to 4% to FY2027 and 3% to 6% to FY2028 at the mid-January compilation and the 22-Jan-2027 results. It names a Riksbank hold on 4-Nov as "the one event that would turn the whole cohort's revisions negative at once". Test: FY2027 EPS below about 20.6 (a 2% cut) on the daily series by 22-Jan.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Avanza's analysts are cutting PTs and trimming revenue while EPS holds, and every Net Upgrade Breadth measure has turned from positive to zero or below in two months.

  • PT Net Upgrade Breadth: +79% (31-Aug), +24% (30-Sep), (11%) on 28-Oct (5 raising, 7 cutting, of 19); on the data file's 100-day count at the 30-Oct month-end, (16%).
  • Revenue Net Upgrade Breadth: +88% (31-Aug), +22% (30-Sep), 0% on 28-Oct (6 and 6, of 16); on the 100-day count at 30-Oct, (6%).
  • FY2027 EPS Net Upgrade Breadth: +90% (31-Aug), +33% (30-Sep), 0% at 30-Oct (7 and 7, of 19).
  • The average PT fell 2.0% from 421.0 (13-Oct) to 412.6 (28-Oct), 16.9% above the 30-Oct SP; the gap moves mostly with the SP.

The brokers cutting, and their stated logic (earnings review, 22-Oct, unless named):

  • DNB Carnegie (Keric, McBeath, Jonsson; 19-Oct): PT to SEK 425 from 430, Buy kept; FY2026 EPS to SEK 18.90 from 19.04, FY2027 to 19.29 from 19.34, FY2028 to 20.79 from 20.93. Its logic: the quarter was "incrementally negative" because every core income line missed, but the weakness is mainly seasonal and sentiment-driven; a return to first-half trading activity would add about 17% to FY2027 EPS.
  • Nordea (19-Oct, Placera, as recorded in the 28-Oct note): PT to SEK 435 from 465, Buy kept; its stated logic is not in the sources read.
  • Jefferies (Roberts and colleagues): Underperform and SEK 335 kept; the miss "reinforced" its view that lower income per customer, competitive pricing, Denmark and technology costs make sustainable EPS lower than the growth story assumes.
  • Deutsche Bank kept Buy and SEK 440, reading 21% savings-capital growth as the engine intact.
  • On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "I quickly scanned the earnings news and thought it was fine. Yet the ER summary reads more negatively (e.g. PT cuts from at least 2 brokers). I am out of step with the market ATM". The data agree that the market reads it more negatively than the EPS consensus: the two named PT cuts are small (1% and 6%), but 7 of 19 analysts have cut PTs, revenue breadth is negative on the 100-day count, and the SP has fallen 9.3% from 12 to 23-Oct. The disagreement is over whether lower income per customer is cyclical (DNB Carnegie, Deutsche Bank) or structural (Jefferies; the memo puts about two-thirds as structural).

Ratings: Buys 10, other 5, Sells 4 at the 30-Oct month-end, from 11, 6 and 4; two ratings left the count, and the sources read do not say which. Over 24 months Buys rose from 2 of 13 to 11 of 21 as the SP rose: Avanza's ratings follow momentum.

9. Peers

Avanza trades with the Nordic and continental brokers and has lagged even them since its results; the United Kingdom platforms have gone their own way (the peer table and chart on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard).

  • Since the 28-Aug high Avanza fell 13.5%, Nordnet 10.2%, Fineco 12.8%, AJ Bell and Integrafin 5.4% each, and Flatex 38.0%.
  • On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "SP fell a lot more than Nordnet and the sector around its 3Q earnings". Confirmed for the week before the results (12 to 16-Oct: Avanza (5.1%), Nordnet (1.5%)); after the results the two fell alike (Avanza (4.4%), Nordnet (5.7%) to 23-Oct), and in the week to 30-Oct Avanza rose 1.0% to Nordnet's 2.9%.

Your four peer checks, in turn:

  • Sell-side estimates: over 3 months FY2027 EPS is up 2% to 3% at Avanza, Nordnet and Fineco and down 3% at Swissquote and Flatex; Avanza's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (0%, dashboard basis) is the lowest of the risers, against Nordnet's +19% and AJ Bell's +62%. DNB Carnegie cut its Nordnet PT to SEK 380 "on lower peer multiples" (memo).
  • SP technicals: Nordnet is 1.2% above its absolute 200D MA with Stock RS 53 (65 a month ago); Fineco is 3.1% below its absolute 200D MA with Stock RS 29 (57); Avanza is 2.3% below with Stock RS 33 (56). Only Nordnet (53), AJ Bell (61), Integrafin (56) and XTB (94) are above 50 in the sector.
  • Newsflow: Nordnet's third-quarter report (22-Oct) was a 2% miss with operating profit up 24% year on year (memo); Deutsche Bank cut Flatex to Hold, PT €31 from €46 (12-Oct); IG Group fell 26.7% in October, a cause not in the sources read.
  • Company guidance and communications: Avanza changed only its Danish cost line; Nordnet launches in Germany in November and is building a private-banking offer aimed at Avanza's affluent customers (memo).

Who turned first: Flatex (Stock RS 66 to 15 from three months to one month ago), then Nordnet (87 to 65) and Fineco (73 to 57), with Avanza last (70 to 56) but second-fastest in October (56 to 33, 23 points), after Fineco (57 to 29, 28 points). Nordnet, whose daily moves track Avanza's most closely (correlation 0.66), is now the stronger on every measure in the table except the three-month EPS and revenue revisions, where the two are level (2% and 1% each).

What the peers imply for Avanza: Avanza's next move depends on the Swedish platforms, not on the United Kingdom ones, and the 4-Nov Riksbank decision moves Avanza and Nordnet together. Avanza's Sector RS cannot recover by Avanza alone, because the sector's weakest members sit at Stock RS 2 to 12. Test, from Mon 2-Nov: an Avanza up day on heavy volume while Nordnet falls would be the first sign that Avanza's own selling is over; a further week lagging Nordnet would say the market is still marking down Avanza's revenue per customer.

What the rules say for Avanza now, and what decides the next step

  • The finding that matters most: the rules now sell Avanza. Two tests gave a sell signal at the 30-Oct month-end: "Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70" (Sector RS 17, Stock RS 33), and "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50" (SS Sales Net Upgrade Breadth (6%) and SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth (16%) on the 100-day count, with Stock RS 33). Under your one-off in/out link Avanza is sold when its replacement is bought, in the order of sale set in Group 3 (Portfolio).
  • Second: the bounce is the weakest of Avanza's four lows in 12 months: +1.0% in five sessions on falling volume (3.1% to 6.4% before), with relative price at a new decline low and Avanza lagging Nordnet.
  • Third: the estimates have not caught up with the price: FY2027 EPS is flat while PT and revenue breadth are negative, and the memo expects 2% to 4% FY2027 cuts by 22-Jan; the Riksbank on Wed 4-Nov can turn the cohort's revisions either way.
  • What the rules say for each tranche: both tranches (0.50% and 2.57%) are sold with the whole holding when a replacement is bought; neither is near its 30% fall. Tranche 2, at 2.57% of capital, is above the 1.67% Core Investment tranche size; this matters only if Avanza is kept.
  • The nearest further test, the 200D MA of relative price flat or falling over the month (read each Friday): +0.24% over the month on 16 and 23-Oct, +0.22% on 30-Oct. At a flat relative price it reads about +0.10% on Fri 6-Nov and (0.18%) on Fri 13-Nov, a sell signal; a 4.3% relative fall (an SP of about 338 at a flat market) brings it to 6-Nov, and only a relative rise of about 3.8% by 13-Nov avoids it.
  • SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 (month-end): a sell signal at the 30-Oct month-end, the second beside the Sector RS test: SS Sales Net Upgrade Breadth (6%) and SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth (16%) on the 100-day count (6 raising and 7 cutting sales of 16 analysts; 5 raising and 8 cutting PTs of 19), with Stock RS 33. Under your one-off in/out link it changes nothing about when Avanza sells: it is sold when its replacement is bought.
  • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high (month-end): (15.4%) at 30-Oct, from (7.6%) at 30-Sep; the line is SEK 313.1 at a flat market, 11.3% below the close, 3.4 times Avanza's usual weekly move.
  • How the tests interact: one fall stacks them. A 4.3% fall brings the relative 200D MA test forward to 6-Nov; an 11.3% fall gives a sell signal on the 52W-high test at the 30-Nov month-end; a 19.1% fall (to 285.81) triggers tranche 1's 30% test, because Stock RS is already below 70, and that tranche sells at the Tuesday after its release even under the link.
  • What would take the month-end readings away: for the Sector RS test, Sector RS back to 50 (33 points) or Stock RS to 70 (37 points) by 30-Nov; a rise of that size has happened once in the file (item 3 (RS), RS). For the breadth test, Stock RS back to 50 (17 points) or both SS Sales and SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth back to zero.
  • For your ruling: whether a sell signal under the one-off in/out link still sells Avanza if, by the time its replacement is bought, the test no longer gives a sell signal at a later reading; the rules file does not say.
  • The record: at the 27-Feb-2026 month-end (Sector RS 38, Stock RS 23) the same test would have sold at 321.08 on 3-Mar, before a rise to 389.6 by 15-Apr; the breadth test would also have sold at 341.42 (3-Feb) and 339.2 (2-Jun), with a mixed record: the 3-Feb sale came one session before the 4-Feb swing high of 350.1 and ahead of an 11.3% fall to 310.5 on 24-Feb, while the 2-Jun sale came 5.3% above the 13-May low of 322.1, before a 20.4% rise to 408.3 by 28-Aug. What differs now: Sector RS is 17, not 38, and the estimate cuts are ahead, not behind.
  • The next reading day: Fri 6-Nov (Industry RS and the relative 200D MA); then the Mon 30-Nov month-end.
  • Adds: the rules block an add, because two Sell Criteria give a sell signal. Where Avanza stands for an add: none of the three (pulling back or basing; may be retesting a rising MA or forming a volatility contraction pattern; healthily retesting a rising MA or breaking out of one): the pullback has become a broken trend at the short and medium MAs (item 1 (SP and MAs)). The Master Dashboard reads Pulling Back 2 of 4, Basing 2 of 4, Healthy Retest 1 of 6 and both volatility contraction patterns 0 of 4. No add trigger is present.
  • Dates: the Riksbank, Wed 4-Nov (a hold is the negative); October statistics, early November; fourth-quarter results Fri 22-Jan-2027 (confirmed), where the memo watches income per customer (above SEK 2,300 the bull case, below SEK 2,050 the bear case).
  • What would change this reading: a close above the absolute 200D MA (361.5) on heavy volume while Avanza outperforms Nordnet.

Richard decides.

12. Adding capital to Avanza: the rules and your add triggers (readings of 2026-10-30)

  • The rules: no sell signal from the six Sell Criteria {red}
    • Where it stands: The rules block an add: two tests gave a sell signal at the 30-Oct month-end, Sector RS (17) below 50 with Stock RS (33) below 70, and SS Sales and SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero with Stock RS below 50.
    • What would change it: At the 30-Nov month-end, Sector RS back to 50 or Stock RS to 70, and also Stock RS to 50 or both breadths back to zero.
  • The rules: room below the holding's limit {green}
    • Where it stands: The rules allow an add on size: Avanza is 3.07% of capital against the 6.68% limit, leaving 3.61% of room.
    • Why: The limit governs purchases only and never forces a sale; the sell signal above is what blocks the add.
  • Where Avanza stands for an add: pulling back or basing; may be retesting or forming a VCP; healthily retesting or breaking out {red}
    • Where it stands: None of the three: the Master Dashboard reads Pulling Back 2 of 4, Basing 2 of 4, Healthy Retest 1 of 6, volatility contraction patterns 0 of 4.

  • Your add trigger: a positive retest of a rising MA {red}
    • Where it stands: Absent: Avanza closed through its rising 150D (16-Oct) and absolute 200D (19-Oct) MAs and has stayed 0.1% to 3.1% below the latter for ten sessions.

Bally's Intralot

Middle Innings Core Investment · 2.0% of capital in 1 tranche (tranche 1, 2.0%, bought 26-Jun-2026) · readings as at Fri 30-Oct-2026

Closes, legs and MAs use the data file's unrounded closes on Athens trading days; the chart file rounds Bally's Intralot to €0.01 (about 0.9%), so its MA crossings and the script's swing ratios differ from the figures here (a data fault: the chart file's rounding).

1. SP and MAs

Bally's Intralot missed the UK Budget-week relief rally: its SP was flat while every UK-exposed peer rose. Bally's Intralot closed at €1.122 on Friday 30-Oct, (0.6%) over the five sessions from 22-Oct and (1.6%) over the 20 sessions from 1-Oct, while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.4% and 3.2% over the same sessions (the data file's beta readings). At its beta of 0.66 the market explained +0.9% of the five sessions, so (1.6%) was Bally's Intralot's own, 0.4 times its usual weekly move of 3.6%: the miss is in the direction, not the size.

  • The trend: Bally's Intralot's SP is up 1.3% over 12 months (from €1.107 on 30-Oct-2025) and 2.0% over 3 months (from €1.100 on 30-Jul), but its relative price is down 11.4% over 12 months and 2.1% over the month on the same dates. The SP has gone sideways since June while the market drifted up.
  • The legs since the 30-Mar low (€0.831): +44.0% to the 23-Jun closing high (€1.197; tranche 1 bought at €1.180 on 26-Jun); (11.1%) to €1.064 on 23-Jul; +10.5% to €1.176 on 14-Aug; (9.9%) to €1.059 on 18-Sep; +7.6% to €1.140 on 1-Oct; (5.3%) to €1.080 on 8-Oct; +4.6% to €1.130 on 21-Oct; (0.7%) to €1.122 on 30-Oct. Each high is lower than the last; the 18-Sep low (€1.059) undercut the 23-Jul low (€1.064) by 0.5%, and each low since 18-Sep is higher: a coil since September, after a marginal lower low.
  • Which legs were its own: the 8-Oct fall, (2.7%), came while the cohort rose 1.8%; the 15-Oct rise, +2.1% on 2.36 times the 50D average volume, was shared with Consumer discretionary (+1.6%).

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review, the research filed on 31-Oct and 2-Nov, and the data file. No company news on Bally's Intralot is dated in the week to 30-Oct in the sources read.

  • 28-Oct, the UK Budget: Athens was shut for Ohi Day, so Bally's Intralot's first reaction was the 29-Oct close: 0.0% on 3.40M shares (€3.81m, 1.31 times the 50D average), closing at the day's high. On 28-Oct Entain rose 3.6%, Evoke 3.7% and Lottomatica 3.4% (chart files). The Budget's gambling measures beyond Machine Games Duty at 25% from April 2027 are not in the sources read.
  • 23-Oct, Evoke's third-quarter update: Bally's Intralot's all-share purchase of Evoke should complete in late fourth quarter 2026 or early first quarter 2027 (research filed 31-Oct). Evoke traded 6.8 times its 50D average volume that day and 8.8 times on 27-Oct.

Two hypotheses for the miss, ranked.

  • (1) Merger arbitrage caps Bally's Intralot's SP. For it: Evoke rose 10.7% in the week, so the discount of Evoke's price to the value of the 0.537 Bally's Intralot shares it will receive narrowed from about 17% (27-Oct) to about 11% (30-Oct), on the exchange rate implied on 23-Oct; arbitrageurs who buy Evoke usually sell the acquirer short. Against it: no short-interest figure for Bally's Intralot is in the sources read.
  • (2) The market prices Bally's Intralot on its parent and its leverage, not on UK relief. For it: the parent's Stock RS halved in October (item 9 (Peers)). Against it: Bally's Intralot did not fall when the parent's SP fell 2.8% on 26-Oct.
  • What would settle it: Bally's Intralot rising with Evoke after completion supports the first; a fall on the parent's next filing supports the second.

The MAs: a flat cluster, and the 50D MA has just slipped below the 150D MA. On 30-Oct the 100D MA was €1.124, the 20D €1.112, the 150D €1.107, the 50D €1.106 and the absolute 200D MA €1.070; Bally's Intralot closed 1.4% above the 50D and 150D, 0.2% below the 100D and 4.9% above the absolute 200D MA. Over 21 sessions the 50D fell 0.7% and the 100D 0.6%; the 150D rose 2.8% and the absolute 200D MA 0.8%. The SP has crossed the 20D and 50D MAs more than ten times since 31-Aug.

  • On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "150D MA now above the 200D MA so the MAs are stacked into proper Stage 2... Positive". Half right: the 150D MA has been above the absolute 200D MA since 11-Aug. But the 50D MA fell below the 150D MA on 30-Oct, and the 50D and 100D are falling: the stack is not the Stage 2 order (50D above 150D above the absolute 200D MA, all rising).

Healthy retest or broken trend? Bally's Intralot against your trend-health tests.

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes, slowly (+0.8% over the month, +1.6% over 3 months).
  • The 150D above the absolute 200D MA: passes (€1.107 against €1.070).
  • The 50D above the 150D and the absolute 200D MA: partly: above the absolute 200D MA, below the 150D since 30-Oct.
  • The SP above each: partly: above the 20D, 50D, 150D and absolute 200D MAs; 0.2% below the 100D.
  • Near the 52W high, away from the low: partly: 6.3% below the 52W closing high, 35.0% above the 52W low.
  • The largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began: no flag: the largest week was (7.2%) to 16-Jul; October's worst day, (2.7%), was smaller than April's (3.2%).
  • Wider swings: no flag: the average daily move was 0.71% over 20 sessions against 0.93% in the 60 before.
  • Lower lows: partly: the 18-Sep low (€1.059) undercut the 23-Jul low (€1.064) by 0.5%, a lower low; since then lows rose to €1.080 (8-Oct) and €1.117; the highs fall.
  • More down days than up days: flagged over six months: 66 down days against 54 up days since 6-May (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: no breakout to reverse.
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly: it tracks flat MAs.
  • Verdict: neither a healthy Stage 2 retest nor a broken trend: a sideways base, tightening. A close above €1.140 (the 1-Oct high) on 1.5 times the 50D average volume would start an advance; a close below €1.080 (the 8-Oct low) on heavy volume would break the base towards the absolute 200D MA.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

The 50D average holds two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug; 18-Sep, an index day at 6.75 times), which lower each ratio by about 10%; the script's figure without them is in brackets.

Bally's Intralot's volume is drying up as its range narrows: a volatility contraction without a breakout. The PMS Per-stock Dashboard's 20D average volume is 0.73 times the 50D average. The last ten closes sit between €1.117 and €1.130, a 1.2% range, against 4.6% over 20 sessions and 11.0% over 63. Two of the ten sessions to 30-Oct, 22-Oct and 27-Oct, traded under a quarter of the 50D average.

  • The heavy-volume days of 25 sessions (1.5 times the 50D average or more): 29-Sep, +2.7% on 5.23M shares (€5.91m, 1.94 times; 2.06), opening 0.9% lower and closing at the high, while the cohort fell; 15-Oct, +2.1% on 6.61M shares (€7.34m, 2.36 times; 2.54), closing at 0.75 of the range, with Consumer discretionary up 1.6% and Betsson up 1.1% on 1.8 times. Both were up days.
  • The Budget session, 29-Oct: 0.0% on 1.31 times (1.43), closing at the high: buyers absorbed sellers, but the price did not follow the peers.
  • Distribution and accumulation: the PMS Per-stock Dashboard counts one distribution day in 25 sessions (13-Oct, (1.0%) on 4.09M shares, €4.46m, 1.44 times; 1.63, closing at 0.33 of the range), down from four at 1-Oct, three at 15-Oct and two at 22-Oct. The two accumulation days are 29-Sep and 15-Oct. Selling pressure has faded through October.
  • Up-day against down-day volume: the PMS Per-stock Dashboard reads 1.13 over 20 sessions and 4.70 over 5; the move from the 18-Sep low runs at 1.74 (calculated from the data file), with three heavy up days and no heavy down day.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Generally, over L6M, down days have much higher volume than up days - a fragility indicator IMHO". The count confirms the fragility; the volume per day only partly does.

  • Since 6-May (126 sessions) Bally's Intralot had 66 down days against 54 up days, and down days carried 1.33 times the up days' total volume (an up/down ratio of 0.75).
  • Per day the difference is small: down days averaged 3.06M shares against 2.80M on up days, 9% heavier, and only 1% heavier once the two index days are left out.
  • The fragility sits in the two summer declines: 23-Jun to 23-Jul ran at an up/down ratio of 0.17 with two heavy down days, and 14-Aug to 18-Sep at 0.22 (0.27 without the two mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep) with three, one of them the 18-Sep index day. The rebounds between them were light: the July rebound ran at 3.74 with no heavy day and failed below the June high.
  • What it implies: holders sold hard twice this summer and buyers have come only in bursts. The rebound from 18-Sep is the first since the June high with heavy up days (24-Sep, 29-Sep, 15-Oct), but it has not yet cleared the 1-Oct high.

Precedent: the November 2025 decline ended in capitulation, a 17.3% Budget-day fall on 5.26 times the 50D average (26-Nov-2025); the March and July 2026 lows ended quietly, on below-average volume.

Relative price sits on its relative 200D MA, and the relative 200D MA is still falling. Bally's Intralot's relative price (0.6211) was 0.7% above its relative 200D MA (0.6168) on 30-Oct, after dipping 0.3% below it on 29-Oct; it first crossed above on 24-Sep and has crossed back and forth since (below on 8-9 Oct and 13-14 Oct, above again on 12 and 15-Oct). Relative price is 12.4% below its 52W high of 24-Nov-2025. It did not confirm the SP's 23-Jun high: relative price was then 16.7% below its own 52W high. The relative 200D MA fell 0.33% over the month.

  • On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "The relative 200D MA is declining, with the SP flat". Confirmed: the relative 200D MA has fallen over the month at every session since 14-Oct-2025. It turns up in mid to late November only because weak January and February 2026 readings leave its window (the closing paragraph).

Peers' volume: Evoke traded 6.8, 8.8 and 3.3 times its 50D average on 23, 27 and 28-Oct, while Bally's Intralot traded 0.81 and 0.19 times on 23 and 27-Oct (Athens was shut on 28-Oct). The heavy trading in the combined Bally's Intralot and Evoke story is happening in Evoke's shares.

Confirms a turn up: a close above €1.140 on 1.5 times the 50D average or more. Confirms renewed selling: two distribution days within ten sessions, or a close below €1.080 on heavy volume.

3. RS

Bally's Intralot's Stock RS has held near 50 on a weak comparison base, while its sector's RS collapsed in October and its industry's stayed below 30.

  • Stock RS was 52 at the 30-Oct month-end: 53 at 30-Sep, 36 at 31-Jul, 45 at 30-Apr and 40 at 31-Oct-2025. It is flat over the month, up 16 points over 3 months and up 12 over 12 months, below the 70 line at every reading since 30-Jun and 2 points above the 50 line.
  • Sector RS for Gambling fell from 58 at 30-Sep to 16 at 30-Oct (14 on 16-Oct), so it crossed below the 50 line in the first half of October.
  • Industry RS for Consumer discretionary was 26 on 30-Oct, below 30 at all eight Friday readings since 11-Sep (low 13 on 18 and 25-Sep).
  • The order of the turn: Stock RS and Industry RS bottomed on 18-Sep (27 and 13), Sector RS two weeks earlier (7 on 4-Sep, 11 on 18-Sep); Stock RS then rose 22 points in a week while Industry RS stayed at 13, and in October the sector fell away while the stock held. It suggests Stock RS is holding because the weak autumn of 2025 is in its comparison window, not because of current strength: Bally's Intralot's relative price fell 2.1% in October.
  • The peers' RS moved the other way in October: Evoke rose from 72 to 90, Lottomatica from 53 to 68 and Betsson from 16 to 28, while the parent, Bally's Corporation, fell from 56 to 23.
  • On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "I've owned it while stock, sector and industry RS were weak". Confirmed: since tranche 1 was bought on 26-Jun (Stock RS 72 that day), Stock RS has not read above 69 (1-Jul), Sector RS above 58 or Industry RS above 46.
  • Precedent: the last time Stock RS stood at 52 (14-Aug) it fell to 27 by 18-Sep, on the one-sided decline in item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high).

4. Consensus by named FY

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "MAJOR NEGATIVE - Just had a guidance downgrade; UK-gambling-related". The sources read show no formal cut to Bally's Intralot's group guidance; they show a softer Interactive outlook on 1-Sep and a consensus EPS collapse on 30-Sep that comes from one broker.

  • The guidance: FY2026 adjusted EBITDA of about €422m was reaffirmed at the first-quarter results (27-May). On the first-half call (1-Sep) the chief executive gave FY2026 Interactive adjusted EBITDA of about €280m if UK market consolidation does not come within six months, against about €295m under the original scenario (earnings review). That is a 5% lower case for the UK-centred division, UK-related as you say, two months old. Bally's Intralot fell 1.8% on 2-Sep and closed below its 50D and 100D MAs.
  • The consensus: FactSet's FY2026 EPS fell from €0.072 at 31-Jul to €0.020 on 30-Sep, (72%); FY2027 from €0.086 to €0.030, (65%); FY2028 from €0.097 to €0.040, (59%). One analyst stands behind each figure, and the new figures match Barclays' of 22-Sep exactly (research filed 2-Nov). The research judges the fall "driven by thin analyst coverage" and a stricter EPS definition, with Deutsche Bank modelling about €120m of annual finance cost on €1.7bn of gross debt. Net Upgrade Breadth recorded no EPS cut (0%, one analyst, 30-Sep): a change of contributor, not a revision.
  • EBITDA and revenue, the firmer measures: FY2026 EBITDA consensus was €408.5m on 30-Oct, (2.7%) over 3 months and 3% below guidance; Deutsche Bank's €390m (13-Jan initiation) is the low end. FY2027 and FY2028 revenue (€1,206m and €1,236m) fell 3.1% and 4.2% over the 3 months to 31-Aug; FY2027 and FY2028 EBITDA (€452m and €473m) held.
  • The trajectory: implied revenue growth is 6.9% into FY2027 and 2.5% into FY2028 while implied EBITDA margins rise from 36.2% to 37.5% and 38.3%: cost cuts and synergies, not growth, carry the estimates.
  • The link to the SP: the SP rose 0.4% on 30-Sep, the day of the EPS collapse, and rose 44% in the spring while FY2027 EPS fell: estimates have not led the SP this year.
  • Test: the third-quarter results on 27-Nov (projected), where the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo sets an alarm on a further EPS cut of 2% or more and on UK growth below 8%.

Bally's Intralot's sell-side readings rest on one or two analysts, and none is cutting: the PT is anchored 25% above the SP.

  • Ratings: two Buys, no Hold and no Sell at the 30-Oct month-end, up from one Buy at the August month-end. Every change in the past 24 months came with a change in coverage; no rating followed the 23.0% fall from the 7-Jan high to the 30-Mar low or the 44.0% rise to June.

The average PT is 25.2% above the SP (€1.122), against a gap of 16% at the May month-end (€1.35 against €1.167), the narrowest month-end gap since June 2024, and 46% at the March month-end. The SP topped on 23-Jun (€1.197) 13% to 15% below the average PT (€1.35 at May, €1.38 at June).

The only non-zero readings of the year came in the first quarter, as the SP fell to its low: revenue and EBITDA +33% at each of the January, February and March month-ends (three analysts), and EPS (33%) with PT +33% at the March month-end.

  • The link to the sell test on SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth: with two analysts, one PT cut makes PT breadth (50%); if Stock RS is then below 50 at the 30-Nov month-end, the test gives a sell signal. The third-quarter results on 27-Nov are the likeliest day for such a cut.

9. Peers

In October the money returning to UK-exposed gambling went to the liquid UK names and to Evoke, not to Bally's Intralot: the reverse of the summer, when Bally's Intralot held up as they fell. Over the month Entain rose 13.4%, Flutter 8.0%, Evoke 8.0% and Lottomatica 3.6%, while Bally's Intralot fell 0.9% (the peer table's rounded closes; (1.6%) over the 20 sessions from 1-Oct in the data file); over 3 months Flutter fell 22.1% and Entain 14.9% against Bally's Intralot's +1.8% (+2.2% from the data file's €1.098 on 3-Aug).

Evoke is not an independent peer, and its price now carries the information. Bally's Intralot pays 0.537 new shares per Evoke share, so Evoke's SP equals 0.537 times Bally's Intralot's SP, less a deal discount. Evoke's 10.7% week narrowed that discount from about 17% to about 11% (item 1 (SP and MAs)). Hypothesis: the market now judges completion more likely; the research filed 31-Oct reports 99.6% shareholder approval and "the majority of regulatory approvals" received, with foreign-investment reviews in six countries outstanding. The implication for Bally's Intralot: a buyer of the combined company can buy it about 11% cheaper through Evoke until completion, which leaves little reason to buy Bally's Intralot's shares first. Test: the discount at the court sanction filings expected in December 2026 (research filed 2-Nov).

The parent, Bally's Corporation, is the cohort member that matters most for the risk to Bally's Intralot. Bally's Corporation's SP fell 3.1% over the month and its Stock RS from 56 to 23; its cash fell from $906.7m at end-2025 to $487.8m by June 2026, and it does not project meeting its covenants after the waiver ends in early 2027 (10-Q and 10-K/A, research filed 2-Nov).

  • On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Risks are huge - Bally's Co and Evoke merger" and "KEY - Read the Bally's memo re. risks there". No Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo exists for Bally's Corporation in the files; the research filed on 2-Nov on BYLOT-GR ("Bally's Corporation Liquidity and Going-Concern Assessment") is the nearest, and its findings are above.

Your four peer checks, on the UK-exposed peers:

  • Sell-side estimates: cut over 3 months at Evoke (EPS (35%)), Flutter ((20%)) and Entain ((10%)), with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of (50%), (52%) and (61%); rising only at Betsson (EPS +3%, revenue breadth +100%), whose UK exposure is small.
  • SP technicals: Flutter and Entain are 22.6% and 14.4% below their absolute 200D MAs; Lottomatica (5.7% above), Betsson (3.0%) and Evoke (20.9%) are above theirs, as is Bally's Intralot (4.7% on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's basis).
  • Newsflow, guidance and communications: Entain's third-quarter UK and Ireland online revenue rose 14% and it reiterated FY2026 guidance; Rank's digital revenue rose 6% in its quarter to 30-Sep; Lottomatica confirmed its FY2026 guidance on 3-Aug (research filed 2-Nov). UK online volumes are holding under the 40% duty, which supports Bally's Intralot's UK growth test (11.6% in the second quarter).

What the peers imply: the UK operating evidence is better than the summer's share prices assumed, and the relief is being priced, but not in Bally's Intralot. No outside stock explains its moves (correlation 0.22 at most, with CrediaBank).

What the rules say for Bally's Intralot now, and what decides the next step

  • The relief passed Bally's Intralot by: Entain, Evoke and Lottomatica rose in the UK Budget week while Bally's Intralot was flat, and the Evoke deal discount narrowing from about 17% to 11% suggests buyers of the combined company are going through Evoke (item 1 (SP and MAs) and item 9 (Peers)).
  • A base, not Stage 2: the SP is coiling in a 1.2% ten-session range on drying volume, with lower highs and, since 18-Sep, higher lows, and the 50D MA has slipped below the 150D MA (item 1 (SP and MAs) and item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).
  • The "guidance downgrade" is milder than it reads: it is the 1-Sep Interactive scenario (€280m against €295m), and the 72% fall in FY2026 EPS consensus is one broker's figures entering the feed (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).
  • Tranche 1 (2.0%, bought 26-Jun-2026): a sale, released when a replacement is bought, under your one-off in/out link. Three Sell Criteria give a sell signal at the 30-Oct readings: Industry RS 26, below 30; the relative 200D MA down 0.33% over the month; and, new at this month-end, Sector RS 16, below 50, with Stock RS 52, below 70. Nothing sells at the Tuesday 3-Nov session unless a replacement is bought.
  • How long each sell signal can last:
    • Sector RS with Stock RS is read only at the month-end, so it stands until 30-Nov whatever happens; clearing it then needs Sector RS up 34 points to 50, or Stock RS up 18 points to 70.
    • Industry RS is read each Friday: Consumer discretionary needs to rise about one place among the 16 industries (to 33) to clear it; it has been below 30 at eight Friday readings in a row.
    • The relative 200D MA cannot turn up by Friday 6-Nov: at an unchanged relative price it would still fall about (0.22%) over the month, and it would still be falling after a 5% rise in relative price. At an unchanged relative price it is about flat at 13-Nov and rising by 20-Nov, because weak January and February readings (relative price 0.55 to 0.61) leave the window: the calendar, not the stock, clears it.
  • The tests not giving a sell signal, nearest first: the sell test on SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth needs Stock RS 3 points lower (49, below 50) and one PT cut at the 30-Nov month-end; relative price, 12.4% below its 52W high, needs a fall of about 16.7% in the SP (to about €0.935) by 30-Nov at a flat market: the 24-Nov-2025 relative high leaves the window, the 52W high becomes the 23-Jun-2026 reading (0.690), and the 25%-below line then sits further from today's relative price; tranche 1's 30% fall needs €0.8261, 26.4% below the close.
  • How the tests interact: a weak third-quarter result on 27-Nov, with a PT cut and a fall of a few percent, would add the Net Upgrade Breadth test at 30-Nov; a repeat of the 2025 Budget-day fall (17.3%) would add the relative-price test too.
  • The record: the last time Stock RS stood at 52 (14-Aug) it fell to 27 within five weeks; but Industry RS was below 30 from 30-Jan to early June 2026 while Bally's Intralot's SP rose 15.0%.
  • Next reading day: Friday 6-Nov reads Industry RS and the relative 200D MA; the 30-Nov month-end reads the other three. The third-quarter results are on 27-Nov (projected); Evoke's completion is due late fourth quarter 2026 or early first quarter 2027.
  • Adds: the rules block an add while any Sell Criterion gives a sell signal. Where Bally's Intralot stands for an add: the first stage, pulling back or basing (it is basing). The Master Dashboard's own tests point to the third stage (a healthy retest, six of six tests, at the 50D MA, 1.44% away), but the 50D MA retested is falling (0.7% over 21 sessions) and fell below the 150D MA on 30-Oct, so it is not a rising MA. Its volatility contraction tests read none of four. Neither add trigger is present.
  • For your ruling: on 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "It is only 2.0% of capital so largely irrelevant... But what's the point...?" and "FINISH!". Under the one-off in/out link the rules sell Bally's Intralot only when a replacement purchase releases it; releasing it earlier lies outside the rules.
  • What would change this reading: a close above €1.140 on 1.5 times the 50D average volume (a breakout from the base); a close below €1.080 on heavy volume (a broken base); a parent filing that calls on Bally's Intralot's cash. Richard decides.

11. Bally's Intralot against each Sell Criterion (readings of 2026-10-30)

  • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70, read at the month-end {C3}
    • Where it stands: Sell signal: at the 30-Oct month-end Gambling's Sector RS was 16 and Bally's Intralot's Stock RS 52.
    • Trend: Sector RS fell from 58 at 30-Sep; Stock RS was 53 at 30-Sep, flat for a month.

What the peers imply for CVS Group: for the direction, the vet groups' strength is a tailwind CVS Group has not yet used, and its 25-Nov update decides whether it catches up. For the sell tests, the Sector RS of Hospitals, clinics and care homes rests on Medicover, Spire Healthcare and the Nordic care operators; a reversal there would lower Sector RS without any CVS Group news.

What the rules say for CVS Group now, and what decides the next step

  • The finding that matters most: CVS Group is testing the top of its 12-month range (1,320p to 1,344p) for the third time since 7-Sep, with sellers on 21 and 26-Oct and rising lows beneath, while the listed vet groups it trades with (Pets at Home, Vimian) are at 52W highs.
  • The month-end cleared a sell test: the 30-Oct readings (Sector RS 66, Stock RS 58) ended CVS Group's sell signal from Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70, so CVS Group now carries one sell signal instead of two.
  • What the rules say: CVS Group's whole holding (both tranches, 2.5% of capital) has a sell signal from the 200D MA of relative price falling over the month; under Richard's one-off in/out link CVS Group is sold when its replacement is bought, in the Assistant Portfolio Manager's order of sale. No tranche has a 30% fall; the rules sell nothing at the Tue 3-Nov session unless a replacement is bought.
  • The 200D MA of relative price: it fell 1.54% over the month to 30-Oct, from (1.62%) a week earlier. On my calculation, if CVS Group's SP moves on Mon 2-Nov to a new level and stays there with the market flat, the 200D MA of relative price turns up by the Fri 6-Nov reading only if that level is about 2,007p (+51%), by 13-Nov if it is 1,632p (+23%) and by 27-Nov if it is 1,487p (+12%). If the SP and the market stay flat, it turns up about 19-Jan-2027 (first read on Fri 22-Jan-2027), once February 2026's high relative prices (about 800 to 840) leave the 200-session window.
  • The nearest of the other tests: a CVS Group Stock RS below 50 (now 58) would complete the test "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50", since PT breadth is already (18%); a Sector RS below 50 (now 66) would restore the sector test. In the week to 25-Sep Stock RS fell 17 points and Sector RS 40, so the 25-Nov update could move both before the 30-Nov month-end, as the 23-Jul update did.
  • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high: CVS Group is 12.8% below; at a flat market the 25% line is reached at an SP of 1,140.7p, 14.0% below the 30-Oct close and level with the 2-Oct low close (1,140p). A return to the 2-Oct low would give a sell signal at the 30-Nov reading.

1. SP and MAs

Helios Towers has fallen 9.6% in 13 sessions from its 13-Oct high and is now sitting on its rising absolute 200D MA, with Thursday's third-quarter trading update (5-Nov) the event that decides which way it leaves it.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file, and from the research filed for Helios Towers on 30-Oct to 2-Nov (the Key Question research files, which cite broker notes of 29 and 30-Oct and Airtel Africa's results of 30-Oct).

Helios Towers has now given back all of its September-to-October gain above its MAs: the close is below the 20D, 50D, 100D and 150D MAs and 0.7% above the absolute 200D MA.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Helios Towers against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes. It rose 2.69% over 21 sessions and 0.51% over the last five.
  • The 150D above the absolute 200D MA: passes. The 150D (208.97p) is 3.1% above the absolute 200D MA (202.59p).
  • The 50D above the 150D and the absolute 200D MA: partly. The 50D is 3.0% above the absolute 200D MA but 0.2% below the 150D since 28-Oct.
  • The SP above each: partly. Helios Towers' 204.0p close is above only the absolute 200D MA (+0.7%); it is 2.2% below the 50D, 1.0% below the 100D, 2.4% below the 150D and 4.7% below the 20D.
  • Near its 52W high: fails. The SP is 16.9% below its 245.39p closing high, and relative price 21.3% below its own 52W high.
  • Away from its low: passes. The SP is 34.9% above its 52W closing low.
  • Stage 3 red flag, the largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began (20-Jul): partly present. The five sessions to 20-Oct, (6.0%), were Helios Towers' largest five-session fall since 20-Jul; 22-Oct's (3.8%) was the second-largest one-day fall, after 31-Jul's (4.4%). Neither followed good news.
  • Wider swings: absent. Helios Towers' average daily range was 2.59% over the last 10 sessions, against 3.02% in the 10 before and 2.88% over 60 sessions: the swings are narrowing.
  • Lower lows: absent so far. The 204.0p close is above the 195.3p low of 15-Sep; only the highs are lower.
  • More down days than up days: present. Six down days against three up days in the last 10 sessions; eight against four since 13-Oct.

  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: emerging. Helios Towers' lows on 27 to 30-Oct came 1.6%, 1.0%, 0.1% and (0.3%) from the absolute 200D MA without a bounce; the SP is sliding along the MA, not yet off it.
  • Verdict: not a broken trend, not yet a healthy retest. The absolute 200D MA is rising, the lows are higher and the selling is light; but three topping flags are present and there has been no bounce. Settled by: an up day on 1.5x the 50D average or more closing in the top third of its range (how two of the three completed declines in the last 12 months ended) confirms a healthy retest; a close below the absolute 200D MA (about 203p by 5-Nov), the first since April 2025, followed by a close below 195.3p (a lower low), would make it a broken trend.

This decline is lighter than Helios Towers' two shorter earlier declines, but not than the longest. Over 13 to 30-Oct up/down volume (average up-day volume against average down-day volume) is 0.83, against 0.52 in the 26-Feb to 23-Mar decline ((14.6%)) and 0.63 in the 6 to 20-Nov-2025 decline ((11.6%)), but 0.97 in the 14-May to 20-Jul decline ((23.1%)), which went on for 46 sessions: light selling did not stop that one. Analysts cut Helios Towers' EPS for all three years after the 30-Jul results while holding revenue and EBITDA: the operating growth is not reaching the brokers' bottom line, which is the memo's drop-through test.

9. Peers

Helios Towers is not trading with the European towers that make up the rest of its sector: from 13 to 30-Oct Cellnex rose 6.2% and Inwit 3.8% while Helios Towers fell 9.6%.

  • Hypothesis: the European towers' October rally is a rates trade, the bond-proxy leg of a defensive rotation. For: their 3-month estimate revisions are flat to negative (Cellnex revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (17%)). Against: the cause of their 9-Oct jump is not in the sources read. It matters because the four-stock sector's RS, which the test "Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70" reads, rises with the European towers whatever Helios Towers does.

Airtel Africa, the customer link your 28-Oct note makes the condition for an add, reported on 30-Oct: operating spending is strong, its guidance implies a second-half step-down, and its shares broke down.

  • On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Add only if Airtel is doing okay fundamentally - check it via KQ RR". The Key Question research filed on 30-Oct to 2-Nov answers it as follows.
  • Fundamentally, the first half is strong: Airtel Africa's capital expenditure "more than doubled" to $657m in the half to 30-Sep, and it reaffirmed full-year guidance of about $1.1bn, "a deliberate strategy to front-load deployments" (Airtel Africa half-year report, 30-Oct). Sharecast (30-Oct) reported "Airtel Africa profits jump as data, mobile money demand drives growth".
  • The second half steps down, close to the memo's lines. By subtraction, Airtel Africa spent about $268m in the July-to-September quarter ($657m less the $389m June-quarter cash capex the memo cites), above the memo's Alarm line of $250m a quarter. The $1.1bn guidance leaves about $443m for the second half, about $220m a quarter: below the Alarm line and above the Invalidation line of $200m. The halves may be on slightly different bases (capital expenditure against cash capex).
  • The market sold Airtel Africa on the results: it fell 5.5% on 30-Oct on 1.5x its 50D average, closing near its low, and is 9.8% down since 13-Oct, 17.1% below its 50D MA and 23.5% below its absolute 200D MA. The cause of the fall is not in the sources read. Helios Towers fell 0.6% that day on 0.57x.
  • The link between the two shares is weak and inconsistent. Airtel Africa fell 17.8% from 15-Sep to 13-Oct while Helios Towers rose 15.5%, then the two fell together (9.8% and 9.6%) to 30-Oct. Airtel Africa is not among the four stocks outside the cohort whose stock-specific moves track Helios Towers' most closely in the 14:31 rebuild of the data file (Vodafone 0.24, Tritax Big Box REIT 0.22, Ashmore 0.22, Oxford Instruments 0.21, all weak); the 06:13 build had Airtel Africa fourth of those four, at 0.21, the lowest.
  • Reading for your condition: Airtel Africa is "doing okay fundamentally" on spending and profit this half; its guidance concedes the front-loading the memo's counter-case describes, and its share price is in a broken trend.

Peers' newsflow: Indus Towers' entry into Africa is a counter-case for Helios Towers, with Airtel as its anchor customer. Emkay Research (29-Oct) reported that Indus Towers has started building towers in Zambia and Uganda with Bharti Airtel as its anchor customer, and Ambit Capital (28-Oct) that its African rollout is designed to earn a return from a single tenancy, a cheaper alternative to the incumbents (Key Question research files). Airtel Africa is the customer whose spending your 28-Oct note makes the condition for an add, so a cheaper tower supplier backed by its parent bears on how much of Airtel Africa's capex becomes Helios Towers tenancies. IIFL (29-Oct) reported a strong Indus Towers order book; the European towers' estimates (3-month revisions flat to negative) did not change Helios Towers' picture. Test: any comment on competition or Airtel Africa's tenancy share in Thursday's update (5-Nov).

What the peers imply: Helios Towers' next move depends on its own 5-Nov update, not on the European towers; but its sector RS, and so one of its Sell Criteria, depends on them.

What the rules say for Helios Towers now, and what decides the next step

  • The finding that matters most: Thursday's update (5-Nov) can fire a fundamental exit. The memo's Invalidation test is organic tenancy additions below 600 in the third or fourth quarter of 2026. Morgan Stanley's preview (30-Oct) implies about 526 and Deutsche Bank's (29-Oct) about 800, and the company's own 2026 guidance (3,500 to 4,000) implies 495 to 745 a quarter for the second half: the Invalidation line sits inside the guided range.
  • The second finding: Helios Towers is retesting its rising absolute 200D MA on light volume, without a bounce, with three topping flags present (items 1 and 2).
  • The third finding: brokers' EPS is not following EBITDA, and Airtel Africa's guidance implies a second-half capex step-down close to the memo's Alarm line (items 4 and 9).

1. SP and MAs

IMCD's SP has held its rising 20D MA through a 4.3% pullback from its 14-Oct high and closed the week at €102.25, 2.3% below that high, at the day's high; the pullback is a retest so far, decided at the 5-Nov results.

  • Over 12 months IMCD is up 19.5% (from €85.55 on 4-Nov-2025); over 3 months +4.9%; over the month +6.5%, against (1.3%) for its industry, Industrials and capital goods, and +3.2% for the Stoxx Europe 600
  • This week IMCD rose 1.0%, behind its industry (+2.0%) and the Stoxx Europe 600 (+1.7%), a fifth of its usual weekly move (5.2%)
  • IMCD's closes stopped at €100 to €101 on 4-May, 6-Aug, 31-Aug and 9-Oct before the 13-Oct breakout; the pullback has come back to that ceiling and held it

The legs of the latest move:

The MAs: IMCD's MAs are stacked in rising order (20D above 50D above 100D above 150D above 200D), and IMCD has not closed below its 20D or 50D MA since 5-Oct.

  • 20D €100.92 (+1.3% over 5 sessions), 50D €98.29, 100D €93.88, 150D €93.41 and absolute 200D €89.75 (+2.9% over 20 sessions, rising since 29-Jul)

  • The 20D MA, tranche 1's MA, is closing on IMCD's SP from below: the four closes leaving its window by Thu 5-Nov (5 to 8-Oct, €95.72 to €98.68) are all below €102.25, so at a flat SP it reaches about €101.8 on 5-Nov, 0.4% below Friday's close

Healthy retest or broken trend? IMCD against your trend-health tests. In your note of 28 to 31-Oct-2026 you asked: "Is the industrial/capital goods/tech pullback a healthy retest or a broken trend?" For IMCD, a healthy retest so far:

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes; +2.9% over 20 sessions and rising for four months
  • The 150D above the 200D: passes; the 150D MA is €93.41 against the absolute 200D MA's €89.75

  • The SP above each: passes; 1.3% above the 20D, 13.9% above the absolute 200D
  • Near its 52W high: passes; 2.3% below the €104.7 closing high of 14-Oct
  • Away from its 52W low: passes; 48.5% above €68.87 (19-Mar)

Your Stage 3 (topping) red flags:

  • The largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began: absent; October's worst day ((2.3%), 20-Oct) and five days ((3.9%)) are smaller than July's (4.4%) and September's (4.2%)

  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly; IMCD has closed 0.3% to 1.6% above the 20D for seven sessions without a bounce (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high))

Verdict: a healthy retest on all six trend-health tests, with four Stage 3 red flags in part (lower lows, more down days than up days, the light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume, and the SP tracking its rising 20D MA) and two absent. What settles it: an IMCD close above €104.7 on 1.5x volume confirms it; a close at or below the 20D MA on 5-Nov followed by a second on ordinary volume would end it, and would confirm tranche 1's exit.

4. Consensus by named FY

IMCD's consensus has not moved for a month before its 5-Nov results; over three months the nearer year rose faster than the later ones, so analysts have pulled earnings forward into 2026 rather than lifted the trajectory.

  • EPS over 1, 3, 6 and 12 months: FY2026 (€5.631) 0.0%, +1.5%, +5.7%, (8.7%); FY2027 (€6.066) 0.0%, +0.7%, +5.1%, (10.7%); FY2028 (€6.624) 0.0%, +0.9%, +5.7%, (14.2%)
  • Over 3 months revenue rose 0.3% to 0.4% for each FY; EBITDA rose 1.1% for FY2026 against 0.5% and 0.6% for FY2027 and FY2028
  • Implied growth: EPS +7.7% from FY2026 to FY2027 and +9.2% from FY2027 to FY2028; revenue +4.4% and +5.2%; EBITDA +5.2% and +5.9%. The implied EBITDA margin edges from 11.4% (FY2026) to 11.5% (FY2027) and 11.6% (FY2028): analysts model almost no further margin recovery after 2026

The SP has run far ahead of the estimates. IMCD's SP rose 32.5% from its 30-Jun low to its 14-Oct high, while FY2027 EPS rose 0.7% from the July month-end. The 6-month rise in FY2027 EPS (+5.1%) came almost all before August. Hypothesis: IMCD's SP prices a return to high-single-digit organic growth, not the flat consensus. For it: management "has reiterated its high-single-digit organic growth objectives" (Jefferies, 12-Oct, cited in the research filed 30-Oct), and Azelis's organic revenue growth accelerated to 8.2% in its third quarter (Morgan Stanley, 30-Oct). Against it: Jefferies (12-Oct) tied recent revenue gains to "short-term supply chain anxieties in the Middle East", the memo's counter case. Test: whether analysts raise FY2027 and FY2028 after 5-Nov, or only FY2026.

The 5-Nov nine-month results are the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's decisive test (memo of 1-Oct). Nine-month organic gross profit growth below 0%, a group gross margin below 24.5%, or a second-half conversion margin below 42% moves the memo's view to neutral and park; nine-month organic gross profit growth below 0% with a gross margin below 24.5% is its Building Block 1 invalidation, a fundamental exit under the rules. IMCD's second quarter printed organic gross profit growth of 7.3%, a gross margin of 25.2% and a conversion margin of 44.9% (earnings review of 10-Aug). The risk to watch: IMCD's gross margin in the third quarter of 2025 was 24.3% (Investing.com, 6-Nov-2025), below the memo's 24.5% bar; the peer read-across (Azelis's third-quarter conversion margin 43.4%, organic gross profit +8.5%, 22-Oct) points the other way.

9. Peers

IMCD still trades as Brenntag's twin, and both have held their highs while Azelis broke; the market is paying the three chemical distributors for cash flow and balance sheet as much as for organic growth.

  • SP technicals: Brenntag, whose own daily moves track IMCD's most closely (0.76 over 63 sessions), rose 1.5% this week to 2.7% below its 16-Oct closing high, above its rising 20D and 50D MAs, as IMCD did (+1.0%, 2.3% below its 14-Oct high). The two moved in the same direction on four of five days this week; on 26-Oct IMCD fell 1.0% and Brenntag rose 0.6%. Azelis is 12.7% below its 14-Oct high, below its 20D (€12.39) and 50D (€12.20) MAs, and flat this week (+0.3%)
  • Volume: Brenntag's rise came on 0.60x to 0.80x its 50D average volume, lighter than IMCD's 0.84x to 1.13x
  • Sell-side estimates: the three differ here. Azelis's estimates are rising (3-month EPS revision +6%, revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +21%, Sell-side Momentum A); IMCD's are flat (3-month EPS revision +2%, revenue Net Upgrade Breadth 0%, Sell-side Momentum B); Brenntag's analysts are cutting revenue (revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (23%), Sell-side Momentum C)
  • Newsflow: Azelis's third quarter (Azelis, 22-Oct): organic gross profit +8.5%, conversion margin 43.4%, but free cash flow €67m, down 52.5% on working capital, leverage 3.46 times and a €350m goodwill impairment; Morgan Stanley (30-Oct) noted Azelis's organic revenue growth accelerating to 8.2%
  • Company guidance and communications: Azelis will make no acquisitions until leverage is below 3.0 times (its CFO, third-quarter call; Jefferies, 22-Oct). IMCD gives no guidance in figures; its first-half free cash flow rose 29%, with leverage at 2.8 times (earnings review)

Hypothesis (the Sector Digest of 28-Oct): the market pays for cash conversion and balance sheet in this group as much as for organic growth. For it: Azelis has the best estimate momentum of the three and the worst SP since 14-Oct, while IMCD and Brenntag, with flat or falling estimates, hold near their highs. Against it: Azelis had risen most (+44.1% from 30-Jun to 14-Oct, against IMCD's +32.5%), so part of its fall is a giveback. Test: IMCD's cash flow and working capital on 5-Nov, then Brenntag's interim statement on 11-Nov.

In the wider sector, estimates are not being paid for: over the month Rexel's Stock RS fell from 49 to 39 despite revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of +64%, while Bunzl rose 10.9%. IMCD's +6.5% puts it with the leaders.

What the peers imply for IMCD: IMCD and Azelis printed almost the same organic gross profit growth in the second quarter (+7.3% and +7.2%, per the memo), so Azelis's third-quarter +8.5% makes a negative IMCD print on 5-Nov less likely. Azelis's SP still fell 12.8% in its results week (16 to 23-Oct closes); the cause is not stated in the sources read, though a press headline (Idéal Investisseur, undated) paired its 8.2% organic growth with a sharp fall in free cash flow, and cash flow is the test IMCD's SP faces on 5-Nov.

What the rules say for IMCD now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most:

  • The 5-Nov results decide tranche 1 under the 10-day stop-loss rule (version 4, from 2-Nov-2026). IMCD's rising 20D MA, tranche 1's MA, will stand only about 0.4% below a flat SP on results day; IMCD's last four results days moved its SP by (7.4%), (4.7%), +3.0% and +5.3%
  • The pullback passes every trend-health test but is not confirmed: relative price has not confirmed the 14-Oct high and no buying day has come
  • The sell-side has gone quiet, not negative. Every Net Upgrade Breadth reading has fallen to about zero as the July raises aged out, with no new cut; in July the results restarted it

Tranche 1 today (2.0%, bought 19-Aug at €97.04): clear under the 10-day stop-loss rule, with no test running. Its 20D MA is €100.92 and its stop-loss, the MA less 6%, €94.86; IMCD's €102.25 close is 1.3% above the MA and 7.8% above the stop-loss. Your 2-Nov-2026 ruling cleared tranche 1's confirmed exit of 15-Sep-2026 under the 10-day stop-loss rule (three consecutive lower closes at or below the 20D MA) as a recorded keep at the 30-Oct close (the rule applies from 2-Nov; earlier confirmations whose price had closed back above the MA were cleared), so tranche 1 is judged afresh from Monday 2-Nov.

How a sale could come, and when:

About four in five of IMCD's last 25 sessions traded above 0.75x, so a results-day close below the MA followed by another on Fri 6-Nov would most likely confirm the exit; after its 18-Feb-2026 results ((4.7%)) IMCD fell a further 7.6% in five sessions
  • The sale: a confirmed exit sells tranche 1, the whole holding, at Tue 10-Nov-2026. A results-day close above about €101.8 keeps the test from starting

D'Ieteren

Early Innings Probing Bet · 2.50% of capital in 1 tranche (tranche 1, 2.50%, bought 12-Aug-2026) · readings as at Fri 30-Oct-2026

1. SP and MAs

D'Ieteren's SP is going sideways in a tightening range under lower highs: up 13.0% over 12 months, down 5.0% over 3 months, and 11.5% below its 10-Feb closing high of €195.44. D'Ieteren's swing highs have come lower at €184.3 (5-Aug), €180.4 (4-Sep) and €175.9 (16 and 21-Oct), while its lows held at €158.9 (8-Jun) and €160.0 (24-Sep). D'Ieteren's swings of 8% or more have shrunk from 37.0% (25-Nov-2025 to 10-Feb-2026) to 13.2% (5-Aug to 24-Sep) and 9.9% (24-Sep to 16-Oct).

The latest legs: D'Ieteren's October advance was partly its own; its pullback since 16-Oct is its own and small.

  • 24-Sep to 16-Oct, +9.9% to €175.9: Consumer discretionary rose 4.3% from 24-Sep to 21-Oct (Market chart, as read for the 28-Oct note), a little under half D'Ieteren's move.
  • 16 to 30-Oct, (1.7%) to €172.9: in the week to 30-Oct D'Ieteren fell 0.5% while Consumer discretionary and the Stoxx Europe 600 each rose 1.7%; after removing the part of its move explained by the market through its beta, D'Ieteren's own move was (2.6%), 0.7 times its usual weekly move of 3.6%.
  • The pullback's one large day was 27-Oct, (1.8%) to €171.8, closing at the low on 0.84x the 50D average (0.81x on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's basis) while its industry rose 0.1% (cause not in the sources read). On 30-Oct D'Ieteren rose 0.9%, closing in the top 14% of its range, while its industry was flat and its sector peers fell 0.3% on average.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file.

The Developments that move D'Ieteren are Belron's, and none came in the last two weeks.

  • 9-Sep, half-year results (earnings review): Belron's adjusted profit before tax up 28.6%, 5% above consensus; D'Ieteren Automotive's down 66.6%, 51% below; FY2026 guidance kept; a formal review of a Belron listing. D'Ieteren fell 3.4% the next day on 4.19x, its heaviest volume in 12 months.
  • 8-Oct: Bloomberg News reported Belron had picked four banks for a listing that could come next year (Bloomberg Línea, 8-Oct, as read for the 28-Oct note); D'Ieteren rose 2.2% on 2.61x while its industry fell 0.8%.
  • 21-Oct: D'Ieteren's own disclosure put its treasury shares at 1,443,038, 2.7% of capital, under the €100m buyback begun in December 2025 (company disclosure, cited in the AlphaSense research of 31-Oct on D'Ieteren's EPS drivers). Hypothesis: the buyback is a steady bid that helps light-volume pullbacks hold.
  • 31-Oct: four structural AlphaSense research reports were filed on D'Ieteren; they report no new event.

The MAs: D'Ieteren's five MAs sit within 1.8% of one another (€170.35 to €173.44), in an order close to the reverse of a Stage 2 (advancing) stack: the 50D lowest, then the 20D, 150D, 100D and absolute 200D.

  • D'Ieteren's 20D MA (€170.81) is the one rising clearly, up 1.3% over 5 sessions; on 28 and 29-Oct the SP traded below it intraday (lows €169.5 and €170.0) and closed above it both days, on 0.50x and 0.54x the 50D average.
  • D'Ieteren's 50D MA (€170.35), the MA chosen for tranche 1, is still falling: (2.0%) over 21 sessions and (0.1%) over 5. At a flat SP it keeps falling to about €169.6 by 13-Nov, as the August and early-September closes of €174 to €180 leave its window, and then turns up as the late-September closes of €160 to €166 leave.
  • D'Ieteren's absolute 200D MA (€173.44) rose 0.8% over 21 sessions but was flat over the last 5, and at a flat SP dips to about €172.6 by 13-Nov as January's and February's higher closes leave it. D'Ieteren's SP has crossed it 13 times in 130 sessions, tracking it rather than bouncing off it: the profile of a base, not of an advance.

On 22-Sep-2026 you wrote in your Notion note: "Very negative SP down day on Friday - major. Why is that". That Friday, 18-Sep, D'Ieteren fell 3.6% to €164.2, closing at the day's low on 3.08x the 50D average, on the option expiry and index review (a mechanical-volume day), while Consumer discretionary fell 1.6%, the Stoxx Europe 600 1.1% and its sector peers 1.6% on average; the cause of the 2.0 points beyond its industry is not in the sources read. In the same note you wrote: "150D MA was falling from a month ago" and "SP gotten very, very scatty/erratic". Both have turned. D'Ieteren's 150D MA has risen 0.3% over the last 21 sessions, though it sits 1.1% below the absolute 200D MA. D'Ieteren's average daily range (high less low, against the close) narrowed from 3.43% over 10 to 24-Sep to 1.52% over the last ten sessions, against 2.14% over 63: the erratic phase ended at the 24-Sep low.

Healthy retest or broken trend? D'Ieteren against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: partly; up 0.8% over 21 sessions but flat over the last 5, and set to dip at a flat SP.
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: fails; €171.52 against €173.44.
