The largest one-day fall since the 24-Sep low, (1.8%) on 27-Oct, and the largest five-session fall, (3.1%) from 21 to 28-Oct, came on below-average volume, not after good news; swings are narrowing; no lower low (the 28-Oct low, €169.5, is 5.9% above 24-Sep's); up and down days level (12 each in 25 sessions); no breakout reversed on heavy volume. Present: the SP tracking its absolute 200D MA instead of bouncing off it.
  • Verdict: neither a healthy retest of a rising stack nor a broken trend: D'Ieteren is a sideways base with contracting ranges, inside a relative trend that is still falling (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high), Volume, and relative price against its 52W high). A close above €175.9 and the absolute 200D MA on 1.5x the 50D average would settle it upward; a close below the €160.0 September low would break the base.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Ratios below leave out the mechanical-volume days (the 31-Aug index review and the 18-Sep option expiry and index review), which lift D'Ieteren's 50D average by about 4%; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's own ratios are in brackets.

D'Ieteren's buying came in one cluster, 6 to 15-Oct, and its pullback since 16-Oct has come on the lightest volume of the quarter: a pattern of demand followed by a lack of supply, not of distribution.

  • 6-Oct: +0.9% on 1.61x (1.55x), 78,528 shares (€12.7m), closing at 0.87 of the day's range: an accumulation day.
  • 8-Oct: +2.2% on 2.72x (2.61x), 138,618 shares (€22.9m), the Bloomberg News report of Belron's four banks; D'Ieteren traded below its 20D MA and closed above it.
  • 9-Oct: +2.5% on 1.59x (1.54x), 82,442 shares (€14.0m), with a 1.3% gap up, though closing at 0.35 of the range.
  • 15-Oct: +0.1% on 1.87x (1.81x), 102,590 shares (€17.7m), closing at the day's low while Consumer discretionary rose 1.6%: a churning day, the advance's clearest sign of supply, even though the volume evidence script counts it as an accumulation day because the close was up.
  • Since 16-Oct all ten sessions traded below the 50D average (0.51x to 0.98x), the last three at 0.51x to 0.65x (€5.1m to €6.5m a day).

Distribution days, on the volume evidence script's definition (a close below the previous close on more than 1.25x the 50D average, last 25 sessions): two, 7-Oct ((0.4%) on 1.31x (1.26x), 64,210 shares, €10.4m) and 14-Oct ((0.4%) on 1.34x (1.29x), 71,450 shares, €12.3m), both inside the advance and neither followed by a lower low. The PMS Per-stock Dashboard counts none, down from 5 on 2-Oct as September's selling left its window; the difference between its count of none and these two days is a question about the data, not about the market.

Up-day against down-day volume: D'Ieteren's 20-session ratio is 1.18 and its 5-session ratio 0.90 (dashboard). Over the move from the 24-Sep low the ratio is 1.16, with 4 heavy up days and no heavy down day: more demand than D'Ieteren's June to August advance (ratio 1.02, no heavy day), less than its March to April advance (1.61, 2 heavy up days); both gave back 13% to 15%.

Volume at the MAs: D'Ieteren regained its 50D, 100D, 150D and absolute 200D MAs together on 13-Oct on 1.15x (1.11x), lost the 100D and 200D on 27-Oct on 0.84x (0.81x), lost the 50D (tranche 1's MA) on 28-Oct on 0.51x (0.50x) and regained it on 29-Oct on 0.56x (0.54x). Inference: sellers did not press the break; light volume is what a retest looks like.

The 50D average itself is inflated by D'Ieteren's September selling days (10-Sep 4.19x, 11-Sep 2.52x, 14-Sep 2.35x, 24-Sep 1.67x), which leave its window between 19-Nov and early December; until then every ratio, including the 10-day stop-loss rule's 0.75x volume line, reads lower than the same shares will later.

Precedent: the 24-Sep low came on a 1.67x down day closing at the low, and the first heavy up day 8 sessions later (6-Oct). After the 25-Nov-2025 and 30-Mar-2026 lows a heavy up day came the next session and the advances ran +37.0% and +20.9%; after the 8-Jun low none came for ten sessions and the advance ran +16.0%. D'Ieteren's bigger advances started with immediate heavy buying; this one did not.

Relative price: D'Ieteren's relative price is 18.5% below its 52W high (117.41, 2-Feb) and 4.6% below its relative 200D MA, below which it has stayed since 6-Aug. Relative price confirmed the SP's lower highs: 101.1 (5-Aug), 98.8 (4-Sep), 98.1 (22-Oct). Its low, about 24% below its 52W high on 22 to 24-Sep, was the nearest approach since purchase to the 25% line. The relative 200D MA's fall over the month steepened from (0.05%) at the 23-Oct reading to (0.42%) at 30-Oct, as the high relative prices of late January and February leave its window.

Would confirm the advance resumes: a D'Ieteren close above €175.9 on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 90,000 shares, €15.6m). Would confirm a turn down: a close below the 50D MA (€170.35) in the bottom half of the day's range on 0.75x or more, the first such day since 9-Oct (close €169.2 below the 50D MA of €171.77, at 0.35 of the day's range, on 1.59x).

3. RS

All three of D'Ieteren's RS readings stand below their sell-test lines, and the October month-end reading shows the stock falling down the rankings while its SP rose.

  • D'Ieteren's Stock RS read 35 at the 30-Oct month-end, against the lines of 70 and 50: from 34 at the 30-Sep month-end, 47 on 9-Oct, 37 on 23-Oct and 31 on 29-Oct. It has been below 50 at every reading since 11-Sep, and its highest in 12 months was 68 (6-Mar).
  • D'Ieteren's Sector RS (Automotive retail, aftermarket, leasing and rental) read 38 at the 30-Oct month-end, against its line of 50: up from 29 on 29-Oct, its lowest in the 12 months stored, and down from 66 at the 31-Aug month-end.
  • D'Ieteren's Industry RS (Consumer discretionary) read 26 on Fri 30-Oct, against its line of 30, unchanged at every Friday since 9-Oct and below 30 at every Friday since 11-Sep (13 at its low, 18 and 25-Sep).

The order of the turn: in September all three RS readings turned down in the same week, the week of D'Ieteren's 9-Sep results; the industry fell fastest and bottomed first, and the sector and the stock bottomed together at the 30-Sep month-end. In October the stock and the sector moved together while the industry held. In the week to Fri 11-Sep, Consumer discretionary's Industry RS fell from 40 to 26, D'Ieteren's Sector RS from 66 to 58 and D'Ieteren's Stock RS from 56 to 49. Consumer discretionary's Industry RS reached its low of 13 on 18-Sep; D'Ieteren's Sector RS reached 33 and its Stock RS 34 at the 30-Sep month-end. Inference: D'Ieteren's September fall was not only a rotation carrying the stock: on 10-Sep D'Ieteren fell 3.4% on 4.19x the 50D average while its industry fell 0.7%. In October D'Ieteren's Stock RS and Sector RS rose together to 47 and 48 (9-Oct), the week of the Belron bank report, and fell together to 31 and 29 (29-Oct). From 9 to 30-Oct D'Ieteren's SP rose 2.2% while its Stock RS fell 12 points.

Precedent: D'Ieteren's lowest Stock RS readings have twice marked its lows, not breakdowns. Stock RS read 14 to 27 from 31-Oct-2025 to early January 2026, around the 25-Nov-2025 low before the +37.0% advance, and 27 to 31 from mid-May to 8-Jun-2026, before the +16.0% advance. Against the precedent: in both cases Sector RS stood at 49 or higher at the month-ends; today it is 38, with three sector peers at Stock RS 11 or below (item 9, Peers).

Would confirm a turn: D'Ieteren's Stock RS above 50 at a Friday reading with Sector RS back above 50. Would confirm the weakness: Stock RS below 27, the level of the two earlier lows, at the 6-Nov reading.

4. Consensus by named FY

D'Ieteren's EPS consensus was cut in September and again in October, fastest for the nearest year: the cuts are D'Ieteren Automotive's present, not Belron's future.

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26) EPS consensus: €12.75, cut 2.4% in October, 4.8% over 3 months, 6.3% over 6 months and 12.2% over 12 months.
  • FY2027 EPS consensus: €15.09, cut 1.7% in October, 3.5% over 3 months and 10.0% over 12 months.
  • FY2028 EPS consensus: €17.38, cut 1.9% in October, 3.8% over 3 months and 6.6% over 12 months.
  • FY2026 was cut 2.4% at each of the 30-Sep and 30-Oct month-ends, after no change in August; 4 of 14 analysts cut FY2027 EPS in the 100 days to 30-Oct, 1 raised it.

Revenue and EBITDA consensus did not move in October; their cuts came in September, after the 9-Sep results, and fell hardest on margin. D'Ieteren's FY2026, FY2027 and FY2028 revenue consensus (€7,981m, €8,275m, €8,513m) is unchanged over the month and down 1.5%, 2.0% and 2.4% over 3 months. EBITDA consensus is down 5.9% (FY2026, €626.8m), 4.2% (FY2027) and 3.3% (FY2028) over 3 months. The implied EBITDA margin fell from 8.2% to 7.9% for FY2026 and from 8.5% to 8.3% for FY2027 over the 3 months: a margin cut concentrated in the nearest year, which is where D'Ieteren Automotive's 66.6% fall in first-half profit before tax lands.

Analysts are pushing D'Ieteren's earnings out, not lowering their trajectory: the implied EPS growth from FY2026 to FY2027 rose from 16.7% (31-Jul) to 18.4% (30-Oct) because FY2026 was cut faster, while growth from FY2027 to FY2028 held at about 15%. Hypothesis: analysts keep Belron's deleveraging and margin path (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 1-Oct puts finance-cost relief at 40% of the growth) and take the Automotive restructuring cost and lower volumes now.

D'Ieteren's 9.9% October advance came on estimates that kept falling: Belron's listing news and the industry carried it. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo lists "sell-side cuts of 2 percent or more with underperformance" as an alarm level already present; October's 2.4% FY2026 cut keeps it present, though none of the memo's Invalidation tests is touched by these figures.

Test: the next month-end, 30-Nov; a third month of cuts with Stock RS below 50 would mean the market and the analysts agree on the car arm. The next company figures are the FY2026 results on 10-Mar-2027.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

D'Ieteren's ratings carry no signal: every analyst covering it has rated it Buy at every month-end since January 2026, and coverage has grown from 10 to 14. The ratings did not move through the 21.3% fall to 30-Mar or the 13.2% fall to 24-Sep: they follow the discount to Belron's value, not the SP.

D'Ieteren's analysts are raising PTs while cutting revenue, EBITDA and EPS: the PTs follow Belron's value, the estimates follow D'Ieteren Automotive.

  • D'Ieteren's PT Net Upgrade Breadth is +36% (6 of 14 raising, 1 cutting, 100 days to 30-Oct), from +46% at the 30-Sep month-end (6 of 13 raising, none cutting).
  • D'Ieteren's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth is (56%) (none raising, 5 of 9 cutting), its EBITDA breadth (62%) (5 of 8 cutting) and its FY2027 EPS breadth (21%) (1 raising, 4 of 14 cutting); all three turned negative at the 30-Sep month-end, after the 9-Sep results, and barely improved in October.
  • The last PT changes named in the sources read are September increases on higher Belron values: KBC Securities to €250 from €245 (Bolero, 16-Sep) and ING to €263 from €237 (Zone Bourse, 28-Sep).

Where analysts are cutting: D'Ieteren's average PT fell 2.0% in October, from €239.5 (29-Sep to 13-Oct) to €237.3 (22-Oct) and €234.7 (28-Oct), its largest monthly fall since September 2024. D'Ieteren's PT counts show one analyst cutting in the 100 days to 30-Oct, against none at the 30-Sep month-end. The brokers behind the October PT and EPS cuts are not named in the sources read; your AlphaSense daily screens, where they would show, could not be read. The stated logic on file for cuts is Deutsche Bank's after the results (profit before tax cut by about 1% for the heavier D'Ieteren Automotive drag, earnings review), which fits the nearer-year margin cuts in item 4 (Consensus by named FY).

D'Ieteren's average PT stands 35.7% above the SP, down from 46.9% at the 30-Sep month-end (the SP rose 6.1% and the PT fell 2.0%), and against 30.8% at the August month-end. In 2026 the gap has run from 15.5% (January month-end) to 49.1% (March). Jefferies wrote that its PT "assumes a punitive 30% discount" unless followed by buybacks (9-Sep, cited in the AlphaSense research of 31-Oct): the PTs are sums of the parts, led by Belron.

Precedent: the same divergence ran at D'Ieteren's March and April 2026 month-ends (PT breadth +30% and +18%, revenue breadth (25%) and (60%)), across the +20.9% rally that ended on 17-Apr, which the SP gave back by 8-Jun.

Test: PT changes when Belron's listing terms, or a delay, are reported; PT cuts without Belron news, continuing October's, would mean analysts have started to mark down Belron too.

9. Peers

D'Ieteren's cohort holds no other stock, so the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's peer table uses its sector, Automotive retail, aftermarket, leasing and rental.

D'Ieteren's sector is still split in two, and D'Ieteren trades between the halves, with the broken half's estimate trend. Inter Cars (Stock RS 95), ZIGUP (72) and Sixt (61) trade 7% to 30% above their absolute 200D MAs with positive revenue Net Upgrade Breadth; Ayvens (11), Inchcape (6) and Auto1 (6) trade 11% to 21% below theirs with negative revenue breadth. D'Ieteren (Stock RS 35) sits 0.3% below its absolute 200D MA, and its revenue breadth, (56%), is still the weakest in the table.

Your four peer checks, 23 to 30-Oct:

  • Sell-side estimates: the split holds; D'Ieteren's 3-month EPS revision, (4%), sits with Inchcape's (8%) and Auto1's (3%), not with Inter Cars' +9%.
  • SP technicals: the broken half bounced hardest in the week (Auto1 +7.6%, Ayvens +3.9%) while Inchcape fell 1.4%; Sixt rose 3.9% and its Stock RS climbed from 42 to 61 in a month. D'Ieteren, (0.5%), lagged five of its seven sector peers in a week its industry rose 1.7%.
  • Newsflow, and guidance and communications: the one guidance change is Inchcape's 22-Oct cut to its FY2026 adjusted operating margin guidance, to about 5.5% from about 6% (Deutsche Bank Research, 22-Oct, cited in the AlphaSense research of 31-Oct); D'Ieteren fell only 0.4% that day. Bilia reported its third quarter on 23-Oct (report not read).

Which half D'Ieteren trades with: neither, by the daily moves. D'Ieteren's own daily moves (after the market and Belgium) correlate only weakly with any stock tested: Sixt 0.23, Barco and Ackermans & van Haaren 0.20, Orange Belgium 0.19, the strongest of 46 candidates. Hypothesis: D'Ieteren trades on Belron news and its own buyback rather than with either half of its sector; the evidence for it is 8-Oct (up 2.2% while Ayvens, Auto1 and Sixt fell on heavy volume) and the week to 30-Oct (down while both halves rose); the evidence against it is the September revenue cuts, which match the broken half's.

Who turned first: Auto1 (Stock RS 27 three months ago, 4 a month ago), then Ayvens (62, 20, 11) and Inchcape (50, 38, 6); D'Ieteren slipped from 40 to 35 while Sixt rose from 40 to 61.

What the peers imply for D'Ieteren: with three sector stocks at Stock RS 11 or below, D'Ieteren's Sector RS, and so its "Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70" test, depends little on D'Ieteren's own move. A day on which D'Ieteren falls with Inchcape, Ayvens and Auto1, without Belron news, would be the first sign that its car arm is setting its price; a D'Ieteren rise alongside Inter Cars and Sixt would show the market pricing its parts and glass businesses.

  • The setup has improved; the evidence behind it has not. D'Ieteren's pullback has held its rising 20D MA on half the 50D average volume, with its narrowest daily ranges since late August (1.52% of the close on average over the last ten sessions, against 1.37% over the ten sessions to 26-Aug); but its MAs are stacked close to upside down, its highs keep coming lower, and relative price is 4.6% below a falling relative 200D MA.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file, and from the AlphaSense research filed for Volex on 29 and 31-Oct (the Key Question analysis of the 19-Oct fall, and the operating-leverage research).

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Fell 12% on 19-Oct. Huge! Trading update. Why did it fall? The update reads well to me". The answer the research supports: the update confirmed rather than raised, and its detail showed the data-centre line slowing from the half before; the brokers raised their numbers anyway.

  • 19-Oct, Volex's first-half trading update (press release, cited in the AlphaSense analysis of 29-Oct): first-half revenue of at least $710m; FY2027 underlying operating profit "towards the upper end of current market expectations"; data-centre revenue well ahead of the prior year "although below the second half of financial 2026"; second-half revenue expected to be broadly similar to the first half.
  • The same update said customers took about $10m of extra first-half deliveries during a US site consolidation, and $11m came from Kepler SignalTek, bought outright in July: both flatter the first half's 22% growth.
  • Jefferies (19-Oct, cited in the AlphaSense research of 31-Oct) estimated that Volex's second half of FY2026 included a one-off customer contribution of $20m to $30m. That would explain why data-centre revenue fell from the half before: the comparison base held a one-off.
  • The analysis of 29-Oct ranks the causes: profit-taking after a strong run on an update that confirmed rather than raised (high confidence); the sequential data-centre slowdown; the pulled-forward $10m; leverage (covenant about 1.2x after Kepler SignalTek and the buyback) as secondary. It found no evidence of lost data-centre share, and Zeus (19-Oct) called the fall profit-taking.
  • 22-Oct, Volex's £20m share buyback ended with 3.68M shares bought and cancelled (company announcement, cited in the analysis of 29-Oct). Hypothesis: the end of the company's own buying removed a steady buyer on the day of the 532p low; the daily size of that buying is not in the sources read.
  • Your reading that the update "reads very well" is the brokers' reading too (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)); the market priced the second-half revenue guide.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: two mechanical-volume days, 31-Aug and 18-Sep, sit in Volex's 50D average and lift it about 13%. The ratios below leave them out, with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's own figure in brackets.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "I note APM's concerns that volume was heavy on the down days and light on the up days - a red flag for adding". The red flag still stands after the add: Volex's 10.9% bounce came without one heavy-volume up day, and tranche 3 was bought on the bounce's lightest day.

  • Volex's five up days from 23 to 29-Oct averaged 0.84M shares, 63% of the October decline's down-day average (1.34M).
  • The best, 28-Oct (+3.6%, 1.20M shares, £6.95m), reached 1.28x (1.12x); 29-Oct, when Volex crossed its 100D and 150D MAs and tranche 3 was bought, traded 0.47M shares (£2.74m), 0.50x (0.43x), the month's lightest session by value traded and against the 50D average (12-Oct traded fewer shares, 0.42M, £2.88m).
  • That same 28-Oct, Huber + Suhner rose 3.8% on 1.76x its 50D average: Volex's peer drew a buyer of size and Volex did not.

Friday's 4.7% fall also came on light volume: sellers did not press either.

  • 30-Oct: 0.70M shares, £3.92m, 0.74x (0.65x), a 1.7% gap down from 590p to a 580p open, closing at 0.22 of the day's range. It is not a distribution day (under 1.25x).
  • Volex's distribution-day count in 25 sessions (dashboard) is 4 (8, 15, 19 and 20-Oct), unchanged since 23-Oct, against 1 on 2-Oct; no new one in the eight sessions since 20-Oct. The accumulation days (6 and 16-Oct) are both from before the low.
  • The dashboard's up/down volume ratio is 0.79 over 20 sessions and 1.18 over 5: the week leans to buyers, the month to sellers.

Volex's October decline packed more heavy selling into fewer days than its June decline: 4 heavy down days of 10 from 6 to 30-Oct (up/down volume 0.75), against 3 of 21 from 28-May to 8-Jul (0.76). The heaviest: 15-Oct ((5.9%), 1.85M shares, £11.7m, 2.34x (2.00x)), 19-Oct ((11.6%), 3.28M shares, £18.4m, 3.80x (3.29x), closing at the day's low) and 20-Oct ((0.2%), 2.12M shares, £11.9m, 2.37x (2.07x)).

Precedent: Volex's last two lows were confirmed by heavy-volume up days, and six sessions after 22-Oct none has come.

  • After the 8-Jul low, up days on 2.0x, 2.3x and 1.7x within five sessions; after the 17-Mar low, 1.7x on the fourth session and 3.7x (+8.1%) on the sixth. After the 22-Oct low, no up day above 1.28x in six sessions. Confirms a low: a Volex up day on about 1.4M shares or more (1.5x without the mechanical days, about £8m at 562p) closing in the top third of its range. Confirms the decline resumes: a close below 532p on above-average volume.

Volume at the MAs: Volex broke its absolute 200D MA on 1.04x (0.90x) on 22-Oct and reclaimed it on 0.86x (0.76x) on 27-Oct; only the 19-Oct break of the 50D, 100D and 150D MAs, on 3.80x, had conviction.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "SP is 23% below its 52W relative high". Volex's relative price is now 21.6% below its 52W high (28-May), 3.4 points from the 25% line, and back below its relative 200D MA.

  • Volex's relative price was 24.9% below its 52W high on 22-Oct and 18.1% on 29-Oct; it fell 4.2% on 30-Oct alone.

3. RS

Volex's Stock RS recovered first and fastest from the October rotation, but its industry has not recovered at all: Industry RS read 46 at Friday's reading, the same as on 23-Oct.

  • Volex's Stock RS: 97 on 9-Oct, 65 on 23-Oct, 85 on 29-Oct, 78 at the 30-Oct reading; 93 a month earlier, 61 three months earlier, 71 a year earlier.
  • Volex's Sector RS (Engineered components, polymers and electronics manufacturing): 82 on 2-Oct, 69 on 23-Oct, 70 on 30-Oct; 74 a month and 81 a year earlier.
  • Volex's Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 79 on 9-Oct, 46 on 23-Oct, 53 on 29-Oct, 46 on 30-Oct; 73 a month and 66 a year earlier; its low in the data file is 40 (13-Mar-2026).
  • The order: Volex's industry turned first (79 to 66 by 16-Oct, while Volex's Stock RS was still 94); Volex's Stock RS then fell 29 points in the week of its update. Volex has recovered 13 of those 29 points; its industry 0 of 33.
  • The link: your own note of 28 to 31-Oct reads the market as "Relative rotation into defensives (and energy)"; Volex's Industry RS at 46 says that rotation has not reversed for industrials. Volex's Stock RS is now carried by Volex, not by its industry.

Test: Volex's Industry RS at the Fri 6-Nov reading; a further fall would leave Volex's Stock RS exposed to its industry as well as its own results.

4. Consensus by named FY

Volex's consensus kept rising through the share price fall: FY2027 (year to Mar-27) EPS rose 2.9% in October while the SP fell 14.5%, so October was a de-rating, not a downgrade.

  • EPS over 1, 3 and 6 months: FY2027 +2.9%, +15.0%, +20.0% (+35.2% over 12); FY2028 +2.5%, +14.4%, +21.1%; FY2029 +2.2% and +13.1% over 1 and 3 months.
  • Revenue: FY2027 +1.8%, +6.8%, +12.2%; FY2028 +1.7%, +6.9%, +13.2%. EBITDA: FY2027 +2.0%, +10.5%, +21.4%; FY2028 +1.8%, +10.4%, +23.5%. Seven analysts cover each year.

The analysts are lifting the near year faster than the outer years: they are pulling earnings forward, not raising the trajectory.

  • In October FY2027 EPS rose 2.9%, FY2028 2.5% and FY2029 2.2%; the implied FY2028-on-FY2027 EPS growth eased from 11.7% (31-Jul) to 11.1%, and FY2029-on-FY2028 from 11.6% to 10.3%.
  • That matches the update's detail: the $10m of first-half deliveries pulled forward and second-half revenue "broadly similar" to the first (item 1 (SP and MAs)).
  • Implied revenue growth is 7.2% into FY2028 and 6.9% into FY2029; the implied EBITDA margin is 13.7% for FY2027, 13.8% for FY2028 and 13.9% for FY2029, up from 13.2% for FY2027 at 31-Jul.
  • EPS has risen faster than EBITDA in October (2.9% against 2.0% for FY2027); part of the gap is below the EBITDA line, consistent with the 3.68M shares cancelled under the buyback (item 1 (SP and MAs)).

The SP and the estimates diverged: Volex's SP fell 19.6% from its 6-Oct high while every FY's EPS rose (FY2027 +2.9%, FY2028 +2.5%, FY2029 +2.2% in October). Hypothesis: the market is pricing a second-half and FY2028 data-centre slowdown that consensus does not yet show. Against it: the update said data-centre revenue is still well ahead of the prior year, and Zeus (19-Oct) forecast FY2027 EPS of 53.5 cents, +23.8%, above the 52.06-cent consensus. Tests: the three first-half tests in the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (Complex Industrial Technology still growing; the largest customer's share stable or lower; cash conversion back above 70%), at the first-half results.

  • The results date: Zeus (19-Oct, cited in the AlphaSense analysis of 29-Oct) gives 12-Nov-2026; the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo gives 5-Nov-2026; the data file holds none.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Volex's analysts did not flinch at the 23.9% fall: 6 Buys of 7 at the 30-Sep and 30-Oct month-ends, every Net Upgrade Breadth reading for EPS, EBITDA and revenue at +100%, and three PT raises into the fall.

  • Jefferies raised its Volex PT to 750p from 700p and Zeus to 730p from 700p on 19-Oct (Zeus also raised adjusted operating profit 2.5%; Jefferies raised FY2027 earnings before interest, tax and amortisation 5%); Berenberg raised to 750p from 700p on 20-Oct, reiterating Buy (Sharecast, 20-Oct). These are the only houses whose research AlphaSense holds (the analysis of 29-Oct).
  • Volex's Net Upgrade Breadth at the 30-Oct month-end: EPS +100% (7 of 7 raising), EBITDA +100%, revenue +100%; PT (14%) (3 raising, 4 cutting) since 21-Oct, against +100% at 30-Sep.

The analysts agree on the numbers and split on what to pay: the 4 PT cuts are not named in any source read. The three named houses all raised; the four cuts sit with analysts whose research AlphaSense does not hold. Hypothesis: the cuts are real but small, since Volex's average PT still rose 0.4% across 21-Oct while 4 of 7 cut. Alternative: some cuts are a currency effect in a dollar-reporting stock's sterling PTs, as the daily PT series moves with the dollar (the 28-Oct note). Test: whether PT breadth turns back to net raising after the first-half results.

Volex's ratings follow valuation, not momentum. Buys fell from 5 to 3 at the May-2026 month-end, when Volex's SP stood above its average PT near the 696p top; they rose back to 6 by August after the fall to 497p. Coverage has grown from 4 to 7 analysts in 12 months.

Volex's average PT (731.3p, 28-Oct) is 30.1% above the 562p close, against 4.0% at the 699p high of 6-Oct. The last month-end gap this wide was July's (31.4%), at 511p near the 8-Jul low, after which Buys rose to 6 and the SP rose 37% to 699p. Hypothesis: a 30% gap points to a seventh Buy, or at least no downgrade, after the results. Against it: in October the SP's direction came from the momentum trade, not from the sell-side (item 1 (SP and MAs)).

Precedent: Volex's PT breadth has turned to zero or below near lows, not highs: (50%) at the August and October 2025 month-ends before the rise to 490p by Feb-2026; zero at the June 2026 month-end before the 8-Jul low.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Zeus initiation on 06-Aug - read it". Zeus's 6-Aug initiation is not among the files read for this run; its 19-Oct note (Buy, PT 730p) is cited in both AlphaSense research files.

9. Peers

Volex trades with the momentum group of its peers day to day and with the strong group on estimates; the week's test, whether the weak peers would follow Volex back above their absolute 200D MAs, failed.

  • Below their absolute 200D MAs on 30-Oct: Huber + Suhner (17.8%), Halma (10.8%), Oxford Instruments (7.5%), Solid State (2.1%); none followed Volex back above, the test the 28-Oct note set. Above: TT Electronics +47.2%, DiscoverIE +17.3%, AQ Group +8.7%, Volex +1.3% (chart files).
  • SP technicals: from 22 to 29-Oct every peer rose (Solid State +4.6% to Volex +10.9%); on 30-Oct all but Solid State fell. Volex's beta (1.59) makes it the group's amplifier: it led both moves.
  • Sell-side estimates: Volex's EPS revision over 3 months (+14%) equals TT Electronics' and beats AQ Group's (+9%), DiscoverIE's (+3%), Solid State's (+1%) and Huber + Suhner's ((2%)). On estimates Volex belongs with the strong group.
  • RS: TT Electronics 97 and DiscoverIE 84 held their Stock RS through October; Huber + Suhner fell to 20 from 42 a month earlier and 82 three months earlier. Volex at 78 sits between the two groups.
  • Newsflow and company communications (the AlphaSense analysis of 29-Oct): Huber + Suhner's net sales rose 31% to CHF 313.9m (Morgan Stanley), though its Communication order intake fell 41.4% against a prior year with large optical circuit switch orders (company release, 20-Oct, read for the 28-Oct note); discoverIE reported 8% organic sales growth; TE Connectivity's artificial-intelligence orders were up 70% in the year to date, and BizLink's September revenue rose 39% year on year. What it means for Volex: the data-centre demand behind its peers is strong, so the evidence does not support lost demand; it supports Volex's own one-off comparison (item 1 (SP and MAs)).
  • Outside the cohort, Oxford Instruments, whose own daily moves track Volex's most closely (correlation 0.65), fell 4.5% on 30-Oct at its day's low.

What the peers imply for Volex: until its results, Volex's daily moves come from the group's momentum trade, amplified. For the test "relative price more than 25% below its 52W high", one more group day like 30-Oct, when the peer group fell together and Volex's relative price fell 4.2%, would take Volex's relative price to within 0.1 point of the 25% threshold (24.9% below its 52W high; a 4.3% fall crosses it) (item 11 (the stock against each Sell Criterion)); a heavy-volume up day in the group, like Huber + Suhner's 28-Oct, that Volex joins is the turn to watch.

What the rules say for Volex now, and what decides the next step

  • The first finding: the bounce has no buyer of size. Volex rose 10.9% to 590p without one up day on 1.5x its 50D average, unlike its March and July lows; tranche 3 was bought on 29-Oct, the bounce's lightest day and highest close.
  • The second finding: the fundamentals moved the other way. FY2027 EPS rose 2.9% in October, all 7 analysts are raising estimates and 6 of 7 rate Volex Buy; the research ranks the 19-Oct fall as profit-taking on a confirming update.
  • The third finding: Volex's industry has not recovered. Industry RS read 46 on Fri 30-Oct, as on 23-Oct, while Volex's Stock RS recovered to 78; the October rotation out of industrials is intact.
  • What the rules say for each tranche today: hold. Volex is a Middle Innings Core Investment, sold only by its six Sell Criteria; none gave a sell signal at the 30-Oct Friday and month-end readings. Volex's 562p close is 14.9% above the 30%-fall level of tranches 1 and 2 (2.80% and 1.62%; 489.3p, 30% below the 699p high), so a 12.9% fall reaches it; it is 36.1% above tranche 3's level (1.10%; 413.0p, 30% below 590p), a 26.5% fall away.
  • The nearest sell test: relative price more than 25% below its 52W high, read next at the Mon 30-Nov month-end. Volex's relative price is 21.6% below its 52W high; at a flat market a close of about 537.6p or below (4.3% down) gives a sell signal. It moved 2.5 points away over the week (24.1% below on 23-Oct), 19.1 points towards the line over four weeks (2.5% below on 2-Oct), and was 28.2% below at the 31-Jul month-end. A sell signal on 30-Nov would sell the whole holding (5.52%) at the Tue 1-Dec session; Volex is not under the one-off in/out link.
  • How the tests interact: a fall to about 537p would likely take Volex's Stock RS below 70 too (it read 65 at 536p on 23-Oct), meeting the Stock RS half of "Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70"; Volex's Sector RS (70) would still need to fall more than 20 points, to below 50. A further fall to 489.3p with Stock RS below 70 would trigger tranches 1 and 2 under the 30%-fall rule, released at a 15% rebound or 63 trading days.
  • The relative 200D MA test comes into reach late in November: not on Fri 6-Nov at any realistic price (it would need an 86% fall). If Volex's relative price fell once on Mon 2-Nov and then held flat, the relative 200D MA would turn flat or falling over the month with a 17.2% fall by the Fri 20-Nov reading (about 465p at a flat market), or a 10.9% fall by the Fri 27-Nov reading (about 501p at a flat market).
  • The record: at the 31-Jul month-end Volex's relative price was 28.2% below its 52W high; today's rule would have sold Volex at the 4-Aug session at 537p, before the 25-Aug statement and the rise to 699p.
  • The next reading day, Fri 6-Nov: Industry RS below 30 (Volex's industry at 46, 16 points away) and the relative 200D MA test (not reachable). Adds: the rules allow an add, with 1.16% of room below the 6.68% limit. Where Volex stands for an add: may be retesting a rising MA (the absolute 200D MA), the second stage, one stage above the Master Dashboard's own reading of pulling back or basing (Pulling Back plausible, 3 of 4 tests; Healthy Retest possible, 2 of 6, SP 1.31% from the absolute 200D MA); not yet healthily retesting, because no up day has carried volume. Your retest trigger is partly present; your broadening trigger is present on guidance and estimates, absent on technicals and peers (item 12 (adding capital)).
  • The dates that matter: the first-half results, 12-Nov (Zeus) or 5-Nov (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo); the Bank of England decision on 5-Nov (Geography Digest).
  • What would change this reading: a Volex up day on about 1.4M shares or more closing in the top third of its range (towards a healthy retest); a close below 532p, under the absolute 200D MA, on above-average volume (a broken trend, and the 25% relative-price line within a day's move).

Richard decides.

Hilton Food

Early Innings Probing Bet · 3.30% of capital in 2 tranches (tranche 1, 1.30%, bought 1-Jul-2026; tranche 2, 2.00%, bought 6-Jul-2026) · readings as at Fri 30-Oct-2026

1. SP and MAs

On dividend-adjusted prices Hilton Food made a new 52W closing high on Fri 30-Oct (719.5p), but on 0.48 times its 50D average volume: a quiet drift to the top of a tightening range, not yet a breakout.

  • Hilton Food went ex-dividend on 29-Oct for its 10.1p interim dividend (Alliance News, 16-Oct, as read for the 30-Oct note), 1.4% of the 28-Oct close.
  • A data fault, still open: the chart file behind the PMS Per-stock Dashboard took that dividend off as about 0.1p, so it shows Hilton Food 0.9% below its 21-Oct closing high of 726.4p. Correctly adjusted, the 21-Oct high is about 716.4p and the 30-Oct close is 0.4% above it.
  • Hilton Food is up 16.8% over 3 months on the chart file and about 68% above its 29-Jan low on adjusted prices. Its legs: 30-Jul to 3-Sep, +24.6%; 3 to 11-Sep, (5.9%); 11-Sep to 21-Oct, +9.0%; since then sideways.

This week Hilton Food kept pace with the market and beat its industry, after a month in which it lagged its industry but beat the market. In the week to 30-Oct Hilton Food rose 1.7% on adjusted prices (0.3% on the chart file), the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.7% and Consumer staples fell 0.1%. Over the month Hilton Food rose 3.9% on adjusted prices against 8.0% for Consumer staples and 3.2% for the Stoxx Europe 600.

Two hypotheses for Hilton Food's flat October, ranked by the evidence. 1. Digestion of the 3-Sep results jump (+12.2% on 6.6 times the 50D average volume). For: narrowing closing ranges on drying volume (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)). Against: only a breakout proves it. 2. Consumer staples' rotation went to Food production's laggards, not its leaders. For: in the month to 30-Oct Nestle's Stock RS rose from 35 to 63 and Danone's from 8 to 31, each SP up about 13.5%, while Hilton Food's Stock RS slipped from 89 to 87. Against: this week Hilton Food beat Consumer staples by about 1.8 points.

Test, by Fri 6-Nov: a Hilton Food close above 719.5p on volume above its 50D average (about 197,000 shares, £1.4m) favours digestion ending in a breakout; Hilton Food falling on days Nestle and Danone rise favours the laggard rotation.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file, with the AlphaSense research on Hilton Food's operating leverage filed on 30-Oct.

  • 29-Oct: an Expert Interview reported in that research says Hilton Food's Brantford plant for Walmart Canada, due to launch in January 2027, "must secure local processing expertise to manage high line speeds effectively".
  • 29-Oct: the UK Competition and Markets Authority opened an inquiry into ABP's purchase of a stake in Dovecote Park, a Waitrose supplier (same research); ABP competes for Hilton Food's UK and Irish packing volume.
  • The same research concludes that retailers "claw back" Hilton Food's automation savings at contract resets, the strongest bear argument read against a further profit leg.
  • No other Hilton Food item dated 23 to 30-Oct is in the sources read, so the cause of the week's light-volume rise is not in the sources read.

On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Q - Risk from Tesco and CEE?". Tesco has already cost Hilton Food volume once; Central and Eastern Europe is Hilton Food's fastest-growing business, with concentration and execution risk rather than demand risk.

  • Tesco: Tesco moved its Scottish stores to 100% Scottish beef and Hilton Food stopped supplying that beef, which held West core meat volumes to +0.6% in the first half of FY2026 (earnings review of the 3-Sep results). Against that, Hilton Food renewed its Tesco partnership (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo), and Morgan Stanley expects Tesco to gain about 0.4 points of volume share a year to FY2028 through own-label value ranges (earnings review). The memo's Tesco tests (an Alarm on in-sourcing or a re-tender; an Invalidation on a core contract lost) are untouched in the sources read.
  • Central and Eastern Europe: Hilton Food's Central European fresh prepared food volumes grew 26% in the first half, riding Żabka's 11,000 convenience stores, which add about 1,000 a year (earnings review). The risks are dependence on Żabka and the up to £30m Polish expansion (Shore Capital), decided around the end of 2026 (Sector Digest); no source read shows a problem.

Every Hilton Food MA is rising and stacked (20D above 50D above 100D above 150D above the absolute 200D MA), and the SP has not closed below its 20D MA since 9-Jul.

Hilton Food against your trend-health tests, to tell a healthy retest from a broken trend. No pullback is under way, so this is a health check. Hilton Food passes the stacking tests, the SP above its 50D, 150D and absolute 200D MAs, near the high and away from the low, more down days than up days (11 up and 9 down over the last 20 sessions), the largest-fall flag (no fall since 29-Jan beyond 27-Mar's (3.1%) on adjusted prices), wider swings (daily range 2.0% over the last 5 sessions against 2.7% over the 20 sessions to 2-Oct) and lower lows (none since 11-Sep). Hilton Food's year ends in December; EPS in pence, revenue and EBITDA in £m.

Analysts have raised Hilton Food's nearer two years and trimmed the third, so the trajectory is being pulled forward, not raised.

  • So the implied growth in Hilton Food's EPS from FY2027 to FY2028 fell from 11.0% at 30-Sep to 9.1% at 30-Oct.

Analysts raise Hilton Food's revenue faster than its profit, so the implied margin keeps slipping. Over three months Hilton Food's FY2027 revenue rose 5.4% (to £4,798m) and FY2027 EBITDA 2.4% (to £156.4m), so the implied FY2027 EBITDA margin fell from 3.35% to 3.26%. The earnings review gives the mechanism: raw materials pass through at cost, so beef inflation lifts revenue and not profit.

All of October's rise in Hilton Food's estimates dates from one day, 8-Oct, when the number of analysts counted fell. Hilton Food's daily FY2027 series stepped up on 8-Oct (EPS +1.0%, revenue +1.3%) and has not moved since. Between 30-Sep and 30-Oct the analysts with a FY2027 EPS estimate fell from 6 to 5, and the ratings went from 4 Buys and 1 other to 4 Buys and none. Hypothesis: a lower-forecasting analyst stopped being counted, which lifted the averages without any raise. Against: the data does not name the analyst, and no raise or withdrawal is in the sources read.

Hilton Food's SP has run far ahead of its estimates. Since the 30-Jun month-end Hilton Food's SP rose 39.7% (chart file) while its FY2027 EPS rose 7.4%. On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "IMHO the case needs another fundamental leg of delivery for SP to power onwards or it'll retrace hard". The dated test: the January 2027 trading update against the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's cleared branch (Canada on time, mid-single-digit core growth in FY2027).

Healthy retest or broken trend? Hexatronic against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes; up 5.6% over 20 sessions, rising in 9 of the last 12 months.
  • The 150D above the absolute 200D MA: passes; 39.96 against 36.89.
  • The 50D above the 150D and the absolute 200D MA: passes, narrowly against the 150D (1.5%).
  • The SP above each: passes; 12.9% above the 50D, 24.1% above the absolute 200D.
  • Near its 52W high: passes; 5.8% below 48.58.
  • Away from its low: passes; 174% above the 16.70 closing low (18-Nov-2025).

Hexatronic against your Stage 3 (topping) red flags

  • The largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began, after good news: partly; the (3.2%) fall of 30-Oct followed good news, but it is smaller than the (5.7%) fall of 24-Sep, the largest one-day fall since the 29-Jul low.
  • Wider swings: partly; October's median daily move (2.0%) is wider than September's (1.5%), and narrower than June's (3.6%), the month in which the stock topped.
  • Lower lows: no, with one exception; the lows of the four declines since 3-Jun each rose (33.36 on 8-Jul, 36.19 on 29-Jul, 37.13 on 18-Sep, 38.76 on 20-Oct), but the 18-Sep low (37.13) undercut the 20-Aug low (38.22).
  • More down days than up days: no; 36 up and 29 down since 29-Jul.
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: no; the breakout was heavy (28-Oct, 2.76x), the first reversal light (30-Oct, 0.60x).
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: no; it bounced 22.0% off the 150D MA.

Verdict: healthy. Hypothesis 1 (the stronger evidence): the report reset Hexatronic's earnings base and Hexatronic is breaking out of its June-to-October base, on the higher lows, the volume, the relative price's higher high (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)) and the estimates (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). Hypothesis 2: a repeat of July, when the 15-Jul report lifted Hexatronic 36.2% in six sessions and it then fell 20.3% in nine with Nokia and Mycronic. For it: Nokia is breaking down (item 9 (Peers)) and consensus has run past the memo's base (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). Against it: July's fall ran on down-day volume 1.7 times up-day volume; October's first down day was the month's lightest. What would settle it: a close above 48.58 on above-average volume confirms the breakout; a heavy-volume close below the 20D MA (42.32) points to July.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

A correction first: the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep sit inside Hexatronic's 50D average and lower the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's volume ratios by 10% to 14%. The volume ratios below (each day's volume as a multiple of the 50D average) leave out those days, with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's own ratio in brackets. The 50D average is 1.40M shares a day (about SEK 57m).

Hexatronic's October advance is its heaviest-volume rise of the year, and the first fall after it came on the month's lightest volume.

  • Hexatronic's seven up days from 21 to 29-Oct averaged 2.77M shares, twice the 50D average and above the 2.42M average of the four up days of the July rebound that failed (8 to 16-Jul).
  • On 30-Oct Hexatronic fell 3.2% on 0.76M shares, SEK 35m, 0.60x (0.54x), after a 2.7% gap down, closing in the bottom fifth of the range. Nokia, Logitech, Mycronic and Kitron fell 3% to 7% that day; Hexatronic's holders did not sell in size into a sector sell day.

Hexatronic's heavy-volume days (1.5x or more) in the last 25 sessions: five up, one down.

  • 2-Oct: +3.3% on 1.78M shares, SEK 75m, 1.78x (1.56x), mid-range.
  • 15-Oct: (3.4%) on 2.23M shares, SEK 91m, 2.29x (2.00x), a distribution day, on a Swedish technology sell day while Ericsson rose 6.8%.
  • 22-Oct, the report: +5.0% on 6.18M shares, SEK 253m, 5.62x (4.99x), after an 8.5% gap up, closing in the bottom quarter of the range (high 46.03); 23-Oct: +2.9% on 2.59x (2.31x), again in the bottom third. Sellers met the July high on both days.
  • 26-Oct: +5.9% on 1.96M shares, SEK 88m, 1.67x (1.49x), closing in the top 6% of the range, above the 5-Oct high (42.81).
  • 28-Oct: +4.0% on 3.46M shares, SEK 161m, 2.76x (2.50x), closing in the top fifth of the range above the July closing high (45.42).
  • Hypothesis: the supply at 45 to 46 on 22 and 23-Oct was holders selling into the report, absorbed by 28-Oct. For it: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (25-Oct) records Handelsbanken Fonder (9.34% to 5.81%), AP3 and Accendo reducing into the rally. Against it: those changes are not dated to these sessions.

Distribution and accumulation: two distribution days in 25 sessions (15 and 19-Oct, both in the pullback), seven accumulation days (2, 21, 22, 23, 26, 28 and 29-Oct); the PMS Per-stock Dashboard counts one distribution day, down from three on 16-Oct. The 20-session up/down volume ratio (up-day volume divided by down-day volume) is 1.91, from 0.82 on 20-Oct; the dashboard's 5-session ratio is 3.06. Over the whole move from the 29-Jul low the up/down volume ratio is 1.31, between the November-to-April advance (1.47) and the April-to-June advance (1.10) that ended at the June top; the October leg carries all the improvement.

Hexatronic's base contracted in price but not in volume: declines of (31.3%), (20.3%), (10.8%) and (9.5%) since 3-Jun, on average volume of 0.96M, 1.27M, 0.92M and 1.07M shares. The October contraction did not dry up and held both distribution days: the weak point of the volatility contraction pattern reading (item 12 (adding capital)).

At the MAs: Hexatronic broke its 50D and 150D MAs on 19-Oct on 1.44x (1.26x), traded below the 150D MA on 20 and 21-Oct but closed in the top 11% and 7% of the range on 1.15x and 1.47x, and recovered every MA on 22-Oct on 5.62x: the recovery drew four times the volume of the break.

Precedent: Hexatronic's declines have ended with one heavy-volume up day near the low (2.5x after 18-Nov-2025, 2.6x after 29-Apr, 4.0x after 8-Jul, 5.62x on 22-Oct). The July rebound is the warning: it too began on heavy volume, rose 36.2% in six sessions, then gave back 20.3% on down-day volume 1.7 times up-day volume.

Hexatronic's relative price is 9.5% below its 52W high (3-Jun) and 17.8% above its rising relative 200D MA; on 29-Oct it confirmed the SP's break above the July high. It reached 26.00, above its July high (25.69, 17-Jul), the first higher relative high since June; its lows have risen (18.93 on 13-Jul, 20.17 on 29-Jul, 20.74 on 17-Sep, 21.62 on 20-Oct). Neither the SP nor relative price has cleared the June high.

Confirms the advance continues: a close above 48.58 on more than 1.40M shares, in the top third of the range. Confirms a turn: a heavy-volume down day closing near its low, or a close below the 20D MA (42.32) on above-average volume.

3. RS

Hexatronic's Stock RS held at the top of the scale through October while Hardware's Sector RS more than halved: Hexatronic's strength is its own, inside one of the weakest sectors.

  • Hexatronic's Stock RS was 96 at the 30-Oct month-end, from 94 (30-Sep), 93 (31-Jul) and 4 (31-Oct-2025).
  • Hardware's Sector RS was 26 at the 30-Oct month-end, from 56 (30-Sep), 38 (31-Jul) and 89 (31-Oct-2025).
  • Technology's Industry RS was 79 at the 30-Oct reading, from 86 at every Friday reading since 25-Sep, 73 (31-Jul) and 40 (31-Oct-2025).

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Sector RS 35 (!) is negative - investigate why". Hardware's Sector RS fell in two steps.

  • Before the reports: from 64 (2-Oct) to 35 (16-Oct), in the Swedish and optical technology sell-off of mid-October (Mycronic (8.1%) on 15-Oct; the 13-Oct Sector Digest's "AI monetization anxiety" for optics and fibre).
  • On the reports: HMS Networks fell 7.6% on 8.9x volume (21-Oct), Kitron 10.1% and Nokia 9.8% (22-Oct), Logitech 10.8% (28-Oct), then Nokia 7.3% and Logitech 4.2% on 30-Oct, taking Hardware's Sector RS from 35 (29-Oct) to 26 (30-Oct) in one session. Beyond the reporting dates, the causes are not in the sources read.
  • For Hexatronic: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (25-Oct) finds the hardware basket explains 6% of Hexatronic's daily variance; its economic peers, Corning and Prysmian, sit outside it. Hardware's slump is mostly other end markets; Nokia is the exception (item 9 (Peers)).

The order of the turn: Hexatronic led its industry by a quarter and now diverges from its sector. Hexatronic's Stock RS rose from 24 (30-Jan) to 85 (13-Mar) while Technology's Industry RS sat at 13 to 20, and Hexatronic's has been 85 or above at every reading since, including June's decline.

Precedent: on 21-Aug Hardware's Sector RS was 17 with Hexatronic's Stock RS at 94; Hexatronic then fell 10.8% and its Stock RS dipped to 85 (4-Sep), with no sell signal. What would change this: a Hexatronic Stock RS below 90 at a Friday reading while Hardware stays below 50 would be the first sign of the sector pulling Hexatronic down.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts raised Hexatronic's FY2027 EPS 10.3% in October, more than FY2026 (7.2%) and FY2028 (7.0%): they pulled earnings into FY2027 rather than raised the trajectory.

  • FY2027 EPS rose from SEK 2.672 (30-Sep) to SEK 2.946 (30-Oct), FY2026 from SEK 2.26 to SEK 2.422, FY2028 from SEK 3.072 to SEK 3.286; five analysts carry each year on both dates.
  • Implied FY2027-to-FY2028 growth fell from 15.0% to 11.5%; FY2026-to-FY2027 rose from 18.2% to 21.6%. This fits the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's (25-Oct) warning that comparisons harden from the second quarter of 2027.

Hexatronic's EPS outran revenue and EBITDA: FY2027 revenue rose 3.8% (to SEK 9,561m) and EBITDA 4.5% (to SEK 1,342m), with the margin flat at 14.0%. With five analysts on both dates, the explanation in the 28-Oct note, that a smaller set of analysts caused the gap, no longer holds; lower interest (adjusted leverage 1.3x, from 1.7x) and a lower tax rate (29.6% against 35.1%; earnings review, 22-Oct) explain more of it than operating profit.

Part of Hexatronic's FY2026 upgrade is probably a one-off. The memo calculates that the SEK 67m Clinton impairment reversal adds about SEK 0.23 a share to FY2026 EPS; the FY2026 rise was SEK 0.16. Hypothesis: little of the FY2026 upgrade recurs; FY2027 is the year that matters.

Hexatronic's consensus has passed the memo's base case: FY2027 EPS of SEK 2.946 against the memo's SEK 2.78 base, SEK 3.2 upside and Redeye's post-report SEK 2.85. The memo expected a "+5-9%" November revision burst; it came in October and reached 10.3%. For the add decision: the next upgrade needs the fourth-quarter report (4-Feb-2027) to beat.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "FX is likely to be a big headwind, similar to Xvivo - major fundamental risk; read the memo". The memo finds the krona a revenue risk more than an earnings risk: a 10% stronger krona removes about 9% of reported revenue but only about 3% of EPS (SEK 0.08), "because production is local", at a 25% to 30% probability. The memo's larger risks are the end of the United States refill (20% to 25%; EBITA (15%) to (25%)) and a hyperscaler pause (15% to 20%). The 16-Oct Geography Digest expects a Riksbank hike in November.

Test: whether FY2028 keeps rising with FY2027 at the 30-Nov month-end.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Every Net Upgrade Breadth reading for Hexatronic is +100% at the 30-Oct month-end, and no analyst is cutting anything.

  • EPS and EBITDA: five of five raised FY2027 (+100%), from +20% at the 30-Sep month-end. Revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth: +100% (five of five at the 30-Oct month-end reading; four of four in the dashboard's series to 28-Oct), from +20% and 0%.
  • Where they diverge: in size. FY2027 EPS rose 10.3% and the average PT 4.5%: earnings are rising faster than targets.

Hexatronic gained a fourth Buy on 30-Oct: four Buys, no Hold, no Sell, the first all-Buy month-end since March 2025. The broker is not named in the sources read. It fits the pattern of the last year, in which Hexatronic's Buys rose after reports (February, July, October) rather than after price moves; the exceptions were Buys dropped at the March and June-2026 month-ends with no report.

Hexatronic's average PT has followed the SP. It was SEK 47.6 from the July month-end to 21-Oct, then SEK 49.75 by 28-Oct (+4.5%), more than double the SEK 22.62 of July 2025; it sits 8.7% above the SP, from 22.8% on 20-Oct. The precedent that matters: at the May-2026 month-end the SP was 7.2% above the average PT, three sessions before the June top and the 31.3% fall. The SP has room to the PT now; it did not then.

Net Upgrade Breadth reached +100% after each of the last three reports, and the SP did opposite things after the first two. After February's, Hexatronic rose 98% to the 3-Jun high, climbing with the estimates; after July's, it fell 20.3% from 16 to 29-Jul, having moved before breadth turned. October sits between: the SP moved first on 22-Oct, and the revisions followed through the week.

The one documented post-report broker view is cautious on the run rate. Redeye (22-Oct) expected to raise its forecasts and Base Case "slightly", discounting the third-quarter Data Center outperformance as seasonality and project timing (earnings review, 22-Oct).

Test: the 30-Nov month-end ratings and PTs, for a PT cut while estimates hold.

9. Peers

The peer table and chart on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard show Hexatronic first or second in its cohort on every measure; the points below are what they imply.

Within its cohort Hexatronic now trades with Ericsson, not Nokia; Ericsson turned first and Nokia has broken down.

  • SP technicals: Ericsson's Stock RS rose from 36 to 69 in the month after its +6.8% on 4.8x volume on 15-Oct, and it is above its absolute 50D and 200D MAs. Nokia's fell from 91 to 44; Nokia is below both MAs and 46.7% below the 52W high it set on 3-Jun, the day of Hexatronic's own.
  • Newsflow: Nokia fell 9.8% on its 22-Oct report (Optical Networks sales +18%, artificial intelligence and cloud orders +118%, a lower free cash flow conversion assumption; Nokia, 22-Oct, as quoted in the 28-Oct note) and 7.3% on 30-Oct, a fall whose cause is not in the sources read.
  • Sell-side estimates: Nokia's 3M EPS revision is +3% with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +50%; Ericsson's and Adtran Networks' are not in the data file. Hexatronic's 3M EPS revision of +11% and revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of +100% lead the cohort.
  • Company guidance: Corning reported Optical Communications +44% and guided fourth-quarter core sales up about 19% (Corning, 27-Oct, as quoted in the 28-Oct note). Prysmian's 29-Oct results are not in the sources read.
  • Adtran Networks (Stock RS 38) moves with neither.

Hexatronic's daily moves still track Swedish industrial technology: Mycronic (correlation 0.60) and Atlas Copco (0.39), level with Nokia (0.39). They fell together on 15-Oct; from 20 to 29-Oct Hexatronic rose 22.0% and Mycronic about 6%; on 30-Oct Mycronic fell 3.6% and Hexatronic 3.2%. Hypothesis: the report loosened Hexatronic's link to Swedish technology for a week, and 30-Oct was the first day it moved with them again.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Weird it's not sold off in October in line with the grid names". Hexatronic did sell off in October, 9.5% from 5 to 20-Oct with Mycronic, Atlas Copco and Nokia, closing below its 50D and 150D MAs; the report on 22-Oct then separated it. The grid names are not among the stocks whose daily moves track Hexatronic's in the data file.

Hexatronic is the strongest stock in a weakening sector, as in August. Only secunet Security Networks (Stock RS 88) is close; HMS Networks (42, from 79), Kitron (54, from 75) and Logitech (34, from 59) fell over the month, mostly on their reports (item 3 (RS)).

What the peers imply: Hexatronic's next move depends more on its own estimates, Corning's and Prysmian's news and Swedish technology sell days than on its sector; Nokia's breakdown is the peer risk, as the one cohort peer also selling into the artificial intelligence optical build.

What the rules say for Hexatronic now, and what decides the next step

  • The finding that matters most: Hexatronic broke out of its June-to-October range on heavy volume, and its first down day came on light volume. It cleared the 5-Oct high (42.81) on 26-Oct and the July closing high (45.42) on 28-Oct on 2.76x volume; relative price made its first higher high since June; all six trend-health tests pass. The 3-Jun high of 48.58 is not cleared.
  • The finding that tempers it: Hexatronic's estimates have run past the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's base case. FY2027 EPS is SEK 2.946 against the memo's SEK 2.78; analysts pulled earnings into FY2027; part of FY2026's rise is probably a one-off. The next upgrade needs the 4-Feb-2027 report.
  • The peer risk: Hardware's Sector RS is 26 and Nokia has broken down; Hexatronic's Stock RS of 96 shows no sign yet of following.
  • Each tranche today: hold all four (1.20%, 2.66%, 0.43%, 0.66%). Hexatronic is a Middle Innings Core Investment, sold by its six Sell Criteria alone; none gives a sell signal at the 30-Oct readings, and nothing sells at the Tue 3-Nov session.
  • Nearest, Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (30-Nov month-end): one part met (Hardware 26), the other far: Hexatronic's Stock RS of 96 must lose 27 points. It moved 97 to 96 in a week, 97 (2-Oct) to 96 in four weeks and 93 (31-Jul) to 96 in thirteen, and has been 85 or above since 13-Mar-2026.
  • Next, relative price more than 25% below its 52W high (30-Nov month-end): 9.5% below, from 15.4% a week ago, 14.9% on 2-Oct and 21.3% on 4-Aug. With the market unchanged, a Hexatronic close below SEK 37.92 on 30-Nov gives a sell signal: a 17.2% fall, about two usual weekly moves, which would also break the 50D, 100D and 150D MAs.
  • How the tests interact: a fall of about 17% reaches the relative price line first; Stock RS stayed at 90 or above through June's 31.3% fall, so that fall would probably give one sell signal, not two. A 27.7% fall (to SEK 33.10) with Stock RS below 70 would trigger each tranche under the 30% test.
  • The record: every month-end since June fell inside the relative price line (19.0% to 24.6% below), while days between went beyond it (26-Jun; 6 to 14-Jul; 28 to 30-Jul; 3-Aug; 4-Sep; 15, 17 and 18-Sep). Whether a month-end lands inside a swing as large as Hexatronic's usual weekly move (8.7%) decides the reading; at 9.5% the line is further away than at any month-end since May.
  • Fri 6-Nov reads Industry RS below 30 (Technology 79) and the 200D MA of relative price flat or falling (rising 4.97% over the month, from 4.54%); neither is close.
  • Adds: the rules allow an add. No sell signal; 1.73% of room below the 6.68% limit; Technology far inside the 30% industry cap.
  • Where Hexatronic stands for an add: the third stage, breaking out of a volatility contraction pattern, by my reading. Declines contracted from 31.3% to 20.3%, 10.8% and 9.5% with higher lows, and the breakout cleared 42.81 and 45.42 on volume above the 50D average. Against it: volume did not dry up in the October contraction, and 48.58 is not cleared. The Master Dashboard's tests point to none of the three: pulling back and basing each pass 2 of 4 tests, a healthy retest passes 3 of 6 with no MA retested, and its volatility contraction pattern tests pass 0 of 4, because the dashboard notes that its pattern detection is not yet built and runs only a Stage 2 check.
  • Your add triggers: a broadening of the case is present (sell-side estimates and technicals; absent in guidance, mixed in peers); the positive retest of the rising 150D MA was partly present and is past (19 to 22-Oct).
  • On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "I need to read the memo and unless I see problems, I add 1% on Monday due to plausible VCP and breakout". The memo's points that bear on it: its positive skew lives in the next two reports (about 70% and 65% odds of a beat, 4-Feb and late April 2027) and turns negative from the second quarter of 2027; its expected revision burst has already happened (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)); the krona costs about 3% of EPS per 10% (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). The data file, rebuilt at 14:31 UK on 2-Nov with readings as at 30-Oct, shows four tranches; an add made on 2-Nov is not in it.
  • For your ruling: the rules size a Middle Innings Core Investment tranche at 1.67% of capital; your note plans 1%, taking Hexatronic to 5.95% against 6.62% with a full tranche.
  • Dates: a likely Riksbank hike in November (16-Oct Geography Digest); Hexatronic's fourth-quarter report on 4-Feb-2027 (the data file; the memo gives 5-Feb).
  • What would change this reading: a close above 48.58 on more than 1.40M shares confirms the breakout; a heavy-volume close below the 20D MA (42.32) puts Hexatronic back inside the base.

Richard decides.

Carlsberg

Middle Innings Core Investment, held under Richard's one-off in/out link with no sell signal · 3.98% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 1.90%, dated 22-Jun-2026, the start of the position record, which may not be its purchase date; tranche 2, 0.86%, bought 7-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 1.22%, bought 19-Aug-2026) · readings as at Fri 30-Oct-2026

1. SP and MAs

Carlsberg's SP fell 4.7% in five sessions from its DKK 980.6 high of 22-Oct to DKK 934.2 on 29-Oct, the day of its third-quarter statement, then rose 3.9% to DKK 971.0 on 30-Oct, 1.1% below its DKK 981.8 closing high of 12-Feb. Over the week Carlsberg rose 0.3% against 1.7% for the Stoxx Europe 600, a stock-specific shortfall of 1.2 points after the 1.5 points its beta of 0.86 implies, within its usual weekly move of 3.3%. Over 20 sessions Carlsberg is up 13.3% against 2.4% for the Stoxx Europe 600, of which 11.2 points are its own, beyond the 2.1 points its beta implies; that is 1.8 times its usual 20-session move of 6.3%; over 3 months it is up 3.6%, over 12 months up 27.3%.

The legs of Carlsberg's last week, against the brewers (AB InBev, Heineken, Royal Unibrew), Consumer staples and the Stoxx Europe 600:

  • 22 to 27-Oct: Carlsberg (2.8%); brewers (3.4%) (AB InBev) to (1.4%) (Heineken) as the rotation into staples paused. 27-Oct's (1.4%) was Carlsberg's own (brewers (0.3%) to (0.6%), Stoxx Europe 600 +0.5%).
  • 28-Oct: Carlsberg +1.0%; Heineken +1.6% on its strong China update.
  • 29-Oct: Carlsberg (3.0%), closing on its low; AB InBev (3.2%), Campari (6.6%), Diageo (2.1%), Heineken (0.2%); Consumer staples (0.5%); Stoxx Europe 600 +0.8%. About half of Carlsberg's fall was Beverages' results-day selling and half was Carlsberg's own.
  • 30-Oct: Carlsberg +3.9%; brewers +1.8% to +3.0%; Consumer staples +1.0%; Stoxx Europe 600 (0.4%). Carlsberg rose by the brewers' rebound plus a further 1.6 points of its own.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file. The earnings review covers the first half, so the third-quarter facts come from the AlphaSense research filed in Carlsberg's folder (the Change Forces report of 29-Oct, the Key Question research of 31-Oct, the Risks report of 2-Nov) and the Digests.

  • 29-Oct, Carlsberg's third-quarter statement: organic revenue +3.0% against a 2.6% consensus, on revenue per hectolitre +3%; reported revenue DKK 25,281m; total volume (0.4%) against a +0.6% consensus (Change Forces report; the Risks report gives organic volume +0.3%); China volume about (10%) on destocking, weather and weak nightlife (Morgan Stanley; Rothschild & Co Redburn); Central and Eastern Europe and India organic revenue +11.7% on the Kazakhstan Pepsi bottling; soft drinks volume +8.5%; full-year guidance reiterated (your 28-Oct note); currency guidance raised to +DKK 100m from nil (Risks report). The chief executive: "We are here to drive value for our shareholders and not drive empty volume."
  • 27 or 28-Oct: the draft prospectus for the listing of Carlsberg India (the sources differ on the date); Bernstein puts the proceeds at up to $700m, which would speed the deleveraging that the memo's buyback case needs. No source read links it to the SP.
  • 30-Oct: Bernstein, "Carlsberg 3Q26: A noisy quarter" and "Quick Take: Carlsberg Management offer reassurance on volume growth and margins at Group Meeting" (titles cited in the Risks report; Bernstein notes the Carlsberg brand growing about 20% in competitive channels). The ratings moved from 20 Buys, 6 other and 1 Sell at 30-Sep to 20, 8 and 0 at the 30-Oct month-end (data file); the data file does not date the change within October.

Hypotheses for 29 and 30-Oct, ranked by evidence. 1. The market sold Carlsberg's China miss, then bought the reassurance and the upgrades. For it: the 30-Oct Bernstein meeting; estimates up 0.5% to 0.7% (item 4 (Consensus by named FY), Consensus by named FY); EPS and EBITDA breadth turned (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth), Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth); 30-Oct's volume and 1.6 points over the brewers. Against it: a mid-range close after failing at DKK 982.0. 2. Beverages' results-day selling and month-end rebalancing, with Carlsberg passive. For it: AB InBev, Campari and Diageo fell with Carlsberg on 29-Oct and every brewer rose on 30-Oct, a month-end. Against it: Carlsberg's +3.9% on 30-Oct was the largest rise among the brewers and spirits makers named (Diageo +3.3%, Royal Unibrew +3.0%). On 29-Oct, though, Carlsberg's (3.0%) fall was smaller than AB InBev's (3.2%) and Campari's (6.6%), which supports this hypothesis for the statement day. Test: Carlsberg holding above DKK 934.2 while AB InBev stays weak supports hypothesis 1.

The MAs: Carlsberg is above every MA, and the absolute 200D MA is rising, but the MAs are still not in the order of an advance.

  • MA order on 30-Oct: 20D (DKK 925.7), 100D (899.0), 50D (889.6), absolute 200D (880.0), 150D (879.4). The 150D sits 0.07% below the absolute 200D MA, and the 50D MA is below the 100D MA.
  • The ceiling: closing highs of DKK 981.8 (12-Feb), 964.8 (30-Jul) and 980.6 (22-Oct), and an intraday high of DKK 982.0 on 30-Oct. February's gave way to a 20.0% fall and July's to a 12.9% fall.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Carlsberg against your trend-health tests (a 4.7% pullback, recovered to within 1.1% of the high)

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes (+1.4% over 20 sessions).
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: fails, by 0.07% (DKK 879.4 against 880.0).
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: passes, by 1.2% and 1.1%; the 50D is flat over 20 sessions.
  • The SP above each MA: passes, 4.9% above the 20D MA to 10.4% above the 150D MA.
  • Near its 52W high: passes, 1.1% below the closing high.
  • Away from its 52W low: passes, 32.5% above the DKK 732.6 low of 18-Nov-2025.

Your Stage 3 (topping) red flags:

  • The largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began, especially after good news: present. 29-Oct's (3.0%) is Carlsberg's largest one-day fall since 9-Sep; 22 to 29-Oct's (4.7%) is the largest five-session fall since the five sessions to 21-Aug ((5.1%)), and the largest since the advance began on 18-Sep (the base's largest was (3.4%)); it followed a mixed statement, not good news.
  • Wider swings: present. Carlsberg's average daily move over 5 sessions is 1.9%, against 1.0% over the previous 20.
  • Lower lows: absent. The 29-Oct low (DKK 934.2) is far above the 1-Oct low (DKK 842.0).
  • More down days than up days: partly; 4 down and 2 up since 22-Oct, against 17 up and 7 down in the advance.
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: absent; there has been no breakout above DKK 981.8, and 30-Oct's heavy volume was buying.
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: absent; on 29-Oct Carlsberg held 1.5% above its rising 20D MA and bounced 3.9% the next day.

Verdict: healthy so far: two of six Stage 3 flags present and one partly, and one trend-health test failed by 0.07%. What settles it: a Carlsberg close above DKK 981.8 on heavy volume settles it as a healthy advance; a close below DKK 934.2 and then the 20D MA settles it as a third failure at the ceiling.

Precedent: Carlsberg's February and July tops turned on selling that was not bought back; this one has been, so far. On 13-Feb, one session after the high, Carlsberg fell 1.8% on 1.64 times before a 20.0% fall; after the 30-Jul high the selling came on 18-Aug (1.62 times) before a 12.9% fall. Carlsberg has kept pace with a market that has risen since February, not led it.

What volume would confirm the advance: a close above DKK 981.8 on 1.5 times the 50D average or more, in the top third of the range. A third failure at the ceiling: a close below DKK 934.2, then below the rising 20D MA (DKK 925.7), on above-average volume.

3. RS

Carlsberg's Stock RS is exactly 70 at the Fri 30-Oct month-end reading, the line that two Sell Criteria read ("below 70"): a one-point fall makes half of each present. Each RS is a percentile rank from 0 to 99.

  • Trend: Carlsberg's Stock RS rose from 47 at the 30-Sep month-end to 73 at the Fri 23-Oct reading, fell to 59 on 29-Oct (the statement day) and rebounded to 70 on 30-Oct. Three months ago (31-Jul) it was 68; twelve months ago (31-Oct-2025) it was 22.
  • On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Stock RS was ~40 and now up to ~67 over L1M. How on earth can it move that much? Seems surprising. Check that it is correct". Confirmed as correct in the data file: Carlsberg's Stock RS was 48 at the Fri 2-Oct reading, 73 at the Fri 23-Oct reading and 70 at the 30-Oct month-end. Carlsberg's relative price rose 10.9% from 2-Oct to 30-Oct, as its SP rose 13.3% against 2.4% for the Stoxx Europe 600 over those 20 sessions. Stock RS is a percentile rank, so a relative move of that size shifts it about 25 points: 29 and 30-Oct showed about 2.5 points of Stock RS per 1% of relative price. The move was the group's as much as Carlsberg's: over the same month Heineken's Stock RS rose from 39 to 67 and Beverages' Sector RS from 51 at 30-Sep to 76.
  • Beverages' Sector RS is 76 (51 at 30-Sep, 85 at 23-Oct, 70 on 29-Oct); Consumer staples' Industry RS is 66 (40 at 30-Sep, 73 at 23-Oct, 59 on 29-Oct). Both are far above their lines (Sector RS 50, Industry RS 30).
  • The order of the turn: Carlsberg's Stock RS turned first (41 at 18-Sep to 51 at 25-Sep); Beverages and Consumer staples followed from 9-Oct and overtook it by 16-Oct (Sector RS 78, Industry RS 59 against Stock RS 63). Since then all three have moved together, down on 29-Oct and up on 30-Oct: Carlsberg is now carried by its group's rotation more than it leads it.
  • The Industry Digest's test of 30-Oct for a durable rotation rather than de-grossing (Consumer staples' Industry RS above 50 at 30-Oct and 6-Nov) is passed at its first reading.

Precedent: Carlsberg's Stock RS in the 70s has marked its last two tops. Carlsberg's Stock RS was 74 at the Fri 13-Feb reading, the day after the February high, then 42 by 20-Mar after a 20.0% fall. It was 68 at the 31-Jul month-end, the day after the July high, then 41 by 18-Sep after a 12.9% fall. Hypothesis: Carlsberg's Stock RS of 68 to 74 (74 at 13-Feb, 68 at 31-Jul, 73 at 23-Oct) is the top of its range rather than a new level, because its 12-month relative return is modest (relative price 8.8% below its February high). Refuted if Carlsberg's Stock RS holds 70 or more at the Fri 6-Nov and 13-Nov readings while the SP closes above DKK 981.8.

Peers' Stock RS: Heineken (67, from 39 a month ago) and AB InBev (66, from 57) sit within four points of Carlsberg, so Beverages' Sector RS and Carlsberg's Stock RS will move together into the 30-Nov month-end.

4. Consensus by named FY

Negative first: Carlsberg's consensus has fallen hard over two years, but not through 2026, and the third-quarter statement of 29-Oct lifted every line.

  • Two years: FY2027 consensus EPS fell 10.4% from 30-Sep-2024 (DKK 78.28 to 70.12); FY2028 EPS fell 14.8% from 31-Jan-2025 (DKK 89.78 to 76.47).
  • This year (31-Jan to 30-Oct-2026): FY2026 EPS +3.7% (DKK 62.78 to 65.09); FY2027 EPS +1.1% (DKK 69.37 to 70.12), though 1.4% below its March high; FY2028 EPS (1.8%).
  • The 2026 cut was in EBITDA, in August and September: FY2026 EBITDA fell 2.0% from the May month-end (DKK 19,824m) to 30-Sep (DKK 19,426m), around the 19-Aug first-half results and China's volume miss; FY2027 EBITDA fell 2.1% from its March high (DKK 20,959m) to 30-Sep (DKK 20,512m).
  • October, after the statement: from the 28-Oct daily reading to the 30-Oct month-end, FY2027 EPS rose 0.6%, FY2027 revenue 0.7% and FY2027 EBITDA 0.5%; over the month FY2028 revenue rose 0.9%.

The shape of the cuts: analysts have lowered the margin and the growth trajectory, not the near year.

  • Implied EPS growth from FY2026 to FY2027 is 7.7% now, against 10.5% at the January month-end; from FY2027 to FY2028 it is 9.1%, against 12.2%. Analysts raised FY2026 EPS and cut FY2027 and FY2028 EPS, which reads as earnings pulled forward and a slower compounder.
  • The implied FY2027 EBITDA margin (EBITDA over revenue) is 21.3%, against 21.7% at the January month-end, while FY2027 revenue is 0.6% higher. The EBITDA cuts are margin cuts.
  • Hypothesis for the margin cuts, from the Risks report of 2-Nov: soft drinks volume grew 8.5% in the third quarter but sells at about a 20% discount to beer (Rothschild & Co Redburn); China's operating margin fell 170 basis points in the quarter on an adverse channel mix (Rothschild & Co Redburn, for Chongqing Brewery); and net finance costs of about DKK 1.8bn a year after the Britvic acquisition. Test: the FY2027 EBITDA margin at the FY2026 results on 9-Feb-2027.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "MAJOR PROBLEM - Consensus EPS has been steadily falling through 2026. Same for EBITDA and Revenue. EBITDA VERY much so." And: "So what am I being naive about? What is driving the SS downgrades?"

  • Partly confirmed: consensus EPS has fallen over two years, and consensus EBITDA fell in 2026; but in 2026 consensus EPS for FY2026 and FY2027 and consensus revenue for FY2027 rose.
  • What drives the downgrades, from the sources read: Jefferies (Hold from Buy, PT DKK 950 from DKK 1,100, 13-Sep, "Growth Losing Its Fizz") forecasts FY2027 organic revenue growth of 2.3% against a 3.7% consensus, as the Kazakhstan Pepsi contract enters the organic base in 2027; China, about 20% of profits, is weakening (Jefferies, cited in the Risks report of 2-Nov); and the mix and margin points above.
  • The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 1-Oct: FY2027 EPS of DKK 70.12 sits inside its modal path (DKK 69 to 71), above its alarm (below DKK 66) and its invalidation (below DKK 63). No Invalidation test fires on consensus.

On 28-Oct you also wrote: "Why is EV/Sales valuation increasing - is that the net debt increasing?" No: Carlsberg's EV/Sales (data file) was 1.83 at the September month-end against 2.01 at February's and moves with the SP, while the memo records leverage falling from 3.9 times to 3.0 times net debt to EBITDA by the first half. October's 14.0% SP rise (DKK 851.8 at the 30-Sep close to DKK 971.0) will lift it; that reading is not yet in the data file.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Carlsberg's Net Upgrade Breadth turned after the 29-Oct statement, for the first time since March 2026; on 28-Oct every measure was negative.

  • EPS (FY2027): 0% at 30-Oct (11 raising, 11 cutting, of 26), from (32%) at 30-Sep and (40%) at 31-Aug, the most negative since November 2025.
  • EBITDA (FY2027): +32% at 30-Oct (12 raising, 5 cutting, of 22), from (29%) at 30-Sep: the highest month-end reading since at least October 2025.
  • Revenue and PT: the daily counts before the statement were (8%) for revenue (2 raising, 4 cutting, of 24) and (21%) for PT (4 raising, 9 cutting, of 24) on 28-Oct. After the statement the 30-Oct month-end reading (100-day counts, data file rebuilt 2-Nov) is +48% for revenue (13 raising, 1 cutting, of 25) and +16% for PT (10 raising, 6 cutting, of 25).

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "MAJOR PROBLEM - Net upgrade breadth for sales is negative - invalidation", "MAJOR PROBLEM - Net upgrade breadth for EPS is very negative" and "MAJOR PROBLEM - Very clear. The entire set of NUB is negative". All three were correct at 30-Sep and on 28-Oct; two days later EPS breadth is zero, EBITDA breadth positive, and revenue and PT breadth positive (+48% and +16% at the 30-Oct month-end). Negative revenue breadth is not an Invalidation test in the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo; with Stock RS below 50 it is half of a Sell Criterion (item 11 (the stock against each Sell Criterion), Carlsberg against each Sell Criterion). Test: whether revenue and PT breadth stay at or above zero at the 30-Nov month-end; in March 2026 positive breadth faded to negative within a month.

Ratings: Carlsberg's analysts upgrade after falls and drift away after rises (ratings that follow valuation). Buys fell from 17 to 12 between September 2024 and June 2025 as the SP rose, and rose to 19 by December 2025 after the September 2025 low.

  • At 30-Oct: 20 Buys, 8 other, 0 Sells, of 28 analysts, against 21, 5 and 1 of 27 at 31-Aug. The last Sell went, and two ratings were added to "other", between the 30-Sep and 30-Oct readings; which brokers moved, and on which day, is not in the sources read.
  • On 28-Oct you wrote: "The SS % of sells has gone to 0, which is positive" and "But the % of buys has also fallen - negative". Both confirmed: Buys are 71% of ratings, from 78% at 31-Aug.

Carlsberg's average PT, DKK 1,043.5 on 28-Oct, is 7.5% above the 30-Oct close, the narrowest gap at a month-end since May 2025. At the month-ends after the February and July 2026 highs (27-Feb and 31-Jul) the gaps were 10.4% and 10.7%, and the SP fell 15.5% and 7.6% by the next month-end (20.0% and 12.9% from the closing highs to the lows): the average PT has acted as a ceiling (hypothesis). Test: whether the average PT rises after 28-Oct, when the daily series stops, as the positive PT breadth at 30-Oct (+16%) implies.
