9. Peers

China exposure split the brewers on the results days, and Carlsberg sits between the winner (Heineken) and the loser (AB InBev). The peer table and chart on the page carry the figures; this item draws the links.

The peers' results days (the day-by-day moves are in item 1 (SP and MAs), SP and MAs):

  • Heineken, 28-Oct: organic revenue +4.6%, Asia Pacific volume +17.3%, China double-digit growth, 2026 guidance of 2% to 6% organic operating profit growth reiterated (GlobeNewswire, 28-Oct); Heineken rose 1.6% that day and is at its 52W closing high.
  • AB InBev, 29-Oct: Chinese beer volumes down 11% (Sector Digest, 30-Oct); AB InBev fell 3.2% and is 5.1% below its 22-Oct high. The Sector Digest reads the 29-Oct split as China exposure: brewers that kept guidance without raising it were sold, and Heineken held.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Heineken reported strong growth in China. That is negative for Carlsberg, given the reported weakness in China". Partly confirmed. Carlsberg's China volume fell about 10% in the third quarter (Morgan Stanley; Rothschild & Co Redburn), while Heineken's grew by double digits, so Heineken gained share. Against the reading: AB InBev (down 11%), Chongqing Brewery (down 9.6%), Tsingtao (down 9%) and Budweiser APAC (down 16.8%) all fell (Sector Digest, 30-Oct). The fall is market-wide and Heineken is the exception, which fits the destocking and weather explanation more than a Carlsberg-specific loss.

On 28-Oct you wrote: "Investigate China weakness ASAP - what is going on, and what are the implications for the Carlsberg investment case?" From the sources read:

  • What is going on: wholesalers destocking after weak summer sell-through and heavy rain, weak nightlife and premium on-trade spending, and weak consumer sentiment (Morgan Stanley; Rothschild & Co Redburn; Risks report, 2-Nov). Morgan Stanley had reported management expecting the third quarter to stay subdued in China.
  • The implication: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 1-Oct sets an alarm at Chinese organic volume in the second half of 2026 down more than 3%; Carlsberg's third quarter, about (10%), is beyond it unless the fourth quarter recovers strongly. Its invalidation is 2027 down again despite easy comparisons, so no Invalidation test fires, but the memo's FY2027 path needs re-testing by the Investment Analyst.
  • On 28-Oct you also wrote: "Isn't this what Jefferies have been saying? China weakness for Carlsberg, down to sell/hold?" Partly: Jefferies cut to Hold, not Sell, on 13-Sep, mainly on FY2027 organic revenue growth (2.3% against a 3.7% consensus) as the Kazakhstan Pepsi contract enters the base, with China as the second reason (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth), Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth).

Richard's four peer checks for Carlsberg's cohort:

  • Sell-side estimates: AB InBev's three-month EPS and revenue revisions are +3% each; Heineken's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth is +21%; Carlsberg's three-month EPS revision is (1%) before 30-Oct's rises. Carlsberg is still the cohort's weakest estimate trend.
  • SP technicals: Heineken at its 52W high; Carlsberg 1.1% below its 52W closing high; AB InBev 5.1% below; Royal Unibrew 29.3% below and below its absolute 200D MA. Heineken, then Carlsberg, lead the cohort.
  • Newsflow, and company guidance and communications (your third and fourth checks): the newsflow is the results days above, Heineken's China growth against AB InBev's and Carlsberg's China falls; Heineken, AB InBev and Carlsberg all reiterated guidance, and none raised it.

What the peers imply for Carlsberg: Beverages' Sector RS (76) now rests on the brewers and bottlers, with spirits weak (Campari 7.0% down in the week, Pernod Ricard's Stock RS 33). A spirits-led fall would weigh on Beverages' Sector RS ahead of the 30-Nov reading even if Carlsberg holds (item 11 (the stock against each Sell Criterion), Carlsberg against each Sell Criterion).

What the rules say for Carlsberg now, and what decides the next step

  • The finding that matters most: the sell signal is gone. At the Fri 30-Oct month-end Carlsberg's Stock RS was 70, so the test "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50", which gave a sell signal at 30-Sep, gives none. Carlsberg is no longer awaiting a replacement under your one-off in/out link.
  • The second finding: estimates and breadth turned up on the statement despite a China miss that crossed the memo's alarm (items 4, Consensus by named FY, to 9, Peers).
  • The third finding: the DKK 981.8 ceiling decides the chart. 30-Oct's heavy buying failed at DKK 982.0, and relative price stays 8.8% below its February high (items 1, SP and MAs, and 2, Volume, and relative price against its 52W high).
  • Each tranche today: hold (tranche 1, 1.90%; tranche 2, 0.86%; tranche 3, 1.22%). Carlsberg is a Middle Innings Core Investment, sold by the six Sell Criteria alone, and none gives a sell signal.
  • For your ruling: the Position Management System reads the sell signals of the relative 200D MA test (each Friday from 11-Sep to 16-Oct) and of the Net Upgrade Breadth with Stock RS test (30-Sep) as ended by the 23-Oct and 30-Oct readings, taking Carlsberg off the order of sale; whether the pending sale under the one-off in/out link instead stands until a replacement is bought is your ruling.

The nearest sell tests, and how they interact:

  • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (read Mon 30-Nov): Carlsberg's Stock RS is at the line, so a one-point fall makes that half present. Beverages' Sector RS of 76 must fall 27 points; it fell 9 over 1 week, rose 26 over 4 weeks and 8 over 13 weeks, and it fell 29 points in two sessions in August (68 on 31-Jul to 39 on 4-Aug) and 30 points in three (38 on 5-Aug).
  • Net Upgrade Breadth below zero with Stock RS below 50 (read Mon 30-Nov): Carlsberg's Stock RS must fall 21 points, roughly an 8% to 9% relative fall at the sensitivity of 29 and 30-Oct (about 2.5 points of Stock RS per 1% of relative price; a guide only, as Stock RS is a rank), and breadth must turn negative again. Stock RS was 47 a month ago.
  • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high (read Mon 30-Nov): 8.8% below; at a flat market the SP would have to fall 17.7% to DKK 799.0.
  • How they interact: one Carlsberg-specific fall of about 10% would make the Stock RS half present in three tests at once (the two above and the 30% tranche fall), but only a fall across Beverages completes the Sector RS test, and only Net Upgrade Breadth turning negative completes the test on Net Upgrade Breadth. Stock RS above 70 and a close above DKK 981.8 move all three away.
  • The record: after Carlsberg's Stock RS of 74 (13-Feb) and 68 (31-Jul), Stock RS fell to the low 40s within five to seven weeks each time; after the second fall the test of Net Upgrade Breadth with Stock RS gave a sell signal at 30-Sep, and Carlsberg's SP is now 14.0% above its 30-Sep close.

The next reading day and the add:

Your positive-retest trigger is absent; the broadening of the case is partly present (item 12 (adding capital), Adding capital to Carlsberg).

Fevertree

Early Innings Probing Bet (from 2-Nov-2026) · 2.0% of capital in 1 tranche (tranche 1, 2.0%, bought 8-Jun-2026) · readings as at Fri 30-Oct-2026

1. SP and MAs

Fevertree's SP has pulled back 2.8% in six sessions from its 22-Oct high (941.0p to 914.5p on 30-Oct), on light volume, after a 13.3% October advance that was mostly its industry's. Over 12 months Fevertree is up 9.7% (from 833.82p on 31-Oct-2025), inside the same 738.45p to 969.79p range it has traded in all year; over 3 months Fevertree is up 7.9%, against Consumer staples up 3.5% and the Stoxx Europe 600 down 1.5%.

Developments: AlphaSense unreachable; written from the earnings review and the data file.

Fevertree's legs since its first-half results:

  • 10-Sep to 6-Oct, a fall, a rebound and a flat spell, all Fevertree's own: the SP fell 6.0% on results day (10-Sep) to 768.45p, rose 13.6% to 872.94p by 22-Sep, then fell 5.0% and stayed flat at about 830p to 6-Oct. The results (adjusted EBITDA margin 10.9%, guidance unchanged; earnings review, 18-Sep), the rebound on the chair's purchase (11-Sep) and Kepler Cheuvreux's upgrade (15-Sep) were Fevertree's own. The flat spell was a light-volume test of a flat 50D MA, and it came while Consumer staples fell.
  • 6 to 22-Oct, +13.3% from 830.5p to 941.0p (your 28-Oct note: "+10 or 12%"): mostly the industry's. Consumer staples rose 7.9% and Beverages' Sector RS went from 51 (30-Sep) to 85 (23-Oct). Fevertree's own part was a large seller absorbed: the C&J Rolls Family Discretionary Trust crossed below 4% on 13-Oct (Fevertree, holding notification, 16-Oct), and a non-executive director, David Lapp, bought 23,800 shares at 935.5p on 20-Oct (director dealing notification, 22-Oct).
  • 22 to 30-Oct, (2.8%) to 914.5p: Fevertree lagged both Consumer staples ((0.1%) over the week) and the Stoxx Europe 600 (+1.7%). On 29-Oct, the brewers' results day, Fevertree traded down 3.1% intraday to 890.5p and closed down 0.8%, alongside AB InBev (down 3.2%) and Campari (down 6.6%) (Sector Digest, 30-Oct). On 30-Oct the alcohol makers rebounded (Pernod Ricard +3.6%, Diageo +3.3%, Remy +2.7%) while Fevertree rose 0.4%, closing in the bottom 22% of its range. This leg is Fevertree's own: it fell with the alcohol makers on their bad day and did not rise with them on their good one. The cause is not in the sources read.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "NEGATIVE - 150D MA below the 200D MA, which is pretty horizontal". Confirmed: the 150D (828.3p) is 1.6% below an absolute 200D (842.0p) that has moved between 829.8p and 872.6p for 12 months, while the 20D, 50D and 100D are rising. Fevertree regained its 20D and absolute 200D on 7-Oct (5.33x the 50D average volume) and its 50D on 8-Oct, and has closed above all of them since. At a flat 914.5p the 150D crosses above the absolute 200D in about 22 sessions (about 1-Dec), and only two sessions sooner at 970p: the cross is a matter of time, not of price.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Fevertree against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: partly: +0.8% over 20 sessions, but only 1.6% above its level of 12 months ago and 1.6% below that of 6 months ago.
  • The 150D above the absolute 200D: fails, 1.6% below; the cross is dated in the paragraph above.
  • The 50D above the 150D and the absolute 200D: passes, by 3.7% and 2.0%.
  • The SP above each MA: passes: 1.9% above the 20D, 6.5% above the 50D, 8.6% above the absolute 200D.
  • Near its 52W high: partly. The close is 5.7% below the 969.79p closing high of 13-Feb.
  • Away from its 52W low: passes, 23.8% above 738.45p (4-Jun).
  • Red flag, the largest one-day fall since the advance began: absent: (1.1%) at most in the pullback, against (2.4%) on 18-Sep.
  • Red flag, the largest one-week fall: partly. The five sessions to 29-Oct fell 3.2%, a hair short of the (3.3%) in the five sessions to 29-Sep.
  • Red flag, wider swings: absent: the average daily range narrowed to 2.7% over the last 10 sessions from 4.4% in the 10 before.
  • Red flag, lower lows: absent. The 29-Oct intraday low (890.5p) held above the 13-Oct close (868.5p).
  • Red flag, more down days than up days: present: six down and three up of the last 10 sessions.
  • Red flag, a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: absent. No session in the pullback reached 1.25x the 50D average volume.
  • Red flag, the SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly. On 29-Oct the SP touched the rising 20D (893.05p) and closed 2.0% above it, but the 30-Oct follow-through was weak.

Verdict: a light-volume pullback with contracting swings inside a 12-month base that is improving but not yet an established uptrend (a Stage 2 advance): more healthy retest than broken trend, with the steady run of down days the one live warning. What settles it: a close below the 20D (897.1p) on 1.25x the 50D average volume or more would make it a deeper test of the 50D (859.0p); an up close above 927p (the 30-Oct high) on above-average volume would make the 20D touch a bounce.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: volume against its 50D average from the volume script, which leaves out two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug, 18-Sep); the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure is in brackets where it differs.

Fevertree's pullback since 22-Oct has come on below-average volume with no distribution day: sellers are not pressing. The six sessions from 23 to 30-Oct traded 0.76x to 1.12x the 50D average, averaging 0.88x. The heaviest session, 28-Oct (down 0.6% on 1.12x, 536,295 shares, £4.9m), closed at the bottom 8% of its range but stayed under the 1.25x that makes a distribution day. The dashboard's up/down volume ratio (up-day against down-day volume) is 0.88 over 5 sessions and 1.08 over 20, and its volume trend (the 20-day against the 50-day average volume) is 0.83: activity is fading on the way down.

Fevertree's heavy-volume days of the last 25 sessions all fall from 6 to 15-Oct, and they read as a large seller met by buyers. The three largest were 7-Oct (+1.1% on 5.33x (5.25x), 1.72M shares, £14.5m), 9-Oct (+1.6% on 6.48x, 2.39M shares, £20.6m, but closing at the bottom 12% of its range after trading to 888p) and 13-Oct ((1.8%) on 6.96x, 2.93M shares, £25.4m, closing at the bottom 10%: the day the Rolls trust crossed below 4%). The 14 and 15-Oct up days on 1.82x and 2.32x (£6.9m and £9.3m), the second closing at the top 9% of its range, showed the supply absorbed. Distribution has faded: the dashboard counts one distribution day in 25 sessions, against three on 2-Oct; the script counts two distribution days (6 and 13-Oct) against four accumulation days, and no distribution day since 13-Oct.

The advance from the 10-Sep low has an up/down volume ratio (average up-day against average down-day volume) of 1.18, with 5 heavy up days against 2 heavy down; on Fevertree that has not predicted the ending: the ratio over each advance of the last 12 months ran from 0.88 to 1.39, and each advance gave way to a fall of 9.6% to 22.7%.

Precedent: Fevertree's declines have started with a sharp day, and its lows have ended with a heavy one. The declines from the 22-Oct-2025, 20-Apr, 17-Jun and 7-Aug highs each began within two sessions with a 2.1% to 3.0% fall closing in the lower third of the range; this pullback has had no fall larger than 1.1%. Four of the last five lows were followed within one to four sessions by an up day on 1.9x to 2.5x the 50D average volume (24-Mar, 9-Jun, 14-Jul, 11-Sep).

Volume at the MAs: on 29-Oct Fevertree touched its rising 20D (low 890.5p against 893.05p) on 0.76x and closed at 60% of the day's range: a held test, not yet a bounce. It last met tranche 1's MA, the 50D, from 1 to 7-Oct, closing below it on five sessions, the first three on 0.63x to 0.81x, until the 7-Oct accumulation day.

Fevertree's relative price has not confirmed the SP's October high. Relative price is 13.1% below its 52W high (13-Feb), against 5.7% for the SP, and was 9.5% below at the 22-Oct SP high. Relative price has stayed above its relative 200D MA since 12-Oct (4.1% above); its February cross above that MA failed within three weeks.

Confirms a retest: an up day on 1.5x the 50D average volume or more, closing in the top third of its range, from at or above the 20D (897.1p). Confirms a deeper turn: a close below the 20D on 1.25x or more, or a 2% to 3% fall closing near the low.

3. RS

All three of Fevertree's RS readings rose through October, dipped together after the 23-Oct reading and bounced together at the 30-Oct month-end: the latest moves are the group's, not Fevertree's alone.

  • Fevertree's Stock RS is 61 at the 30-Oct month-end: 51 at the 30-Sep month-end, 34 at the 31-Jul month-end, 33 at the 31-Oct-2025 month-end; it peaked at 68 at the 23-Oct reading and read 57 on 29-Oct. It is 11 points above the line of 50 and 9 below the line of 70.
  • Beverages' Sector RS is 76 (51 at 30-Sep, 68 at 31-Jul, 23 a year ago), down from 85 at 23-Oct and up from 70 on 29-Oct: 26 points above its line of 50.
  • Consumer staples' Industry RS is 66 (40 at 30-Sep, 46 at 31-Jul, 20 a year ago), down from 73 at 23-Oct and up from 59 on 29-Oct: 36 points above its line of 30.

The order of the turn: Fevertree first, then the sector, then the industry. Fevertree's Stock RS rose from 19 (11-Sep reading) to 41 (18-Sep), on its own rebound from the results low; Sector RS turned from 25-Sep and Industry RS from 9-Oct. The rotation into Consumer staples then carried a recovery already under way. But the rotation lifted the larger Beverages names more than Fevertree (item 9, Peers): Fevertree's Stock RS rose 10 points over the month, against 28 for Heineken, 25 for Coca-Cola Europacific Partners and 23 for Carlsberg.

The Industry Digest's test of 30-Oct (Industry RS above 50 on 30-Oct and 6-Nov means a rotation, not de-grossing) is half passed: Consumer staples' Industry RS was 66 on 30-Oct, and the 6-Nov reading is still to come.

Precedent: the last time Fevertree's Stock RS stood in the 60s (62 to 65, 13 to 27-Feb, at the SP's 52W high), it fell to 29 by 20-Mar as the SP fell 22.7%. Test: Stock RS above 60 on 6-Nov with Sector RS above 70 supports the rotation holding; Stock RS falling while Sector RS holds would mean money leaving Fevertree for its larger peers.

4. Consensus by named FY

Fevertree's SP rose 9.8% over the month to 30-Oct while every consensus line held or edged down: estimates did not lead October's rise, and the operating lines are still being cut.

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26): EPS (0.4%) over 1 month and (2.0%) over 6; revenue (0.5%) and EBITDA (0.7%) over 3 months.
  • FY2027: EPS (0.5%) over 1 month, flat over 3, +1.8% over 12; revenue (1.2%) over 3 months and (3.2%) over 12; EBITDA (1.6%) and (2.3%).
  • FY2028: EPS (1.0%), revenue (2.6%) and EBITDA (4.0%) over 3 months; the cut came at the 31-Aug month-end, and the 10-Sep results did not reverse it.

The later years are being cut faster than the nearer ones: analysts are trimming the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward. Over 3 months FY2028 EBITDA fell 4.0% against 1.6% for FY2027 and 0.7% for FY2026. The implied path is still steep: consensus FY2028 EPS is 26.9% above FY2027 on revenue 7.3% higher, with the implied EBITDA margin climbing from 12.4% (FY2026) to 14.7% (FY2027) and 16.7% (FY2028). That margin path rests on US onshoring, which has slipped to cans only in 2027: Fevertree expects "only can production to be localized" in 2027, about half of US volume (Deutsche Bank, 6-Oct, in the Sector Digest), so glass production now falls after 2027. If that is the company's guidance, it meets the Alarm in the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo ("glass guided past 2027") and reaches its Invalidation test ("full onshoring guided past Q2 2027"); the sources read give Deutsche Bank's summary, not the company's own words.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Why are EPS estimates creeping up but revenue isn't - FX or more fundamental?" The evidence points to the share count, not FX. Over 12 months FY2027 EPS rose 1.8% while FY2027 revenue fell 3.2% and EBITDA 2.3%; that gap is what a smaller share count produces. Jefferies raised its FY2026 EPS 3% "purely on buyback accretion" at the results, and Fevertree is returning £60m by buyback in 2026 after £100m in 2025 (earnings review, 18-Sep). Whether currency moved the sterling revenue estimates is not in the sources read. And EPS is no longer creeping up: FY2027 EPS is flat over 3 months and (0.5%) over 1, and FY2027 EPS Net Upgrade Breadth fell from 50% at the July month-end to 0% at October (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)).

The FY2026 line carries a test the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo sets. Consensus FY2026 EBITDA of £49.8m on £400.6m of revenue is a 12.4% margin, which needs a second-half margin of about 13.7% after the first half's 10.9%; the memo's Alarm is a FY2026 margin below 12%. The memo's main test, the United States off-trade scanner growth (16% in July and August, Alarm below 10% for a quarter), has no new print in the sources read: the AlphaSense research filed on 1-Nov (US scanner sales trends) finds no Fevertree figure after August. In Europe, Fevertree's off-trade sales grew 6.6% in the four weeks to 4-Oct against 11.2% over 12 weeks and 7.8% for European soft drinks (Morgan Stanley, 30-Oct, as summarised in the UK and Europe research filed on 1-Nov): if that summary is right, a slowdown to growth below the category's. Test: the next US scanner reading, and the FY2027 revenue and EBITDA lines at the 30-Nov month-end.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Fevertree has the most Buys in two years, but the analysts' numbers are not following their ratings: PT, revenue and EPS breadth are flat to negative.

  • Ratings: 7 Buys, 5 other ratings and 3 Sells at the September and October month-ends, from 5 Buys, 7 other and 3 Sells at August and 2, 12 and 2 in mid-2025; 15 analysts cover the EPS line.
  • Ratings follow valuation, not momentum, on Fevertree: Kepler Cheuvreux upgraded on 15-Sep, three sessions after the results low; the fifth Buy came at the June month-end after an 18% fall from February to May. If that holds, new Buys are unlikely near the average PT.

The average PT has drifted down 2.1% in three months, from 967.3p (31-Jul) to 947.3p (every reading from 7 to 28-Oct), while the SP rose; the SP now sits 3.5% below it. In the last two cases where the SP reached the average PT at a month-end (July 2025, February 2026), Fevertree fell 14.9% and 20.0% by the next month-end; in May and June 2025 it kept rising.

Net Upgrade Breadth (FY2027; raised less cut, over analysts with an estimate), latest reading:

  • EPS: 0% at 30-Oct (6 raised, 6 cut, of 15), from 50% at 31-Jul, 13% at 31-Aug and 7% at 30-Sep: positive since January, now gone to zero.
  • EBITDA: +36% (7 raised, 2 cut, of 14), from (36%) at 31-Aug and +21% at 30-Sep: the one line turning up, though the EBITDA consensus still fell 0.3% over the month, so the raises are small.
  • Revenue: (9%) on 28-Oct (2 raised, 3 cut, of 11), negative at every month-end since August 2025 except July 2026.
  • PT: (13%) on 28-Oct (3 raised, 5 cut, of 15), negative at every month-end since March except July.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Q - Declining PTs are a real negative IMHO. Why is that? Same for PT breadth. Why?" The brokers cutting are not named in the sources read, so their stated logic cannot be given; the data file counts five PT cuts against three raises over 100 days. What the sources do show: the cuts came before and around the results (the average PT fell 1.9% in August), the PTs named at the results were unchanged or higher (Jefferies Buy 1,100p held; Kepler Cheuvreux 910p from 900p, 15-Sep; Deutsche Bank Hold 900p, 6-Oct), and the operating estimates were cut in the same months (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). Hypothesis: the PT cuts follow the FY2028 revenue and EBITDA cuts and the slower onshoring, valued at unchanged multiples. Test: a named PT change in the next four weeks, with its stated reason.

The divergence that matters: more analysts rate Fevertree a Buy, but more cut its revenue and PT than raise them (Sell-side Momentum graded D, 28-Oct). Revenue Net Upgrade Breadth below zero also fails the sales breadth test for the Pool of Stocks Eligible for Middle Innings Portfolio Selection (item 12 (adding capital)).

9. Peers

The October rotation into Beverages favoured the larger names over Fevertree, as you suspected. On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "The sector and industry RS turning upwards could be positive - or could lead to rotation into the "big boys" stocks like Diageo out of Fevertree; TBD - unclear IMHO". Over the month to 30-Oct Fevertree's Stock RS rose 10 points (51 to 61) and its SP 9.8%, in the bottom third of the 11 Beverages stocks on both. Heineken (+28 points), Coca-Cola Europacific Partners (+25), Carlsberg (+23), Coca-Cola HBC (+22) and Remy (+20) gained far more; Diageo (+11) about the same; only AB InBev (+9) and Campari (down 4) less. The evidence leans to your second reading: the rotation is lifting Fevertree, but less than the large caps, so Fevertree is not where the rotation's money is going.

Your four peer checks:

  • Sell-side estimates: On 28-Oct you wrote that the APM "notes that upgrade breadth is stronger at CCH, Remy, Heineken, Campari; AG Barr, Carlsberg, AB Inbev and Diageo are negative - BAD for Fevertree vs. other names". Confirmed on the 30-Oct data: revenue Net Upgrade Breadth is +22% at Coca-Cola HBC and Remy, +21% at Heineken and +20% at Campari, against (16%) at Diageo, (13%) at AB InBev, (10%) at AG Barr, (9%) at Fevertree and (8%) at Carlsberg. Over 3 months the dashboard's EPS revision, which does not name the FY it reads, is +5% for Diageo and (1%) for Fevertree; on Fevertree's own FY2027 line, EPS is flat over 3 months (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).
  • SP technicals: every Beverages stock except AG Barr (0.3% below) closes above its 50D MA; Fevertree's 6.5% above it is mid-pack, behind Remy (11.7%), Heineken and Carlsberg (9.2% each). Over the last week Fevertree (2.5%) fell with the alcohol makers (AB InBev (4.6%), Campari (7.0%), Remy (2.9%), Diageo (1.9%)) while its own cohort rose (Coca-Cola HBC +3.2%, Coca-Cola Europacific Partners +2.1%, AG Barr +0.5%).
  • Newsflow: on 29-Oct the market sold the brewers that kept guidance without raising it, split along China exposure (Sector Digest, 30-Oct); Fevertree, with no China exposure, traded down 3.1% intraday with them.
  • Company guidance and communications: Coca-Cola HBC has raised its guidance (Sector Digest, 15-Oct) and its FY revenue estimate is up 27% over 3 months (hypothesis: the African acquisition being consolidated); the brewers reiterated rather than raised on 29-Oct. Fevertree's guidance is unchanged since 10-Sep, and its next scheduled communication is the full-year results, projected for 30-Mar-2027.

Who Fevertree trades with now: the large Beverages names, not AG Barr. Over the last 20 sessions Fevertree's daily moves correlate 0.59 with Campari, 0.54 with Heineken and 0.40 to 0.44 with AB InBev, Diageo and the two Coca-Cola bottlers, but 0.14 with AG Barr; the dashboard's longer measure names Carlsberg, AB InBev, Pernod Ricard and Remy (0.33 to 0.38, moderate). On 30-Oct, though, Fevertree did not follow them up.

What the peers imply for Fevertree: its next move depends on the large alcohol makers, and it gains least when they rise. A fade of the Beverages rotation would also keep Fevertree out of the Pool (item 12 (adding capital)).

What the rules say for Fevertree now, and what decides the next step

  • Finding 1, the pullback: orderly (light volume, no distribution day, the rising 20D held), but Fevertree lagged the 30-Oct rebound and the rotation favours larger peers (items 1, 2 and 9).

The data file, rebuilt at 14:31 UK on 2-Nov under your 2-Nov rules, agrees: it reads Fevertree as an Early Innings Probing Bet, tranche 1 clear, with no sale due.
  • Tranche 1 (2.0%) under the 10-day stop-loss rule: clear, with no test running. The MA is the 50D at 859.0p and the stop-loss the 50D less 6% at 807.5p; the 30-Oct close of 914.5p is 6.5% above the MA and 13.3% above the stop-loss. Nothing sells at the Tuesday 3-Nov session.

Fevertree's falling relative 200D MA therefore does not block an add: the rules allow one, and the data file rebuilt at 14:31 UK on 2-Nov shows the add allowed, at 3%.
  • The size of an add: a Probing Bet tranche is sized to lose 0.5% of capital at its stop-loss, capped at 3%. At 914.5p the stop-loss is 11.7% below, giving 4.3%, so the 3% cap binds; at a 50D retest it is also 3%. Your note names 1-2%. Room: 4.0% below the 6% limit; Consumer staples would go from 9.3% to 12.3% of capital, inside the 30% cap.
  • What would change this reading: a US scanner print below 10% (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's Alarm) or two closes below the 50D on ordinary volume; on the other side, a bounce from the 20D on 1.5x volume, or a close above 969.79p, the 52W high.

Richard decides.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "RS positive for stock and sector and industry". At the 30-Oct readings that holds for Sdiptech's Stock RS and, falling, for its Sector RS; it does not hold for its Industry RS.

  • Sector RS (Diversified industrial groups): 72, from 92 on 16-Oct (its 13-month high), 80 on 23-Oct and 75 on 29-Oct: 20 points in two weeks. It is above the line of 50, and above the 57 of three months ago and the 56 of a year ago.
  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 46 on Fri 30-Oct, the same as on 23-Oct, from 79 on 2 and 9-Oct and 73 at the 30-Sep month-end. Sdiptech's Industry RS is 16 points above the line of 30; in a ranking of 16 industries each place is worth about 6.7 points, so Sdiptech's Industry RS readings step 46, 40, 33 and 26, and a fall of three places is the first to read below 30.

The order of the turn is industry first, sector second, stock not yet: a rotation out of industrials pressing on Sdiptech from above. Industry RS turned after 9-Oct, as defensives outperformed (your 28-Oct market note: "Relative rotation into defensives (and energy)"); Sector RS turned after 16-Oct, as large members fell (Lifco, thyssenkrupp, Hill & Smith; item 9 (Peers)). Sdiptech's Stock RS dipped to 80 on 29-Oct, after the 26-Oct fall, and recovered to 84 by the month-end. Hypothesis: Sdiptech's own earnings momentum is holding its Stock RS up against its groups. Evidence for: its FY2027 revenue consensus rose 4.0% in October (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). Against: a 12-month composite moves slowly, so it can lag a stock that has gone sideways for a month.

Precedent: each RS has stood this low before and recovered (the record is under "What the rules say for Sdiptech now", in The record paragraph). Stock RS fell to 68 on 24-Jul, the low before Sdiptech's 16.6% rise to 14-Oct.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts raised Sdiptech's FY2027 revenue about twice as fast as its EBITDA in October, and its EBITDA about twice as fast as its EPS: the third-quarter report added sales that do not fully reach earnings.

  • FY2027 (year to Dec-2027), in SEK, over the month to 30-Oct: revenue up 4.0% to 5,584m, EBITDA up 2.1% to 1,407m, EPS up 0.9% to 17.58. Over three months: revenue up 5.6%, EBITDA up 3.3%, EPS up 6.6%.
  • FY2028: revenue up 4.3% to 5,956m, EBITDA up 2.2% to 1,506m, EPS up 1.8% to 19.61 over the month.
  • FY2026: revenue up 2.4% to 5,184m and EBITDA up 2.4% to 1,205m over the month. FY2026 EPS reads 13.31, (6.9%) on the month from 14.30 at 30-Sep. The 30-Sep reading sat 8% above the 13.23 of both the July and August month-ends; against 31-Jul, FY2026 EPS is up 0.6%. The one-off SEK 13m bond-redemption charge cut FY2026 adjusted EPS by about 2% (earnings review).

Sdiptech's implied EBITDA margin is falling for every year. The implied FY2027 EBITDA margin is 25.2% at 30-Oct, from 25.7% at the 30-Sep and August month-ends; FY2028 is 25.3%, from 25.8% at 30-Sep. This fits the report: Supply Chain & Transportation, the largest and lowest-margin segment, grew sales 27% at an 18.5% margin (21.0% a year earlier) on raw-material costs at Mecno and low-margin chassis deliveries at Hilltip, and the chief executive said the pressure continues through the fourth quarter (earnings call, 23-Oct). The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's implications note (23-Oct) puts the bear reading: "the growth is dilutive by construction".

Analysts are raising the trajectory at the EPS line, slightly. The implied EPS growth from FY2027 to FY2028 is 11.5%, from 10.5% at 30-Sep and 10.8% at 31-Jul; for revenue it is 6.7% (6.4% at 30-Sep) and for EBITDA 7.0% (7.0%). The EPS step comes from below the EBITDA line: the refinancing cut future interest cost and lifted future adjusted EPS by about 5% (earnings review).

Sdiptech's SP led the estimates, and the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's tests give a mixed reading. The SP rose 12.4% before the report and has gone sideways since. The memo's FY2027 test (adjusted EPS of SEK 16.5 or more with group return on capital employed of 14% or more) passes on EPS at 17.58 and is short on return on capital employed, 13.6% in the third quarter. The memo's Water & Bioeconomy margin alarm (below 21.8% at the third quarter) was triggered at 20.3%, and its invalidation clock, "still below 22% two quarters on", runs to the fourth-quarter report on 10-Feb-2027 (implications note, 23-Oct). Leverage (2.90 to 2.95 times against a 3.0 times alarm) is the other reading that report settles.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

For the first time in the 24 months of the data file, an analyst covering Sdiptech does not rate it a Buy. The rating count moved from 5 Buys, 0 other, 0 Sells at the 30-Sep month-end to 5, 1 and 0 at 30-Oct, and the number of analysts with estimates rose from six to seven for revenue and EBITDA and from five to six for EPS. The pattern fits a new analyst starting coverage below Buy; no source read names the broker or its reasoning. Until then every covering analyst had rated Sdiptech a Buy through its 43% fall to January 2026 and its rise since.

Sdiptech's average PT keeps rising after its SP stalls; the gap to the SP is 22.1%. The average PT rose 2.6% to 312.7 on 25 and 26-Oct, after the report, and is up 21% from 258.4 at the January 2026 month-end. DNB Carnegie's Carl Korsheden trimmed his PT to SEK 340 from 342 on 26-Oct, "citing a reduction in peer group multiples", reiterated Buy, cut FY2026 adjusted EPS by 1%, left FY2027 unchanged at SEK 19.5 and raised revenue forecasts by 2% (earnings review). The brokers that lifted the average are not named in the sources read.

The 27-Oct note's hypothesis, that Sdiptech's average PT acts as a ceiling about 12% to 16% above its SP, has held a third time in 2026: Sdiptech came within about 12.5% of its average PT on 14-Oct, fell 6.7%, and analysts then raised the PT. Test: a Sdiptech close above about 272, the SP at which the average PT of 312.7 stands 15% above it (312.7 divided by 1.15), with no further PT rise would start to break the pattern; a close above about 279 would bring the gap below 12%, narrower than any time in the pattern.

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure and thinner than at its peak.

  • PT: +43% at the 30-Oct month-end (3 of 7, net), from +50% at 30-Sep, +83% at the July and August month-ends and +100% in May and June. No analyst cut a PT in the 100-day count; the thinning is fewer raising.
  • Revenue and FY2027 EBITDA: +71% each at 30-Oct (5 of 7, none cutting), from +67% at 30-Sep; revenue was +100% in July and August.
  • FY2027 EPS: +33% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 6), from +80% at 30-Sep (4 of 5, none cutting). This is the one measure where an analyst is cutting. DNB Carnegie cut FY2026, not FY2027, so the broker cutting FY2027 EPS is not named in the sources read; the likely logic is the report's margin and interest items (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).
  • The divergence: breadth is widest for revenue and EBITDA and narrowest for EPS and PT, the same order as item 4 (Consensus by named FY): analysts agree on more sales, and fewer of them carry it to earnings or price.

Where analysts are cutting, and their stated logic: DNB Carnegie trimmed its PT on peer multiples, not on Sdiptech's numbers; the Sector Digest (30-Oct) holds the hypothesis that "a lower multiple for Swedish serial acquirers appears to cap PTs". Test: further PT trims citing peers.

Precedent: Sdiptech's SP turned with breadth in early 2026: PT breadth went from (40%) at the January month-end to +83% at the April month-end, and the SP rose 45.6% from its 9-Feb low (167.0) to 17-Apr (243.2). Test: PT breadth back above +50% at the November month-end.

9. Peers

Sdiptech is the one leader of its cohort (diversified serial acquirers in a reset) still holding up; Indus Holding, the other leader, is breaking down. Indus Holding fell 11.6% over the month to 30-Oct, closed 7.1% below its 50D MA and 14.7% below its 52W high, and fell 2.1% and 3.2% on 29 and 30-Oct; its Stock RS fell from 97 a month ago to 84. Its falls came on light volume (0.6 to 0.8 times average): no buyers rather than heavy selling. The 27-Oct note's test was whether Indus Holding falls towards its absolute 200D MA on days Sdiptech also falls, which would show the cohort's leaders being sold together. Both fell on 30-Oct (Indus Holding 3.2%, Sdiptech 0.4%), and Indus Holding's distance above its absolute 200D MA has narrowed from 20.9% on 27-Oct to 13.9%: the test is partly met. Indus Holding reports on 12-Nov. Implication for Sdiptech: a second cohort leader breaking would leave Sdiptech exposed to a cohort de-rating that its own numbers have not earned.

Your four peer checks, for the cohort and the Swedish acquirers around it:

  • Sell-side estimates: Sdiptech leads its cohort on revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (+67% on the peer table's basis) and ties Indus Holding on three-month revenue revisions (+4% each); on three-month EPS revisions Sdiptech (+7%) is second to Indus Holding (+10%), whose revenue breadth is 0%; Storskogen's EPS revision is (1%) with revenue breadth +60%; Volati's revenue breadth is (100%). DNB Carnegie cut Lifco's FY2027 and FY2028 EPS by 5% to 6% on customer losses (earnings review).
  • SP technicals: Sdiptech (0.9% above its 50D MA) and Storskogen (0.6% above) are both resting on the MA; Indus Holding (7.1% below) and Volati (3.9% below) are under it; Lifco is 11.1% below its 50D MA and 24.9% below its 52W high, with Stock RS 14. In the wider sector, Stock RS fell over the month for thyssenkrupp (95 to 63), Hill & Smith, TKH, Aalberts and Smiths; only Sdiptech, Schouw and Röko rose.
  • Newsflow: the third-quarter reports on 23-Oct sorted the Swedish acquirers by organic growth: Sdiptech 13%, Lagercrantz 12%, MedCap 3.5% and Lifco 1.6% (earnings review's peer scoreboard). Lifco fell 12.0% on 7.2 times its average volume that day; Storskogen reported net sales up 4% and a margin of 9.7% from 9.5% (Storskogen, third-quarter report, 23-Oct).
  • Company guidance and communications: Sdiptech's chief executive called 2026 margins representative of the sustainable level (earnings call, 23-Oct); no peer guidance change is in the sources read.

The cohort's report-day winners were sold together on 26-Oct. Röko rose 12.7% on 23-Oct on 12.2 times its volume and fell 9.4% on 26-Oct; Sdiptech rose 4.6% and fell 4.2%. Storskogen, Indus Holding and Volati, which had not jumped, rose 1.6% to 4.0% that day. Hypothesis: 26-Oct was profit-taking in the Swedish acquirers that moved on their reports, in a market selling third-quarter beats in industrials, rather than a verdict on Sdiptech's quarter. Against: Sdiptech's margin missed consensus by 0.9 points, a Sdiptech-specific reason to sell.

What the peers imply for Sdiptech's sell tests: the threat runs through the groups, not the stock. Sdiptech's Sector RS fell 20 points in two weeks as large sector members fell, while its Stock RS rose.

The data faults

  • The dashboard data were built at 06:13 UK on 2-Nov under the retired rules, and rebuilt at 14:31 UK under your 2-Nov rules while this run was writing. The input files and this run's first facts came from the 06:13 build (Fevertree a Core Investment; Alfen's exits dated August; MA SLs on Core Investments; the breadth test not computed). Every section was checked against the 14:31 data; where a writer used an 06:13 figure the checker corrected it. Effect: none left in the note that I know of; Hexatronic's and Alfen's Sector RS, for example, changed by one point between the builds.
  • AlphaSense daily screens unreachable: Pink Chrome reached the first screen's list, then a bot check page ("Press & Hold to confirm you are a human"), which I did not try to pass; the built-in browser showed the login page. Effect: the Developments rest on the research filed, the earnings reviews and the data files.
  • Hilton Food's dividend: the chart file takes the 10.1p dividend of 29-Oct off as 0.1p, lifting the 50D MA to 691.5p from a corrected 682.3p. Effect: Hilton Food's MA SL distances are reported on both bases.

  • Bally's Intralot: the data file's daily series has 13 non-trading-day rows, including 28-Oct. Effect: none on the readings used.

  • Slot labels: Sdiptech's tranche 4 and Arteche's tranche 3, both on Core Investments, carry the Probing Bet slot label PB1 in the data file. Effect: none on the rules.
  • Results dates: Theon's 9-Nov update is not in the data file (its next date there is 19-Mar-2027); Volex's results are 12-Nov (Zeus) or 5-Nov (memo); Hexatronic's 4-Feb (data file) or 5-Feb (memo).
